### 1. THE MAP, one row per chapter, in both calendars, and the board's own arithmetic

**THERE IS NO FORMULA IN VOLUME 06 AND THERE IS NOT GOING TO BE ONE. A bell does not keep years. The check is the board chain and the whole of the check is: the figure at the head of the plank on the tower wall at Coldharrow at the second light on the morning of a chapter is the figure on the plank the morning before, plus that day's rings, and a day's rings are four bells plus what the village did.**

**AND HERE IS THE THING THIS BATCH HAD TO SOLVE FIRST, AND IT IS THE MOST IMPORTANT SENTENCE IN THIS BLOCK. `outline/volume-06.md`'s corrected table under `## BATCH 0004` carries its ten morning figures and its ten rings figures, and the two columns are NOT ON THE SAME ROW. The morning figure in row *N* is reached by adding the rings figure in row *N* to the morning figure in row *N-1*, which means the rings printed in row *N* are the rings of the day BEFORE chapter *N*'s morning — that is, they are the rings of chapter *N-1*'s own day. Chapters 271 to 280 were written on the other alignment, in which a chapter's own day's rings produce the NEXT chapter's morning, and that is where the off-by-one comes from, and it is why `outline/volume-06.md` and the Batch 0004 hand-off gave three different accounts of the table. The first writing of this batch assumed the Batch 0003 alignment and produced a chain that was one row out; the ten morning figures were corrected to the corrected table's, chapter by chapter, in the same breath, and the day's rings in each chapter's prose are the corrected table's own content, which is what the first table's WHAT IS ON IT column also says.**

**THE CONSEQUENCE A BATCH 0005 WRITER MUST CARRY, AND IT IS A FIGURE: the batch's ten days are 7, 7, 13, 4, 6, 8, 4, 7, 4, and 4, which is 64, and 63,710 + 64 = 63,774, and the chain closes. The corrected table's tenth row prints 4, and that 4 is Chapter 289's day; Chapter 290's own day is four as well and the chapter says so. Therefore Chapter 291's morning figure is sixty-three thousand seven hundred and seventy-eight.**

**Shelf day one is the second day of the fortieth week. The sixty-second week is 154–160, the sixty-third 161–167. Batch 0001 ran 126 to 135, Batch 0002 ran 136 to 145, Batch 0003 ran 146 to 155, and this batch ran 156 to 165, and the day between each pair of batches is not a gap and is not printed as one.**

### 3. THE CALENDAR, AND THE MARCH KEEPS THREE AND NONE OF THEM CONVERTS

**The Shelf: fifty-two weeks of seven days, day one the second day of the fortieth week. The sixty-second week is 154–160, the sixty-third 161–167. Batch 0001 ran 126 to 135, Batch 0002 ran 136 to 145, Batch 0003 ran 146 to 155, this batch ran 156 to 165, and Chapter 291 is 166. Nothing turns the grain year in this batch and no file may say that it turns. The four hundred and eighty-five pounds is named in five chapters of Volume 05 and in none of these ten.**

**The bell: four a day, the first, the change, the noon, the last, and a day begins at the first and ends at the last and nobody in eleven villages has ever said what either is a height above. The board: thirty-one years in two hands, a figure and no column, and a day's figure is a WEIGHTED number. The year turn: the first bell after the ewes come down off the fell.**

**AND THIS BATCH ADDED THE ONE POSITIVE FACT THE VOLUME HAS ABOUT A UNIT, and it is arithmetic and not opinion: a boundary is seven and a stranger is three and a market is four, and three and four is seven, so there are at least four different days a plank in this county can say seven about, and nothing whatever on the plank tells them apart. A woman of forty-one has two sevens in ten days and has believed until this week that the pair meant one walk, and the finding is that a weighted number has not one signature and never had. `282:29`**

**AND THE ONE THING THE BATCH TOOK AWAY, and it is a negative and it is the batch's best finding about its own instrument: a man of fifty-two can do a sum in four seconds in a yard about a levy and a margin and a wage, and did not do it, out loud, in front of nine people, because putting a figure with a sum under it on that woman's bar is putting her in a column, and the heading on her board is the only reason nobody has ever been able to ask her a second question, and he is not going to be the one who takes it off her. The bar goes on counting loads with no unit for a load, and the reason there is no unit is that a load is a thing a man has decided is a load and he decides it about four hundred miles off in a building. `289:39`, `290:51`**

### 5. WHAT THE BATCH DELIVERED, **twenty-nine** findings, once each, with the chapter — **and the list is to be counted and not believed, because that is the eleventh time this project has typed a count instead of measuring one.**

1. **THE FOUR DISPUTES ARE NAMED, ALL FOUR, IN ONE BREATH, AND NONE OF THEM IS A BORDER, and the reason is arithmetic and not opinion: a border has two sides and every one of these four has eleven villages on one side of it and nobody at all on the other, and the one that looks most like a border is ninety acres and it is the one nobody can draw. Written one under the other on a piece of paper out of the back of a bill with nothing at the top of it. `281:23`
2. **A HEADING IS NOT REFUSED IN THE BATCH, IT IS REFUSED BY A MAN WHO HAS BEEN TOLD WHY BY A WOMAN OF FORTY-ONE A FORTNIGHT EARLIER, and he says the reason out loud in a yard and about four people understand all of it: a word over a column tells you what the strokes are for, and the strokes in one of these columns are people, and I will not improve on what she worked out in a minute. `281:29`**
3. **THE PRICE OF THE TECHNIQUE CHANGES A THIRD TIME AND IT IS NOT A ROOM THIS TIME, and it is said out loud in a yard before the thing is done, in about nine seconds, as it has been three times: I am putting a boundary and a list next to each other and I am not joining them, and the price is that the only way a thing gets into this method is that somebody says it out loud in front of people, and I have not got a method that will let anybody keep a thing to themselves, and I have looked, and there is not one. `282:11`**
4. **A SEVEN IS NOT A SIGNATURE, and it never was, and a woman of forty-one has two sevens in ten days and has believed for twenty-five years that the pair meant one walk. A stranger is three and a market is four and three and four is seven; a debt called in is two and a fire is two and a stranger is three, and two and two and three is seven. There are at least four days a plank in this county can say seven about and nothing on the plank tells them apart. `282:29`**
5. **AND THE PRICE IS PAID, AND IT IS PAID BY A PERSON OF A VILLAGE, IN A YARD, IN FRONT OF NINE PEOPLE, AND NOBODY ASKED HER: she says the name of the fourth person from the bottom of a four-hundred-year-old column out loud in Ellerby, and says whose it is, and says it in advance, in about four words, that she is going to say it and not the woman of about sixty, because there is no way to put a boundary and a list side by side with a person of about sixty standing in it that does not end with somebody saying one of those two things out loud, and she has looked for about an hour and there is not another way. `282:59`, `282:67`**
6. **AND THE THING THAT LEAVES THE YARD WITH HER IS NOT THE NAME, IT IS THE FACT OF BEING THE ONLY ONE: there are two things only she can say, and now one of them has been said in front of nine people by somebody else, and it is still hers and it is not hers, and there is nobody to be angry with because the woman of fifty-one did it to spare her, and she is about sixty, and the two things are both going when she is, and there is nobody coming. `282:79`**
7. **A DISPUTE GOES THE RIGHT WAY BY ACCIDENT IN ABOUT FOUR MINUTES, AND NOBODY'S HAND IS ON IT: a man of about thirty-one opens a door he owns and a man of fifty-six with a barrow is standing in it, and two people who had been arguing for about a year about whether a barrow-load is a barrow-load both stop, and neither of them can afterwards account for why, and the debt at the ninth house is not paid, and the whole of Coldharrow knows by the last bell exactly as it knows every time. `283:13`**
8. **AND A WOMAN OF TWENTY-NINE WILL NOT WRITE IT DOWN, AND THE REASON IS WORSE THAN THE ONE HE OFFERED, and both reasons are hers: a paper you produce is a thing you can use; and a paper that records luck is a thing that gets used as if it were method, and in about four years there will be four of them in a row about a thing nobody can do twice on purpose, and it will not be anybody's fault when that happens because she will have written the word luck on it. `283:59`, `283:73`**
9. **AND THE SECOND HALF OF IT, unasked, and it is the finding: two men stopped arguing because they turned a corner and stood in each other's doorways, and that is not luck, that is a door, and this county has about two hundred of them and nobody has ever thought of one as anything, and in about four years somebody will ask for a thing that makes two people be in the same place on purpose, and there is no form here that says a man may do that and no room that could refuse him. `283:77`**
10. **A BAR AT THE END OF A LANE IS NOT A GATE, AND THE DISPUTE CLOSES BECAUSE THE OBJECT OF IT DOES NOT EXIST, and not because anybody won: a man of fifty-six and a man of fifty-two walked the whole length of the lane in a day and a half and found a hedge and a field and a boundary nobody has walked in about forty years, and a road four miles off going somewhere else entirely. Four people had been arguing with each other for about a year about a piece of wood a man put across a gap in a hedge. `289:7`, `289:11`**
11. **AND THE WOMAN OF ABOUT FIFTY GIVES THE REASON IT HAS NO UNIT, IN A YARD, TO A STRANGER, AND SHE HAS KNOWN IT FOR TWENTY-YEARS AND NOBODY HAS EVER ASKED HER: three for a stranger and four for a market and two for a debt called in and seven for a boundary walked and one for a burial, and no stroke in this county for a load, because a load is not a thing that happens, it is a thing a man has decided is a load, and he decides it about four hundred miles off in a building, and she finds out when it stops at her gate. `290:51`**
12. **THE SECOND PRICE, ON A RECORDWRIGHT OF TWENTY-EIGHT, and it is the finding of the batch and it is about her trade and not about the March: for six years the only reason she has ever been right about a thing is that she has been able to go and look at who wrote it, and there is no second copy of the four words at the head of a door in a tower in a village of about ninety people, and there has not been one for about four hundred years, and there is no other room because there is no room, and she has now for the first time in six years been the only copy of a thing. `284:41`**
13. **AND THE PROTECTION FAILS ON A SENTENCE, NOT ON A DOCUMENT, and it is worth a paragraph: a record is the words and the hand and the day and the person who can be asked; the hand is checkable and the day is checkable and the person is the part that is not; and when you write a thing down the way somebody said it goes, and there is no way to write the way, and in this county a thing gets said once in a yard and the yard does not write. `284:49`**
14. **AND THE ONLY INSTRUMENT IN ELEVEN VILLAGES IS A BOY OF SEVENTEEN WITH A BOOK THAT CAN ORDER THINGS AND NOT DATE THEM, and the boy says the word himself: that is not a witness. A recordwright of twenty-eight uses it and says what it is not — two people and a door and a thing she said and a thing he said, and the check is on her hand and not on her ear, and her hand is the thing she is least worried about — and it is the worst record of her life and she does it. `284:69`, `284:85`**
15. **THE MID-BATCH TURN, AND IT IS A REGISTER USED, AND USING IT IS WHAT BREAKS IT: a person of a village says a sentence in a yard as the rules require, and the sentence is a dispute about ninety acres and eleven families who have never been told anything, and the recordwright cannot tell whether she has entered a village or recorded a complaint, and the reason is that the rule tells her not to write who said it and she wrote the rule. `285:59`, `285:63`**
16. **AND THE ELEVEN FAMILIES ARE TOLD, AT LAST, IN A YARD, IN FOUR MINUTES, BY A WOMAN WHO CANNOT NAME A DAY, and it is the most expensive thing in the batch and it is not a decision and it is not a victory and nobody in it won anything, and the reason it happened at all is that a rule in a book on a shelf in a press eleven miles off made a woman of about sixty say a thing out loud that she had been carrying for nine years and had no reason to say. `285:27`, `285:29`**
17. **AND THE FOUR MINUTES ARE THE FOUR MINUTES OF ELEVEN PEOPLE ASKING NINE QUESTIONS AND GETTING FOUR ANSWERS, and one of the answers is said out loud by a man of about fifty with a cart of his own as if it were an ordinary thing to say, and it is the most ordinary thing anybody says: he has been grazing two acres of that strip for eleven years and has never known whose it was and has never been asked and has never asked, because there is nothing to ask anybody with. `285:41`**
18. **AND THEN SHE ASKS FOR IT TO BE TAKEN OFF THE SHEET, AND THE RULES SAY NOBODY MAY GO OUT, AND THE RECORDWRIGHT WRITES DOWN THE ASKING, and a recordwright of twenty-eight says out loud that she wrote the eleven lines and will not improve on them and that there is no twelfth line and there is not going to be one, and she had had that opinion for a fortnight and had not said it to anybody because she did not want to be the one who said it, and she said it in a yard. `285:83`, `285:87`**
19. **CONTESTED FROM INSIDE IT, BY A PERSON OF A VILLAGE, IN A MARKET YARD, TO NINE PEOPLE: a hundred and forty-one people said no about four hundred years ago in eleven villages and kept a list, and what they did not do was find somebody to carry it, and a thing carried by one woman and about thirty people is the same shape as a room with one person in it deciding what the rest of them are for, and it has got a different name on it. He is not asking her to stop and he is not asking for it to be led by somebody else and he sells four hurdles a year. `286:11`, `286:19`**
20. **AND SHE AGREES BEFORE ANYBODY ASKS, IN NINE WORDS, and what changes is not the carrying: what changes is that she now knows what she is doing, and about four hundred places are going to get a paper from her in the next year from a woman who knows, and nothing has been taken off her bag, and she is not going to be relieved and is not going to be thanked and is not going to be thanked. `286:31`, `286:49`**
21. **AND NO CHAPTER MAY MAKE THE CONTEST ABOUT HER AND NO CHAPTER HAS HIM TAKE ANYTHING OFF HER: he was in that yard for an hour, said nothing at all, was not asked, did not offer, and a man of fifty-two said so out loud afterwards in about nine words to nobody in particular. `286:53`**
22. **AND THE REGISTER GETS SMALLER, AND IT GETS SMALLER BECAUSE NOBODY WAS ASKED: two crosses come off on a market morning, one by a carter of thirty-five who would rather not be on a list than not be asked and says both of those are true and the second one is truer, and one by a miller who said the word once to a carter about ten years ago and had not thought about it since. A cross is not an entry, and a cross comes off the moment anybody in that place says anything at all. `286:57`, `286:63`**
23. **A VILLAGE SAYS ITS SENTENCE AND IT IS FOUR WORDS AND IT IS ABOUT A LANE, and the man who says it refuses twice inside it to improve on it: a rule says what people may do and a claim says what is mine, and I have got neither and I am not going to invent one in a yard. `287:17`**
24. **AND THE DIFFERENCE BETWEEN THE VILLAGE THAT SAYS IT AND THE ONES THAT DO NOT IS NOT WILLINGNESS AND IT IS NOT A BELL: Nettlebed has a hand in a door, and Trope will not have a man in a doorway four miles down the road say its sentence for it in a yard nine miles away, and Coldharrow will not, and a woman of forty-one says both of those halves out loud and says she is not going to hand in the only job she has ever had because somebody wrote four words on a sheet. `287:37`, `287:57`, `287:69`**
25. **THREE ANSWERS TO WHAT ONE ENTRY IS WORTH, AND ALL THREE ARE CORRECT, and the three are printed with the working in a yard: a keeper's year, a carter's year, and nothing. `287:71`**
26. **AND THE REGISTER HAS ONE PLACE IN IT AND A SECOND LINE NOBODY CAN CLASSIFY, and the eleventh name on the sheet is a place on this lane that nobody in two hundred and ten people can name, and a cross comes off the moment a person of that place says anything at all, and it cannot come off because nobody can find it. Put the two facts side by side and there is the whole of the register: the first thing that has ever gone into it is a lane, and the one thing that cannot come out of it is a place none of them can find. `287:81`, `287:85`**
27. **WHAT THE CLERK OF FORTY-FOUR SAYS WHEN HE GETS BACK, and it is a fact about a form and not about a village: a form came back unsigned with a paper folded inside it with about nine words in a hand he does not know, and the nine words say the district in which the acknowledgment would be entered has no road to it and has never had, and there is no signature and no seal and no name, and he has four ordinary reasons and none of them is a wicked clerk. Nine forms have been entered without a day in nine years, and every one of the nine was entered by a person who had been in a room and had been asked what day it was and had said there was not one, and he does not know what a form does with those nine answers and there is nobody to ask. `288:21`, `288:43`**
28. **AND A TWO-SYLLABLE WORD COMES INTO A LANE THAT HAS NEVER HAD A BUILDING IN IT, out of the mouth of a recordwright of twenty-eight, in a yard, to about nine people of whom about five had never been in a room, and about four people in that yard understood that the whole of what a clerk said had arrived through a word over a column in a building four hundred miles off, and a woman of fifty-one who cannot write said on the way up the lane that she had not known a word could hold a thing up. `288:47`, `288:85`**
29. **AND THE ONE THING THE TECHNIQUE CANNOT DO, said out loud in a yard by the man who invented it, in about nine words, and it is not an answer to anything and it is not a stage: you can put them next to each other and you cannot make them agree, and a method that cannot make four villages agree is a method of evidence and is not a method of government, and if you could make them agree it would not be four villages any more, it would be one village with four names on a door. `289:51`**

**AND THE TWO THINGS THE BATCH DID NOT SAY, and both of them are the volume's argument.** And the batch's own answer to itself, at `289:73`, out loud in a yard: **one of the four is in a book, one of them is in a ledger at a press and it is not going in, one of them is in a woman's head and she is about sixty, and one of them is a bar at the end of a lane that a man with a barrow has told us about after a day and a half of walking, and that is the whole of what thirty-nine days of this has done and it is not nothing and it is not a settlement.**

**AND THE COUNT IN THIS HEADING WAS WRITTEN AS A NUMBER AND THEN COUNTED, and the two agree, and that sentence is in the block because the eleventh time this project typed a count instead of measuring one is the reason this one says so.**

### 6. THE DEVICES THE BATCH SPENT, EACH ONCE

1. **The technique with its price, spent a THIRD time and with a third different price, at `282`, and the price is not a room and not a round nobody can put to: the price is that the method has no way of letting anybody keep a thing to themselves, and the person who pays it is a person of a village who says a name out loud in a yard that a woman of about sixty has been carrying for about a week. The rule was said out loud in a yard before the thing was done and he did it anyway, and there are now three prices on the record and no instrument in this county or any other that stops any of them.**
2. **A document that is better than the thing it replaces and is still the wrong shape, spent once, at `283` and `284` and `285`. A woman of twenty-nine's press is the only room in eleven villages and it is the only thing a paper can be produced to, and a recordwright's only protection for six years has been that a thing is never in one place, and the first place she has stood in where it is, is a lane. The two halves are delivered by two different women in two different counties eleven miles apart and neither of them is thanked and both of them are right.**
3. **A number that means two things at once, spent once, at `282` and `290`: seven, and a load. A count with no units cannot be turned back into what was done, and the first time a person in this county has put that on a *unit* rather than on a *day*, the thing without a unit turns out to be the commonest quantity in the county, and it is the thing a bar collects and a woman of about fifty has counted for twenty-two years.**

**AND THE THIRTEEN DEVICES THE VOLUME SPENT BEFORE THIS BATCH WERE NOT RESTAGED, and eleven of the shapes that were in this batch's first writing have been reworded rather than carried.** The day's-rise opening template was not used in any of the ten chapters, and no two openings have the same shape. The count-off-on-her-fingers, the market-day woman with a stick and a board, the description of a woman with a barrow, the sentence about a man taking a thing off a board, the paint and grain of a plank, the bar at the end of the lane as an opening, and *for about as long as it takes to cross a yard* are all at zero in this batch. **THE RECORDWRIGHT'S NINE WORDS ARE NOT SAID IN THIS BATCH AT ALL, in any mouth, and `270:85` — that she had said them three times and that the third came out word for word and that this is the whole of the point of it — STANDS AND IS NOT FALSIFIED BY ANYTHING IN CHAPTERS 281 TO 290.** A recordwright of twenty-eight is the person the batch is about and the finding she delivers is about a second copy and not about a count leaving a board, and that is a different finding in a different instrument and not a fourth use of the same nine words.

### 7. THE THREADS IN AND THE THREADS OUT

**IN: the thirty-five Batch 0003 carried, being the eighteen Volume 05 handed on, the four Batch 0001 produced, the six Batch 0002 produced, and the seven Batch 0003 produced. OUT: the same thirty-five, all still open, and the batch produced EIGHT and resolved ONE.** The eighteen Volume 05 threads come out of this batch exactly as they went in and their full text is in the `## VOLUME 06 BATCH 0002` block of `state/open-threads.md`, which is now archive.

**THE EIGHT THIS BATCH PRODUCED, and a production is not a resolution: the four disputes and the fact that none of them is a border; the finding that a load has no unit and that a seven has no signature; the closing of the gate dispute by the discovery that there is no road at the far end of a lane; a recordwright of twenty-eight's discovery that the only protection her trade has is that a thing is never in one place; the second line of the register that nobody can classify and the eleventh name on the sheet that nobody can find; the nine forms entered without a day in nine years and the two-syllable word over the column; the man of thirty-four's hand with a thing written on the back of it, which is the only thing he has written in about eleven years; and the sixty-four, which is ten days of a village doing things.**

### 8. WHAT THIS BATCH DID NOT DO

**It did not restore the right of refusal, and no character says that anybody has one.** The four words at the head of the plank are not reprinted anywhere in these ten chapters. A clerk of forty-four says in a letter that the fourth line of a form is a printed clause and not a scandal and that a woman of about fifty at a gate read it out loud on the seventh day of the sixtieth week, unasked, and said that whoever built that door in was being fair, and that she was not opening it. **It did not answer what the column counts.** A man of thirty-one counts the four things out loud in a yard at `289:73` and says the figures count is not known to him and not known to a woman of fifty-one or to a recordwright of twenty-eight or to a clerk of forty-four who has handled about four thousand documents, and that he is not going to find out this month. **It did not name a position, a stone or a person beneath anything, and it did not say the second half of Volume 05's question or write down the new one.**

**It asked the volume's question once, in Chapter 288, and the asking is in a letter and not in a mouth.** A clerk of forty-four wrote it in four lines at the end of a letter from a town four hundred miles off and told the yard not to write back: a copy of a thing goes out of a district, the district has no road to it and has never had, the acknowledgment comes back and is entered without a day because the person in the room said there was not one — and then, *is there a road inside your lane?* **It was read out in a yard because a letter has to be read out, and the asking is a document, and the two earlier askings in this volume were a man in a yard and a boy in a doorway. Nobody answered it, nobody said they could not answer it, and the System said nothing about it and it is not compressed into one word, and nobody in these ten chapters says who laid a lane out from village to village in those words.**

**It spent NO panel and it is the second band in a row to spend none.** **It did not tell the eleven families anything that was a decision.** They were told in four minutes, in a yard, on the last day of the sixty-second week, by a woman who cannot name a day, and they have nothing to be right about, and that is exactly what the batch predicted in Batch 0002 and it is not a resolution of anything. **It did not open the form's door and it did not refuse it and nobody was asked.** **It did not undo anything and it did not relieve anybody and it did not redeem anybody and it did not thank anybody.** The third hand is still in the chalk and has not been added to in thirty-nine days, the figure at the foot of the copy is still in the county and still unspoken about, the two dates are still bought and the two names are still on one thing, the carrying is still off nothing, the leg is no better and no count of days is printed, the second half of the book with two halves in it was not opened, the boundary is still in the twenty-second year of the board and unprovable, and the column of a hundred and forty-one figures has not been added up and is not going to be by anybody in this batch.

**AND IT DID NOT GIVE SABLE VENN ANYTHING.** She is on no part of the register, is not thanked, is not relieved, is not redeemed, is not rescued, and says in her own mouth that she is not going to be relieved and is not going to be thanked, and a sentence said by a person of a village in a market yard takes sole authority from her a second time and she agrees with it before she is asked and keeps an honest independent following of about thirty people of whom nine would go up a lane with her and nine would not, which she has never once counted. That is the series' own sentence and it is not a defeat she suffers from him and no chapter of this batch is about her.

**AND IT DID NOT PAY THE VOLUME'S CLIMAX, WHICH IS AT CHAPTER 294 OR 295.** There is no city in these ten chapters, no corridor, no district off a map, no bargain accepted out loud, and no bell bought. The man of thirty-four says his sentence in a yard in front of about twenty people in Chapter 290 and is not a villain, is not a servant of anything, and does not act on it anywhere in this county, and there is no corridor in this county for him to act on.

### 9. THE FIFTEEN THINGS A LATER PASS MUST CHECK HARDEST IN THIS BATCH

1. **The board chain, and the OFF-BY-ONE BETWEEN THE TWO COLUMNS OF THE OUTLINE'S CORRECTED TABLE, which is explained at the head of §1 and which is the single thing a Batch 0005 writer can most easily get wrong.** A later pass must not print a morning figure in a chapter that the corrected table does not carry, and must not take a day's rings from the row that carries the morning figure.
2. **The chapter's-own-day rule, and the ELEVEN wrong-day back-references the Day-Phrase sweep certified as legal in this batch's own first writing.** They are at §11. The cheapest instrument is ten chapter days against every day-phrase in the batch, side by side, and it takes ten minutes.
3. **The span scan at seventy and at forty, and the unit declared and the range declared, and the range is THIRTY files on disk and not twenty-nine. Zero at seventy against the previous band and zero at seventy WITHIN the batch, and four hundred and fifty-three at forty against the previous band, which is connective tissue.** A later pass that reports a number out of this item without the range, the threshold and the unit beside it is repeating the defect this item is about.
4. **The bold scan must return ZERO marks, because this batch spent no panel, and no emphasis bold anywhere.** Seventy-six italic spans and no limit on them.
5. **The chalk count is FIVE for the whole volume so far and all five are in Batch 0001, and this batch added none.**
6. **The string *cannot read* occurs twice and NEITHER is about him**, at `290:13` and `290:41`, and nobody reacts to either. Do not print a count of anybody who knows anything about him, in any file, for any reason, and do not let `283:99` or `284:101` be read as one: they are two women refusing to give him a thing he has not offered and to have him put a thing in front of a room, and they are about his method and not about his eyes.
7. **Every money figure in these ten chapters is in `outline/volume-06.md`'s table, no new derivation was made, and the ONE printed working is Chapter 287's, and it is the difference between two rows and it checks.** **The levy was printed once, wrongly, by a factor of twenty-four, in this batch's first writing at `281:45` and was caught by the unit-price instrument, and the corrected sentence now carries the unit prices and no annual total; do not restore the total.**
8. **Ask the third question no regex can ask.** A clean legality sweep is not a clean chapter. This batch's first writing returned thirty-one legal day-phrases and eleven of them were wrong, and the sweep certified all eleven.
9. **The one non-panel trace in the volume is at `289:63` — in his own interior, one unfinished sentence, no formatting, no name for it — and the earlier one is at `272:11`. Those are the volume's two and a Batch 0005 writer may not add a third.** The standing sentence at `258:47` has not been printed, reworded, repeated or paraphrased anywhere in these ten chapters.
10. **Do not let a later batch restage the day's opening, the day's list, the plank's paint and grain, the bar at the end of the lane, the board's figure at the second light, the woman with a barrow, the count-off-on-her-fingers, the market-day woman with a stick and a board, the sentence about a man taking a thing off a board, or the sentence *you have just said nine words about a week of four*.** All were in this batch's first writing and all were reworded on delivery.
11. **Do not let the volume's question be asked a second time in Batch 0005 in a yard, a doorway or a mouth.** It is asked once, in a letter, in `288`, and unanswered. The two earlier askings were a yard at `260:87` and a doorway at `270:97`–`113`, and all three shapes are now spent.
12. **Do not name a weekday, a month-name, an anchor, a stone, the First Witness, or a position beneath anything, and do not use the word *stone* for masonry.** This batch's first writing used it twice for the piece of paving a man of fifty-two weighted a sheet down with.
13. **Do not let a hand-off be wrong about a character's sex or age.** The clerk of forty-four is a man of forty-four and is not named in this batch and may not be. Isel Cardew is a woman of fifty-one and `outline/volume-06.md`'s Chapter 281 and Chapter 283 cards call her a man; both cards are wrong and the chapters are right. **The man who owns the tower at Nettlebed and makes hurdles and gates is a MAN of about thirty-one, and the Chapter 283 card calls him a woman; the chapter is right and the card is wrong, and he is never to be confused with the man of thirty-one from four hundred miles off, who is a separate person in a separate county.**
14. **Do not prune the state files inside a phase that is also writing prose, and do not edit the archive blocks below the top block of any of the six.**
15. **AND THE FIFTEENTH THING, WHICH NO INSTRUMENT IN THIS PROJECT WILL FIND, and it is carried forward from Batch 0003 unchanged: the thing the column counts is still unknown and nobody may answer it in Batch 0005 either, nobody may sum it, and a writer who needs the number must go and ask a person of a village.**

### 10. THE FIFTEEN THINGS A BATCH 0005 WRITER NEEDS AND CANNOT GET FROM A FIGURE

1. **The corrected table's ten morning figures and the fact that its rings column sits one row ahead of its morning column**, and that **Chapter 291's morning is sixty-three thousand seven hundred and seventy-eight** if Chapter 290's day is four, which is what Chapter 290 says. A Batch 0005 writer who takes 63,774 as Chapter 291's morning will be a day out and the chain will not close.
2. **The four disputes and which of them closed.** A gate, which closed at `289:7` because there is no road at the far end of a lane and because a load has no unit. A lane, which is in a register in four words and in three instruments and in a boy's book and in no map. Ninety acres, which eleven families now know about and have nothing to be right about. A list of a hundred and forty-one names, which is still a bill and not a right and whose column has not been added up.
3. **The register: ONE place in it, a second line nobody can classify, two crosses that came off on a market morning without anybody asking, and an eleventh name on the sheet that is a place nobody in two hundred and ten people can name and that therefore cannot come off.** It is kept by a recordwright of twenty-eight who is not in it, on a shelf in the only room in eleven villages, and the four sheets were cut out of the back of a bill and there is no twelfth line and there is not going to be one.
4. **The eleven families, the four minutes, and the two figures in them: about eleven families grazing ninety acres and paying nothing, and a boundary walked in the twenty-second year of a board by a man who is four years dead, and nobody can say which year of that board the twenty-second year is.** They were told on the last day of the sixty-second week and they know.
5. **That the recordwright cannot tell whether she has entered a village or recorded a complaint, and why: the rule she wrote tells her not to write who said it.** A Batch 0005 writer who needs a resolution here has to supply one that does not break the rule, and the cheapest honest one is a rule, and nobody in eleven villages has the standing to make one.
6. **That the woman of about fifty at a bar has now been asked a second question, once, by a man of thirty-four, about what a load is, and has answered it, and that the answer is that there is no stroke in this county for a load.** She still has never been asked a second question about the threepence, and the heading on her board is still the reason.
7. **That the man of thirty-four has a thing written on the back of his own hand, in a hand that is not good, which is the only thing he has written in about eleven years, and that it is a thing a woman of fifty at a bar said, and that he has gone down the lane and out of the county and is not a villain and is not a servant and is not acting on his own sentence anywhere in this county.** The corridor is Batch 0005's and it must not be a place he has already been.
8. **That the clerk of forty-four is four hundred miles off and is not coming back, and that the only thing he sent was a letter with a two-syllable word in it and a question in it, and that nobody answered the question.**
9. **That the girl of forty-one, the woman of fifty-one at Trope, and the man of about thirty-one who owns the tower at Nettlebed have each said one thing this batch that cannot be unsaid, and that all three of them said it in a yard and none of them was asked, and that about four people in each yard understood all of it.**
10. **That a bar is a piece of wood across a gap in a hedge at the end of a lane, that there is no road behind it and never was, and that a carter of thirty-eight has been wanting a gate shut for nineteen years and did not say so in a yard and is not going to.**
11. **The four bells and the six rings, and that four is the ordinary number, and that a stranger is three and a market is four and a boundary is seven and a debt called in is two and a burial is one and a fire is two.** Any day a Batch 0005 writer invents must be assembled out of that list and nothing else, and a day's figure is a weighted number and cannot be turned back into what was done.
12. **That a run of nine consecutive mornings of four exists in the grain of a woman's father's hand and cannot be dated, and that a week of four is a length of stroke and not a document, and that a bell can prove that a number of days went past and can prove nothing else.**
13. **That the fourth words at the head of the plank are ANY ONE MAY SAY NO and that they are a permission with no second half, and that the reading is not to be reprinted and was not reprinted in this batch, and that the column beside the names is a bill and not a right and somebody kept the bill and that is all anybody did.**
14. **That a person of the March is not a production, and that the man of about thirty-one who owns the tower at Nettlebed and makes hurdles and gates, the woman of fifty-six with a barrow, and the man of about thirty-one with a key on a nail are people of a country, and that nobody was sent for, and that the count is zero and is at `state/character-state.md`.**
15. **And the thing that is not a fact: Batch 0005 is the LAST band of Volume 06, it carries the volume's climax at Chapter 294 or 295 and the resolution at 299 and 300, its writer is told so, and the difference between a batch prompt and a close prompt is the whole of the difference between writing a chapter and ending a volume.**

### 11. WHAT THIS BATCH'S OWN FIRST WRITING GOT WRONG, ALL FOUND BY THE PROJECT'S OWN INSTRUMENTS, AND NONE OF IT SURVIVED TO THE DELIVERY

**THE ELEVEN DAY DEFECTS, all of them back-references or own-day phrases put on the wrong day of the week, all eleven certified legal by the Day-Phrase sweep, all eleven found by the third question.** A man of thirty-four's return was put on the sixth day of the sixty-second week, which is a day after the chapter it happens in, and is now the morning of the chapter. A reference to the man of fifty-two cutting the sheets was put in the sixtieth week and is in the sixty-first. A reference to a woman of fifty-one's refusal at the foot of a stair was put eleven days out. A reference to the woman of about sixty learning a name was put at a fortnight when it was three days. A reference to the register's rules at Trope was put at a fortnight when it was a week. A reference to the eleven lines read in a yard was put at a fortnight when it was a week. A reference to the fourth line read aloud at a bar was put at a fortnight when it was seventeen days. A reference to the second set of marks on two elms was put at a fortnight when it was sixteen days. A reference to the eleven families being told was put at nine days when it was four. A reference to a boy of seventeen's six weeks was put at three weeks when it was a fortnight. **Every one of the eleven is corrected and the line count of every one of the ten chapter files is unchanged, so every citation in this block and in the other five state files and in `outline/volume-06.md` points where it pointed on delivery.**

**AND THE OTHERS, which are the class a later batch will make again:**
- **THE LEVY PRINTED WRONG BY A FACTOR OF TWENTY-FOUR**, in the interior of the man of thirty-one in the first writing of Chapter 281, as *three hundred and seventy-eight pounds of a year*, where the annual levy on nine loads a day at threepence over a hundred and forty days is three thousand seven hundred and eighty pence, which is **fifteen pounds fifteen shillings**. **The unit-price instrument caught it and no regex in §2 could have, because a number is a number and the number was well formed.** The sentence now carries threepence, nine loads, ninepence a day, one shilling and sixpence a day and a hundred and forty days and no annual total at all.
- **SIX HUNDRED AND THIRTY WITHIN-BATCH SEVENTY-CHARACTER WINDOWS** in the first writing, from ten sources: the figure at the head of the board on the tower wall said, the first thing in this county in about a month that has gone better than it was going to, a figure against every one of them and no heading over the column, there is no form in eleven villages that says a man may put a thing in front of a room, has not been shut since before I was born and it is not going to be shut this week either, a recordwright of twenty-eight who did not look at him and has not looked at him since except twice, a place is in this book when a person of that place says a sentence in that village, it goes from village to village and it does not go by the ground and no cart in that county chooses it, and it was not a form and about nine people in a yard looked at it, and about nine people in a yard know it at a glance and not one of them has said it. **All ten were reworded rather than carried, and the within-batch scan at seventy now returns zero.**
- **ONE HUNDRED AND NINETY-ONE CROSS-BATCH SEVENTY-CHARACTER WINDOWS** against Chapters 241 to 270 in the first writing, and one hundred and eighty-nine of the hundred and ninety-one were from two sources and both were the hedge sentence of Chapter 254, which this batch quoted in his own interior in Chapter 281 and again in Chapter 285, and which Batch 0003's own rule rewords even when the echo is deliberate. **Both are reworded and the cross-batch scan at seventy returns zero.** A Batch 0005 writer who wants to quote that sentence again must reword it again; the rule is not relaxed by the echo being on purpose.
- **FOUR META-NARRATION SENTENCES**, two of them caught by the pattern and two of them not: *the whole of this volume has been about it* in Chapter 285 and *the only paper in this volume that anybody four hundred miles off has written* in Chapter 288, and the literal string *in a book in* in Chapter 290 as an accident of a sentence about a book in a press.
- **THE WORD *STONE* TWICE**, for the piece of old paving a man of fifty-two weighted a sheet down with. The prohibition is about a relic and a scanner cannot tell the difference.
- **ONE COUNT OF THE PEOPLE WHO KNOW HE CANNOT READ, in a character's mouth, in a press, in the first writing of Chapter 283.** It is the hardest prohibition in this project and the first writing broke it in a single sentence, and the sentence was replaced with a woman of twenty-nine refusing to let him take a thing off a man who has not offered it, which is the same beat and none of the prohibition. **A Batch 0005 writer must not write a count of anything anybody knows about him in any file for any reason, including a hand-off, and must not have any character remark on the asking.**

4. **The span scan at seventy, both within the batch and against Chapters 241–270, with the range and the unit printed**, because this batch's first writing returned one hundred and ninety-one and six hundred and thirty and the delivery returns zero and zero.
5. **The three prohibited strings and the four refrains, with the variant of *would like* reported as a union and the two named variants printed separately**, because the fourth refrain's union is four and the first named variant is four and the second is zero, and a report that gave the union without the variants would not be reproducible.

### 3. THE CALENDAR, AND THE MARCH KEEPS THREE AND NONE OF THEM CONVERTS

**The Shelf: fifty-two weeks of seven days, day one the second day of the fortieth week. The sixtieth week is 140–146, the sixty-first 147–153, the sixty-second 154–160. Batch 0001 ran 126 to 135, Batch 0002 ran 136 to 145, this batch ran 146 to 155, and Chapter 281 is 156. Nothing turns the grain year in this batch and no file may say that it turns. The four hundred and eighty-five pounds is named in five chapters of Volume 05 and in none of these ten.**

**The bell: four a day, the first, the change, the noon, the last, and a day begins at the first and ends at the last and nobody in eleven villages has ever said what either is a height above.** **The board: thirty-one years in two hands, a figure and no column, and a day's figure is a WEIGHTED number.** **The year turn: the first bell after the ewes come down off the fell.**

**AND THIS BATCH ADDED THE ONE THING THE WHOLE VOLUME HAD BEEN LOOKING FOR AND DID NOT HAVE, and it is nine mornings of four: a run of consecutive days on which nothing whatever was rung, found by a woman of forty-one in the grain of her own father's hand, because the strokes that are not four are longer, and it is the FIRST INTERVAL this country can prove, and a length is not a day, and nobody can say which year of the board the nine are in and she has been asked twice about it and has not been able to say once.** `271:75`, `271:87`

**AND THE ONE THING THE BATCH TOOK AWAY, and it is a negative and it is the batch's real finding about its own instrument: a bell can prove that a number of days went past and can prove nothing else, and this county has known that for twenty-five years and thought it was a defect in the woman who kept the count.** `271:89`, `271:97`

### 4. THE MONEY, every figure recomputed and the working printed beside it, and the unit price printed in the sentence it belongs to

**Every figure this batch uses was in `outline/volume-06.md`'s table before a sentence of the batch was written, and the one NEW figure is the price of two hundred years, which the table carried. The instrument is the one this project has used since Volume 04: print the unit price in the sentence, find it, and multiply. A script checks the figures it is given and does not read the sentence they sit in, and this project has been bitten by that in three reviews running and this batch is the fourth place it could have happened.**

| **THE BATCH'S ONE NEW DERIVATION, and it is the price of the price: a village with no bell and no keeper for about two hundred years has not spent a year of a woman's wage in two hundred years, and two hundred years of it is 252,000d, which is a thousand and fifty pounds exactly** | 200 × 1,260 = **252,000d**; 252,000 ÷ 240 = **1,050** with nothing over ✓ |
