### 5. WHAT THE BATCH DELIVERED, **thirty-one** findings, once each, with the chapter — **and the list is to be counted and not believed, because that is the eleventh time this project has typed a count instead of measuring one.**

1. **A board with a word at the top of it is the only thing in eleven villages that has a heading, and it is the reason its keeper has never been asked a question, and the woman of forty-one would give eleven shillings for one and would not know what to do with it.** `271:57`, `271:63`
2. **The fourth line of Form 6 is a door that nobody has to open, and a woman of about fifty at a bar read it out on her twenty-second year at that bar because it was lying on a cart in the rain and she had a board under her arm, and nobody asked her, and she is the fourth person in this county to read that sheet and the only one who was not asked.** `271:29`
3. **THE BATCH'S FIRST FINDING AND IT IS A POSITIVE ONE: a run of nine consecutive mornings of four, found in the grain, is the first interval in this county that anybody can prove, and the strokes that are not four are longer, and it is nine and not ten because there is a longer stroke either side of it, and it cannot be dated, and the woman who found it has been asked twice about it and cannot say once which year of the board it is in.** `271:75`
4. **A week of four is a rule and not a document, and it turns out to be true, and it is a length of stroke, and Tarin Keel had said it on the first day of the fifty-eighth week and could not say how he knew it and can now.** `271:77`, `271:81`
5. **A bell can prove that a number of days went past and nothing else, and a length is not a day, and the woman of forty-one has known that for twenty-five years and thought it was a defect in her.** `271:87`, `271:97`
6. **THE TWO VILLAGES ARE NOT SHORT OF A BELL AND THE TWO OF THEM ARE NOT SHORT OF ANYTHING, and the mill at Trope has a wheel you can see from four hundred yards, and the last man in a village to want one of these went without, and the cause ran the other way, and a man of sixty-three has chewed on it in four counties for eleven years and has now been handed the whole of it in a yard and did not do one thing toward it.** `272:39`, `275:47`
7. **NOBODY WENT INTO THE TOWER, and it is the first thing in twenty-two days that anybody has refused to do, and there was no form for it and no vote and no falling out, and the man of thirty-one said so and said why, and the woman of forty-one said the stronger version of it: there is nothing in there she has to know about this morning, because you cannot make a day out of a door and a length is worth more.** `272:71`, `272:79`
8. **A woman of fifty-one who has rung a handbell for about forty years, has never been asked who taught her, has had the question put to her three times, and has not answered it once, and the sentence she gives instead is that there is no why and there has not been one since before she could ask one.** `272:29`
9. **THE PERSON WHO FINDS THE BOARD CAME UP THIS LANE LOOKING FOR A FORM, and his whole trade is watching what a document does when it leaves the place it was measured in, and nobody had ever gone after one in the history of the thing, and he came on his own account, and he brought nobody and no authority and has no good name left to spend, and he says so in about nine words before anybody asks him.** `273:39`, `273:51`, `274:105`
10. **A boundary was walked a second time in ten days by a man of about sixty-one who put the marks on it nine years ago, and he did it because a stranger from a room asked him for a day, and it is on the board as a seven, and the board has two sevens in ten days and nothing whatever that says they are the same walk, and a man of sixty-one would not let a stranger do the walking with him.** `273:23`
11. **AND A WOMAN OF FORTY-ONE REFUSES TO BE USED AS THE ARITHMETIC A SECOND TIME, in her own mouth, standing on the fourth of eleven worn steps: if I read it out then it is a woman of forty-one reading out a number in a yard, and there is no column on my board that says who did what, and we had all of that out loud on the sixth day of the sixtieth week and I am not going to have it again about a different number.** `274:47`
12. **A hundred and forty-one names with a figure against every one of them and nothing whatever over the column is a bill, and the man who counts carts for a living declined to add it up in front of nineteen people and was right, and the reason he gives is that a column with nothing over it is not a total and adding it produces a number that is wrong about the world, and there is one of those about once a week in this county and the woman of forty-one has spent twenty-five years not making any.** `274:43`
13. **THE MIDPOINT REVERSAL, AND IT IS DELIVERED BY A DOCUMENT-READER NOTICING WHAT A DOCUMENT DOES NOT SAY. A clerk of forty-four has read the Codification and four hundred copies of it and has never once in twenty-two years found a document that admitted to having taken something: not one. The sentence at the head of that plank is the reason the whole of it took nine seconds to find out.** `274:73`
14. **AND IT IS DELIVERED IN AN ORDINARY MOUTH AND NOT IN A PANEL, and the ordinary mouth is a woman of fifty-one who was not asked: We did not lose it. Nobody took it off us. We were never in a room and there was nothing to be in one. There was a thing written on a door and then a great many things were written on a great many more doors about four hundred years later.** `274:83`
15. **AND NOTHING IS RESTORED, and it is said in the same plain words and in about four sentences: we could not refuse the bar, we could not refuse a copy, nobody in this lane has been able to refuse one single thing at any time that anybody can remember, we cannot refuse anything now, and we could not before, and the two are the same. What we did was stop saying yes, and there was nothing there to say yes to, and the cost was a bell apiece, and it is in that column, and there is no name over the column, and I am not going to stand here and tell you that makes it a right. It makes it a bill. Somebody kept the bill. That is all anybody did.** `274:87`
16. **The four lines about a water and a height and a payment stopped being a curiosity in about four seconds in a tower, because a copy of a ruling landing in a place that has a four-hundred-year-old list of people who said no is not a stranger's rule landing on a broken place, and a man of thirty-one said that out loud and had been wrong about it three times in the same way and said so.** `274:99`
17. **THE PRICE, with the working printed in a yard, and the man it is aimed at is in the yard and is not asked a single question about it: two hundred years of a woman's wage is a thousand and fifty pounds exactly, which is a hundred and fifty-five bells and three pounds fifteen shillings. The price of two hundred years of refusing a bell is a hundred and fifty-five bells, and nobody refused a hundred and fifty-five bells, and the figure is absurd on purpose and the absurdity is the whole of it.** `275:13`
18. **AND WHAT A MAN OF SIXTY-THREE WHO SELLS BELLS SAYS ABOUT IT IS ABOUT HIS OWN TRADE AND NOT ABOUT THE MARCH, in about four minutes, and it is the worst thing anybody has said about him: in eleven years I have never once had a village say no to me, not one, I have had two say nothing, and I have wanted one to say no, and that is what a man in my trade is for, and I have a bench and a forge and four men, and I would have gone home on the last day of that week and forgotten this yard by the middle of next month. And he is not redeemed and he ends the speech by saying he is going to quote six pounds fifteen in the spring and is not going to quote it lower.** `275:37`
19. **AND THE WHOLE OF THE MARGIN OF THE TWO VILLAGES FOR TWO HUNDRED YEARS IS TEN POUNDS AND TEN SHILLINGS, which is to the penny a carter's year, and it has been coming in at threepence a load off a bar that is not shut and going nowhere for about two hundred years, and there is exactly one man in eleven villages who could ever have been paid out of it and he is standing at the end of the ninth cart and called a nine-shilling debt in that morning and has never had a penny of it and has never once asked what a bar is for.** `275:77`, `275:83`
20. **THE PANEL, and it decided nothing, and a recordwright of twenty-eight put her satchel down on a cart's side for the first time in twenty-six days, and the finding around it is that a document read to nine people who want nothing from it is the first one she has ever read, and that a recordwright who has spent six years believing that the mark of a real record is that it can be produced in a room has got a plank on the inside of a door that nobody has ever produced anywhere.** `276:19`, `276:34`
21. **THE VOLUME'S CENTRAL PRESSURE, ARGUED A SECOND TIME AND WITH A FOURTH THING IN THE ROOM THAT IS NOT A POSITION AT ALL: a carter of thirty-eight gives up the whole of nineteen years of wanting a road in exchange for a road that has a day on it, which is a different sentence from the one he said in Batch 0002 and is further along; a woman of forty-one refuses to say a hundred and forty-one names out loud in a yard because a name said out loud in a yard is a person in a room and she has been one for a morning; and a woman of fifty-one says that a thing kept for four hundred years by ordinary people at a cost is not theirs to spend, and she is right, and the man of thirty-one is not asked and is in the middle of it.** `277:11`, `277:45`, `277:57`
22. **AND THE PRICE OF THAT ARGUMENT, said in her own mouth by the woman of nine years' standing: she has carried about four hundred pieces of paper in nine years and has never once asked a single person what they wanted, and the eleven villages have been deciding this for four hundred years without a road and a bell and a bench and are not waiting, and she is not going to start asking now, and she would like it written down by a woman of forty-one.** `277:69`
23. **THE REGISTER, and its shape is what it does not contain: fifty-one places on four sheets cut out of the back of a bill, anybody may read all four, nobody may go in without a sentence said by a person of that village in that village, and no gate, no toll, no bar, no day, no date, no bench, no seat, no room with a door on it except the press it stands on a shelf in, no vote, no meeting, no officer, and no money in it anywhere. Not one of the four of them is on it. Not one of the eleven villages on this lane is in it and none has asked to be.** `278:7`, `278:11`, `278:37`, `278:45`
24. **THE SENTENCE THAT TAKES A SOLE AUTHORITY OFF A WOMAN WITH A BOARD IS SAID BY A PERSON OF A VILLAGE, IN A VILLAGE, AND NOT BY HIM: you have thirty people in this country who will do what you tell them, and in eleven weeks you have not asked this village one single question about what it wants, and I have been watching you not ask since the third day of that week, and a thing that eleven villages decided four hundred years ago cannot be carried by one woman with people who follow her. And she agrees with it before she is asked, and says she would have said it in about four days and is glad she has not had to sit on it, and says she is not relieved, and nobody in that yard thanked anybody.** `278:19`, `278:29`
25. **A CARTER OF THIRTY-FIVE WHO LIVES AT THE END OF A BAD ROAD CALLED STENNICK puts a lane that is on nobody's list on a cart's side, and the technique is done a second time and the price is paid a second time and the price is not the one anybody expects: it is not that the two men find out about each other, it is that the price of what has been done is that neither of them has anybody to put the round to, and there is a third thing about a hundred and forty-one names on a door that neither of them will ever see.** `278:87`, `279:37`, `279:45`
26. **THE WALKED LANES ARE A NETWORK AND NOT A SET OF LANES, and the proof is arithmetic and not opinion, and it is three instruments that have got nothing to do with one another: the order the debts go round in at a bar, which is not the order of distance; the order the names are in on the outside wall of the last house in Ellerby, which holds for people who were not in Ellerby when it was written; and a line a boy of seventeen has run since he was twelve, in the same order every year, which does not go by the ground. And no man's cart would choose it, because a cart goes where there is ground and this goes where there is a village.** `279:37`
27. **AND THE ONLY RECORD IN THE MARCH THAT HOLDS ALL ELEVEN IS A BOY'S BOOK, and it is five years old, and it is on his back, and nobody in that yard asked anything about it, and the volume's question is not asked a third time and is referred to once as a thing in a book that has a mark on the end of it.** `279:65`, `279:69`
28. **THE BATCH'S CLIMAX IS A FAILURE OF AN INSTRUMENT AND IT IS THE BOY'S OWN, IN HIS OWN MOUTH: his book has two places in it, what crossed and what did not, and there is a third place on that door and he can see it and does not know what it is for, and the order on that plank is the order somebody decided and his book is the order things happened in, and those are two different orders and he cannot read one with the other. He writes nothing. He says he will think about it and will not be hurried. He goes and does his day.** `280:27`, `280:53`
29. **AND THE FINEST THING SUCH A BOOK CAN SAY ABOUT THAT WALL IS THAT ONE OF THESE CAME BEFORE THAT ONE, and there are four hundred years between them, and not one of them is a day he can name, and there is not one instrument in eleven villages that could turn that into a day, and he has had five years.** `280:31`
30. **A DOCUMENT COULD HAVE BEEN PRODUCED OUT OF IT IN ABOUT FOUR SECONDS AND WAS NOT, and the chapter says so in his own interior and not in anybody's mouth: if I had said read it out, and the two hundred years of a woman's wage in that column is a figure I gave a woman of twenty-nine in a press, and if she had put that on the foot of a sheet the way she put my number there, then this plank would be a document. And nobody in that tower thought of asking him, because nobody in that tower had any reason to, and he stood there and let it not come up.** `280:43`
31. **AND THE ROUND NUMBER NOBODY CHOSE: the board read sixty-three thousand five hundred and ten on the first morning of Chapter 251 and will read sixty-three thousand seven hundred and ten on the first morning of Chapter 281, and the difference is exactly two hundred, and nobody in two hundred and ten people chose it, and there is no column for it and no heading over it and no name against it and no year at the top of it and there never was one.** `280:63`

**AND THE COUNT IN THIS HEADING WAS WRITTEN AS A NUMBER AND THEN COUNTED, and the two agree, and that sentence is in the block because the eleventh time this project typed a count instead of measuring one is the reason this one says so.**

### 6. THE DEVICES THE BATCH SPENT, EACH ONCE

1. **A heading over a column, and the consequence that a heading is a category and a category is a place a person can be put, spent once, at `271`, and paid at `275` and again in `280`'s last act.** The bar-keeper's *AT THE BAR* is the only heading in eleven villages and it is the reason she has never been asked a question; the answer — a heading over a column is the only reason a hundred and forty-one names are still people — is thought and not said, in `275:85` and again in `280:43`, because he cannot produce a record.
2. **The technique with its price, spent a second time and with different people, at `279`, and the price is a different price and the device is not a restage: in `267` the two people at the centre of it were two women and the price was that a woman of forty-one found out she had been in a room for nine years; in `279` the two are a carter of thirty-eight and a carter of thirty-five and the price is that neither of them has anybody to put a round to.** The rule was said out loud in a yard in both chapters and the man of thirty-one looked for an instrument both times and there is not one.
3. **A hand dated instead of a story dated, spent once, at `273` and `274`.** A clerk of forty-four dates a hand at about four hundred years and refuses a year, and a woman of fifty-one dates a column by family at about two hundred years, and the two datings are done with two instruments and the chapter says which is which. **This is the answer to the design risk named in the cards: the four hundred years in this batch is a HAND and not a floor, and the man of about thirty-five with a barrow who says four hundred because that is what you say is not in any chapter of this batch, and no mouth that has been in Ellerby says the words at all.**
4. **A document that does not say what it took, spent once, at `274`, and it is the volume's midpoint and it is not a revelation and not a conspiracy and nobody hid anything: a clerk of forty-four has read four hundred copies of an instrument and not one of them has ever admitted to having taken something, and that is not because it is honest and it is not because it is dishonest, it is because the people who made it were not in a room.**

**AND THE SPENT DEVICES BOTH EARLIER BANDS SPENT WERE NOT RESTAGED, and three of them were in this batch's first writing and all three have been reworded rather than carried.** The day's-rise opening, the count-off-on-her-fingers, the market-day woman with a stick and a board, the description of a woman with a barrow at a gate, the sentence about a man taking a thing off a board, the hedge sentence of the fourth day of the fifty-eighth week, the description of the man of about thirty-five with a barrow, *because he has not written down what is said in a yard since a market town four hundred miles off*, *about nine hundred people who cannot pay a pound three shillings and sixpence for a day out of a village*, and *whatever she decided about him happened in that second* are all at zero in this batch. ***For about as long as it takes to cross a yard*: ZERO, and that is a change and not an oversight, and the change is deliberate, because Batch 0002's own span scan found this phrase standing five times in five chapters while the record said it had been handled, and the cheapest way to make sure it cannot happen again in a seventh chapter is to stop using it.*** **THE RECORDWRIGHT'S NINE WORDS ARE NOT SAID IN THIS BATCH AT ALL, in any mouth, including in a report of a clerk's yard, and `270:85` — that she had said them twice, that the third came out word for word, and that this is the whole of the point of it — STANDS AND IS NOT FALSIFIED BY ANYTHING IN CHAPTERS 271 TO 280.** That was the batch's largest single risk and it is closed.

### 7. THE THREADS IN AND THE THREADS OUT

**IN: the twenty-eight Batch 0002 carried, being the eighteen Volume 05 handed on, the four Batch 0001 produced, and the six Batch 0002 produced. OUT: the same twenty-eight, all still open, and the batch produced SEVEN and resolved NOTHING.** The eighteen Volume 05 threads come out of this batch exactly as they went in and their full text is in the `## VOLUME 06 BATCH 0002` block of `state/open-threads.md`, which is now archive.

**THE SEVEN THIS BATCH PRODUCED, and a production is not a resolution: the two villages that have neither a bell nor a board; the tower and the board on the inside of a door and the four words at the head of it; the column with no heading and the fact that a man of fifty-two declined to add it up; the register of fifty-one places with a recordwright keeping it and no village in it; the round that eleven villages go in and that three instruments hold independently; the panel's undecided question, which is whether a thing kept four hundred years in a village that has no bell may be read by anybody, and by whom; and the two hundred.**

### 8. WHAT THIS BATCH DID NOT DO

**It did not restore the right of refusal, and no character says that anybody has one.** A woman of fifty-one says in an ordinary mouth that it binds nobody, that the people of the lane could not refuse the bar or a copy at any time anybody can remember, that what they did was stop saying yes, and that it makes a bill and not a right, and the clerk of forty-four says he has no authority and has not opened a door and is not going to. **The words *right of refusal* are in one chapter and they are a phrase at the head of a four-hundred-year-old plank and they are not a right anybody holds.** It did not turn the null-zone question into a power, and it did not name a position, a stone or a person beneath anything, and it did not say the second half of Volume 05's question or write down the new one.

**It did not ask the volume's question a third time.** It has now been asked twice, at `260:87` in a yard to nobody without a mark and at `270:97`–`113` in a doorway to a person with one, and in Chapter 279 it is referred to once as a mark at the end of a question in a boy's book and is not put in anybody's mouth. **Nobody answers it and the System does not raise it.**

**It spent one panel and it is the second of three.** **It did not tell the eleven families anything.** They are named in Chapter 280 as a strip of ninety acres that eleven families are grazing and paying nothing for and have never been told about, and nobody in eleven villages has been told anything. **It did not open the form's door and it did not refuse it and nobody was asked**, and the fourth line was read out a second time by a person who was not asked and she did not open it and said why. **It did not undo anything and it did not relieve anybody and it did not redeem anybody and it did not thank anybody.** The third hand is still in the chalk, the figure at the foot of the copy is still in the county and still unspoken about, the two dates are still bought and the two names are still on one thing, the carrying is still off nothing, the leg is no better and no count of days is printed, the second half of the book with two halves in it was not opened, the boundary is still in the twenty-second year of the board and unprovable, and the column of figures has not been added up and is not going to be by anybody in this batch.

**AND IT DID NOT GIVE SABLE VENN ANYTHING.** She is on no part of the register, is not thanked, is not relieved, is not redeemed, is not rescued, and says in her own mouth that she is not relieved, and a sentence said by a person of a village in a village takes sole authority off her and she agrees with it before she is asked. That is the series' own sentence and it is not a defeat she suffers from him, and the man of thirty-one is in the yard and says nothing for an hour and a clerk of forty-four says so out loud to nobody in particular.

### 9. THE FIFTEEN THINGS A LATER PASS MUST CHECK HARDEST IN THIS BATCH

1. **The board chain: 63,647 to 63,706 in nine exact steps and 63,710 on the first morning of Batch 0004.** Run it morning by morning against the table in `outline/volume-06.md` and do not believe a figure out of a block.
2. **The chapter's-own-day rule, and the NINE wrong-day back-references the Day-Phrase sweep certified as legal.** They are at §11. The cheapest instrument is ten chapter days against every day-phrase in the batch, side by side, and it takes ten minutes, and on this batch it was the only instrument that found anything at all.
3. **The span scan at seventy and at forty, and the unit declared and the range declared, and the range is THIRTY files on disk and not twenty-nine.** **Zero at seventy, and the review of Batch 0003 found that this figure was hiding forty shared runs against this same range and had to be measured before it could be believed; the two sources and both rewordings are at §2 item 4.** Nine hundred and twenty-six at forty, which is connective tissue. **A later pass that reports a number out of this item without the range, the threshold and the unit beside it is repeating the defect this item is about, and the two numbers in this item that were not reproducible have both been replaced and the method is printed at §2 item 4.**
4. **The bold scan must return exactly four marks, which is one panel in two lines at `276:19`–`20`, and no emphasis bold anywhere else in the batch.** Seventy-six italic spans and no limit on them.
5. **The chalk count is FIVE for the whole volume so far and all five are in Batch 0001, and this batch added none**, and the word *chalk* is in three chapters and every one of them says nothing was added to it.
6. **The string *cannot read* occurs three times and only one of them is about him**, at `274:99`, and nobody reacts to it and it is a disclosure of nothing. Do not print a count of anybody who knows anything about him, in any file, for any reason, and do not let Chapter 280's paragraph be read as one: it is the ABSENCE of the asking that is the finding, and a Batch 0004 writer must not convert it into one.
7. **Every figure in these ten chapters is in `outline/volume-06.md`'s money table except one derivation, and that one is the two hundred years, and the working is printed in the fiction at `275:13` in a yard.** **AND THE ONE FIGURE THAT IS NOT IN THE TABLE AND IS NOT A NEW FIGURE IS THE TEN POUNDS AND TEN SHILLINGS at `275:77`, and it is the difference between two table rows and the working is printed.**
8. **Ask the third question no regex can ask.** A clean legality sweep is not a clean chapter, and this batch is the batch that proves it hardest: thirty-three legal phrases and nine defects in the first writing, **and a tenth at `276:58` in the delivery itself, which every sweep above had certified, and which the review of Batch 0003 found by reading a bar's mouth against a bell-founder's day.**
9. **The four weekdays and the eleven spans are gone and the standing sentence at `258:47` has not been printed, reworded, repeated or paraphrased, and the ONE non-panel trace in the batch is at `272:11`, in his own interior, in one unfinished sentence, with no formatting and no name for it.** A Batch 0004 writer may add one more and may not add a third in this volume.
10. **Do not let a later batch restage the day's opening, the day's list, the plank's paint and grain, the bar at the end of the lane, the board's figure at the second light, or the sentence *you have just said nine words about a week of four*.** All six were in this batch's first writing and all six were reworded on delivery.
11. **Do not let the batch's own new material be read as the reversal being spent twice.** Ellerby's board of names is public and outside and complete and a floor. The board in the tower is private and inside a door and a list of people and a hand that a clerk can date. **Chapter 273 makes the distinction in the fiction and no chapter may let the two rhyme again.**
12. **Do not name a weekday, a month-name, an anchor, a stone, the First Witness, or a position beneath anything, and do not use the word *stone* for masonry.**
13. **Do not let a hand-off be wrong about a character's sex or age.** The clerk of forty-four is a man of forty-four and **is not named in this batch and has not been named in eleven chapters of Volume 05, and no chapter of this batch gave him a name and a Batch 0004 writer may not.** Isel Cardew is a woman of fifty-one. Petrow Sill is a man of thirty-five. Wenna Skell is a woman of about sixty. Nell Callow is twenty-nine. Sable Venn is a woman.
14. **Do not prune the state files inside a phase that is also writing prose, and do not edit the archive blocks below the top block of any of the six.**
15. **AND THE FIFTEENTH THING, WHICH IS THE ONE NO INSTRUMENT IN THIS PROJECT WILL FIND, and it is the finding of this batch: the thing the column counts is still unknown and it is a question the panel left open on purpose.** Nobody may answer it in Batch 0004 or 0005, nobody may sum it, and a writer who needs the number must go and ask a person of a village.

### 10. THE FIFTEEN THINGS A BATCH 0004 WRITER NEEDS AND CANNOT GET FROM A FIGURE

1. **That the two villages in eleven are Trope and Nettlebed, and that Trope has never had a tower and Nettlebed has had one with nothing in it for about two hundred years, and that a bell in Trope is a handbell at the lane end rung by a woman of fifty-one.** A Batch 0004 writer may not discover this again; it is a finding and it cost a nine-mile walk and it is in two chapters.
2. **That the four words at the head of the plank are ANY ONE MAY SAY NO, in a hand dated at about four hundred years by a man whose trade is dating hands, and that a recordwright of twenty-eight read it three times in one morning and got three right answers, and that the reading is not reprinted in this batch's prose and may not be reprinted in Batch 0004's.**
3. **That a hundred and forty-one is the count and that about seventy of them are in the other ten villages and every one of them can be got at, and that the one in a blue coat cannot, and that the difference between a hundred and forty-one and Ellerby's two hundred and nine is sixty-eight and nobody has ever worked out what it is.**
4. **That the column is a figure against every name with no heading, that the last nine figures are in a woman of fifty-one's grandmother's hand, that the column stopped about two hundred years ago, and that NOBODY ADDED IT UP and a man of fifty-two refused to in front of nineteen people on purpose.** Chapter 275's thousand and fifty pounds is a DIFFERENT figure and the two are not the same and must not be conflated.
5. **That a clerk of forty-four is walking down the lane to a town that keeps weeks he has never learned in Chapter 280 and will be four hundred miles off in about ten days, and that he comes back because a thing kept four hundred years in a village that has no bell is going to have to be looked at by somebody, and that he cannot spend a good name and is the only person in this county who can be relied on to say what a document is.**
6. **That the register exists, is on four sheets cut out of the back of a bill, is kept by a recordwright of twenty-eight at a press in Coldharrow-edge, has fifty-one places on it and NOT ONE VILLAGE IN IT, and that nobody may go in without a sentence said by a person of that village in that village.** Its bounds are at `278:37` and `278:45` and they are eleven lines and they are load-bearing.
7. **That the boy of seventeen's book has a third place in it and that he has not found it yet, and that he has said he will think about it and will not be hurried and that he can be asked about it in about six weeks.** Six weeks from the second day of the sixty-second week is about the middle of the sixty-fourth, and that is a date nobody in this country can produce and the asking of it is Batch 0004's problem and not a day.
8. **That Sable Venn has about eleven weeks in the March, has asked nobody anything in any of them, has about thirty people who will do what she says, and has been told what thirty people are for in eleven words by a woman of fifty-one, and that she is not relieved.**
9. **That three men from four hundred miles off came up the lane in the sixth day of the sixty-first week about that door, that one of them is a man of thirty-four who cannot read the thing he reads out, that they were told only that a document is a thing that can be produced, and that one of them said *if it cannot be produced then it has not happened*, which is Batch 0005's corridor in a stranger's mouth and must not be used before the corridor exists.**
10. **That a carter of thirty-five called Petrow Sill lives at the end of a bad road called Stennick, has about eleven people in his house and nine farms on his lane, and that the second lane in the network is his.**
11. **That the round is the same in three instruments and that all eleven of its places are on the register and that the eleventh village on the lane has still never been named in twenty-eight days.**
12. **That the four bells are a height above nothing and that the second line of a copy of four lines is a schedule and not a river and that the copy is under a seat of a timber cart and will be under a seat on the day it goes.**
13. **That a woman of about fifty at the bar has a board with AT THE BAR at the head of it, has been at that bar twenty-two years, has said no to a date about nine hundred times, has never once said the word no to anything else, and that she is not asked anything by anybody in this batch either.**
14. **That the two hundred is a round number nobody chose, and that it is four bells a day for fifty days, and that it is the whole of the argument of the volume in one subtraction, and that the board has no column for it.**
15. **And the thing that is not a fact and is the only reason this list exists: a person of the March is not a production, and Isel Cardew and Petrow Sill and a man of about thirty-one with a hurdle and a key on a nail are people of a country, and nobody was sent for, and the count is at `state/character-state.md` and a later pass may not add to it without saying so.**

### 11. WHAT THIS BATCH'S OWN FIRST WRITING GOT WRONG, ALL NINE FOUND BY THE THIRD QUESTION, AND THE OTHERS FOUND BY THE PROJECT'S OWN INSTRUMENTS

**THE NINE, AND EVERY ONE OF THEM IS A BACK-REFERENCE PUT ON THE WRONG DAY OF THE WEEK, WHICH IS THE ELEVENTH CLASS OF DEFECT THIS PROJECT HAS NOW PRODUCED AND THE FIRST TIME ONE BATCH HAS PRODUCED NINE OF THEM.** The Day-Phrase sweep returned every one of them legal and the third question found all nine. **`273:111` put a man with a barrow four days ago where he was seven; `272:87` put a line said on the fifth day of the fifty-ninth week on the second; `275:13` and `275:31` put two references to Chapter 253 in the fifty-ninth week when Chapter 253 is the third day of the FIFTY-EIGHTH; `276:24` and `276:38` put a tower reading three days before when it was two, and a kitchen eight days ago when it was nine; `277:13` put a sum done in six days when it was twelve, and `277:31` and `277:43` put two references to Chapter 265 nine days ago when they were twelve; `279:11` put the technique nine days ago when it was twelve.** All nine are corrected and the chapter line count of every one of the ten files is unchanged, so every citation below this point still points where it pointed on delivery.

**AND THE OTHERS, which are the class a later batch will make:**
- **TWELVE WEEKDAYS, in six chapters, and they are the standing prohibition and they were in the prose in the plainest possible English.** Removed.
- **THE RECORDWRIGHT'S NINE WORDS WERE SAID A FOURTH TIME, at `274:7`, inside a report of a clerk's yard, and it would have made `270:85` false, which is the exact defect the repair of 27 September 2026 made and which this batch made again in a different room.** It is the third time this project has put those nine words in the wrong mouth. **A Batch 0004 writer must treat the sentence as spent, and the way to treat a spent sentence is not to reword it.**
- **THE WORD *STONE* SIX TIMES, for masonry.** The prohibition is about a relic and a scanner cannot tell the difference.
- **A LIST OF TWENTY RING-COUNTS INVENTED IN CHAPTER 271 AND WRONG IN ITS TENTH ITEM AND ITS LENGTH.** The first writing of Chapter 271 had the man of fifty-two read out twenty days of rings, and the list had twenty-one items and the tenth was a four where the chain says a five. **It is out, and what replaced it is a run of nine mornings of four found in the grain, which is better, is datable by nobody, and cannot be got wrong.**
- **THREE HUNDRED AND SEVEN SHARED SEVENTY-CHARACTER WINDOWS on the first writing, from thirteen sources, of which the ones a later batch will make again are: the reprinted fourth line of a printed form, a man of sixty-three's eleven-year uncomputed cause, a woman with a barrow at a bar that is not shut, the paint and grain of a plank, the other half of a bell-founder's sentence, the market as the only day anything comes up the lane, a day's opening in the shape of the sixth day of the sixtieth week, *and the shape of Chapter 267's opening promise*, and *the only honest thing left to do in this country*.**
- **FOUR META-NARRATION SENTENCES, and one of them was not caught by the pattern: *it is not the volume's cost*, in Chapter 274, which is this project's own vocabulary in a chapter's mouth.** The pattern catches `this volume` and did not catch that, and the sweep is a shape and not a reading.
- **SEVENTY-ONE INSTANCES OF *MAN OF THIRTY-ONE* against the fifty-seven delivered**, and the cut was made in the chapter with the most people in it, which is the right way round and is also the only way it could have been done.
- **AND ONE DEFECT THE REVIEW OF 27 SEPTEMBER 2026 CLAIMED IT HAD FIXED AND HAD NOT**, and this phase found it by reading and not by scanning: `state/continuity.md`'s live Batch 0002 block still said **sixty-three thousand five hundred and forty-two** in two places, where the figure is sixty-three thousand six hundred and forty-two, and the repair record says that figure was corrected in `270:29`. It is corrected in the live block below this one. **The repair note is now a record of what somebody believed had been done, and the number it corrected was corrected in the chapter and not in the block that describes the chapter, which is the eleventh time this project has fixed a figure in the prose and left it wrong in the record of the prose.**

### 12. THE REPAIR OF THE REVIEW OF BATCH 0003, WHICH FOUND EIGHT FINDINGS AGAINST THE BAND THIS BLOCK RECORDS, AND ALL EIGHT ARE CLOSED

**NO CHAPTER WAS RESTARTED, NO CARD WAS CHANGED, THE PLANNED PLOT IS UNCHANGED, AND ALL FOUR PROSE REPAIRS WERE MADE INSIDE EXISTING LINES, so every `chapter-02xx.md:n` citation in this block, in the other five state files and in `outline/volume-06.md` still points where it pointed on delivery. Three of the four are in Chapters 271, 274 and 275 and one is in 276, and the review's own instruments were re-run over the ten files afterwards and every figure in §2 is the re-measured one.**

1. **A WRONG DAY OF THE WEEK IN A BAR, AT `276:58`, AND IT HAD NEVER BEEN FOUND BY ANY INSTRUMENT IN THIS PROJECT.** A woman of about fifty at the bar, with twenty-two years at that bar and a memory for what a stranger is worth, said there had not been nine at that bar since a timber cart and two barrows and a man with lime came through **in the fourth day of the fifty-ninth week**. **It is the fourth day of the SIXTIETH week, which is Chapter 268, and the fourth day of the fifty-ninth week is Chapter 261, where the board went up five and the timber cart LEFT.** A woman of forty-one's own rule, restated in Chapter 272, is that a bell cannot say which year a thing was in, and a woman at a bar who has been at the bar twenty-two years can, and this project got it wrong in her mouth. **The Day-Phrase sweep returned the phrase legal, because the phrase is legal: the sixty-first week is the fifth day, the sixtieth is the fourth, and a legality check cannot know that a bar remembers a day. This is the twenty-first thing the third question has found and the second time it has found one that every sweep in §2 had certified.** The repair also made her list of that morning match `268:3` — **a timber cart and two men with a barrow, not two barrows** — and the figure nine is CORRECT and was left alone: the day went up thirteen and **nine of the thirteen were strangers**, which is the unit she is counting in, and the repair changed the day and the noun and did not touch the nine. **A TENTH DEFECT WAS FOUND IN THE SAME REVIEW AND WAS NOT IN IT: `277:47`'s *the sixth day of that week* is CORRECT — the sixtieth week's sixth day is Chapter 270, and that is the morning the woman of forty-one read the sheet out in a yard and became a person in a record for one morning — and it was checked and left alone, and a check that comes back clean is worth as much as a check that does not.**
2. **AN UNPAYABLE FORWARD REFERENCE AND A MISATTRIBUTION IN ONE SENTENCE, AT `275:83`, and both were false against the chapters.** The sentence promised that **the sixth of this week** — the sixth day of the sixty-first week, which is Chapter 277 — **was going to happen in a press in a village eleven miles off**, and Chapter 277 is a yard at Coldharrow and its only press is a woman recalling Chapter 269, and the press eleven miles off is Nell Callow's and nothing in the batch goes there again. **The same sentence then gave the argument to a woman who had carried four hundred pieces of paper for nine years without her name at the foot of one of them, which is Sable Venn's figure and is hers at `277:69` and `278:29`, and Sable Venn was not in that stair. The woman who said it was Isel Cardew, fifty-one, at `274:87`: *it is in that column, and there is no name over the column*.** The sentence keeps its whole chain — a heading over a column is a category, a category is a room — and now has the right woman, the right place, and no day it cannot pay: **a woman of fifty-one told him yesterday, in a stair at Nettlebed, that there is no name over the column of figures against a hundred and forty-one names.** *Yesterday* from the fourth day of the sixty-first week is the third day, which is Chapter 274, which is correct. **A forward reference a batch does not pay is not a hook, it is a debt, and the day was the only false part of a chain the volume still needs, so the day went and the chain stayed.**
3. **TWO SEVENTY-CHARACTER RUNS SHARED WITH CHAPTER 270, WHICH §2 ITEM 4 CLAIMED WERE NOT THERE, AND WHICH ARE NOW GONE.** `271:117` and `274:47`, both reworded, both listed at §2 item 4 with the before and the after. **The rule is at §9 item 3 of the Batch 0002 block below and it is the standing rule of this project: a run shared with the previous batch is a defect and is reworded rather than left, and it does not matter that one of the two is a deliberate and signalled callback, because a rule a writer can argue himself out of on the grounds that the echo was on purpose is not a rule.**
4. **THE BATCH 0004 HAND-OFF PROMPT GAVE THREE ACCOUNTS OF ITS OWN ARITHMETIC TRAP AND BLAMED THE WRONG ARTIFACT, and a writer who believed it would not have known which of the file's three tables to trust.** `outline/volume-06.md:540` said the running line was wrong at seven rows, the prompt said the first table had six wrong running figures, and the first table is in fact wrong at **nine of ten rows**, including the two the outline's list omitted, while **the running line is right at all ten and the corrected table is right at all ten**. All three of those statements have been made to agree, and **no figure in either table was changed, because the trap is the device and the trap is deliberate: the morning-figure column of the first table is wrong and its WHAT IS ON IT column is the plan.** Both files now say which column is which.
5. **AND THE THREE SECONDARY FIGURES, all of which were in this block or in the hand-off and none of which was in a chapter.** The span scan's range was called **twenty-nine files and is thirty**; the `stone` prohibition was carried into Batch 0004 in the present tense on a premise that had been spent by the delivery, and both files now say Batch 0003 and say the word is zero; and the refrain union was reported as **three and is five**. All three are corrected at §2 items 4, 8 and 10 and in the Batch 0004 prompt.
6. **AND TWO THINGS THE REVIEW CITED THAT ARE NOT IN THE FILES, recorded so that a later pass does not go looking for them.** It cited `outline/volume-06.md:515` twice for a **twenty-nine files** claim, and that line is Batch 0004's starting condition and carries no such claim and never did; the real carriers were this block at item 4, this block's Batch 0002-era item at `135`, `state/batch-summary.md:57` and the Batch 0004 prompt, and all four are now fixed. **It also reported `stone` as zero over Chapters 251 to 280, and it is zero except for the compound *milestones* at `251:7`, and it reported the chapters' use of *stair* and *steps* as forty-six when it is thirty-eight.** None of the three changes anything; they are here because a review that is wrong about a file is a review that has to be read rather than obeyed, which is the standing instruction of this project and the reason this section exists.

### 1. THE MAP, one row per chapter, in both calendars, and the board's own arithmetic

**THERE IS NO FORMULA IN VOLUME 06 AND THERE IS NOT GOING TO BE ONE. A bell does not keep years. The check is the board chain and the whole of the check is: the figure at the second light on a morning is the previous morning's figure plus that day's rings, and a day's rings are four bells plus what the village did. A later pass must not go looking for a `+n`.**

**Shelf day one is the second day of the fortieth week. The fifty-ninth week is days 133–139, the sixtieth 140–146, the sixty-first 147–153. Batch 0001 ran 126 to 135 and this batch ran 136 to 145, and the day between them is not a gap and is not printed as one. Chapter 271 is Shelf day 146 and its morning board is 63,647.**

| 262 | 59th, 5 | 137 | **63,584** | 0 = **4** | 12 | **A SECOND DAY OF FOUR.** Four accounts and none of them on the list; the route-man's chalk test refused; **a number that anybody can move is not a record**, and the only person it can be moved to is the woman who keeps it |
| 263 | 59th, 6 | 138 | **63,588** | 7 (a boundary walked) = 11 | 13 | the strip is walked again for the first time in nine years and it proves nothing; **he buys a date and a carter of thirty-eight buys a second one and neither tells the other**; two dates is £2 7s, thirty-one days and sixpence, a year of her less £2 18s |

| 270 | 60th, 6 | 145 | **63,642** | 1 (a stranger) = 5 | 20 | **WHAT HE WROTE ON A BOARD IN A PLACE HE WAS NOT IN.** A hundred and thirty-two, which is the whole of nineteen days' rings; **and the question asked a second time, in a doorway, to a person, by a boy of seventeen, with a mark on the end of it, and nobody answers him** |

**THE BOARD CHAIN, run and printed so that it can be checked. Every row is a morning count.** 63,579 + 5 = 63,584 ✓; + 4 = 63,588 ✓; + 11 = 63,599 ✓; + 8 = 63,607 ✓; + 6 = 63,613 ✓; + 5 = 63,618 ✓; + 7 = 63,625 ✓; + 13 = 63,638 ✓; + 4 = 63,642 ✓; + 5 = **63,647** ✓, and 63,647 is Chapter 271's morning and is printed in `outline/volume-06.md` and not here twice.

**THE DAY COUNT IN THE SEVENTH COLUMN, and the rule: a chapter's own day, Chapter 251 being the first.** 261 is the eleventh day and 270 is the twentieth, and the prose says eleven, twelve, thirteen, fourteen, fifteen, sixteen, seventeen, eighteen, nineteen and twenty against those, one in the chapter that carries it, and **the batch's first writing had four of them wrong and all four are corrected: a man of thirty-one in Sable Venn's mouth in Chapter 268 said he had been living on her nine words for eighteen days when it is nine, and in Chapter 269 the same figure is ten days and nineteen days respectively, not eighteen.** The hand-off card for 261 said ten days; the chapter's own day is eleven and the chapter says eleven.

**AND ASK THE THIRD QUESTION NO REGEX CAN ASK — is the named day the day the event happened on.** It found **FIVE** defects in this batch's own first writing, all five corrected on delivery: two chapter openings that carried the previous batch's opening verbatim, a back-reference in Chapter 269 that put the day a woman of forty-one said a county cannot produce a date on the wrong day of that week, and two day-count figures. **THE REVIEW OF 27 SEPTEMBER 2026 RAN IT AGAIN AND IT FOUND SIX MORE THAT THE DAY-PHRASE SWEEP HAD CERTIFIED AS LEGAL** — `265:5`, `265:7`, `265:61` and `265:73` all read *that week* from inside the sixtieth week and so pointed at days that had not happened, `261:91` said *since the fifth day of that week*, which is the following day, and `266:43` said *the fourth day of that week*, which is three days ahead. All six are corrected, the running count is ELEVEN defects this question has found across the batch, and **the Day-Phrase sweep found none of them, which is the strongest argument in this file for running both instruments and never one.** Every day-phrase in the batch is in a legal band, agrees with its chapter's own day, and resolves to the right week.

1. **THE WEEKDAY SCAN: ZERO.** Pattern `\b(mondays?|tuesdays?|wednesdays?|thursdays?|fridays?|saturdays?|sundays?)\b`, case-insensitive, over all ten files.
2. **THE MONTH-WORD SCAN: EIGHT hits and ZERO month-names for a calendar, and the eight are re-measured against the chapter files and not carried forward.** **Six are the modal verb *may***, at `262:15`, `264:23`, `264:71`, `267:17` twice on one line, and `270:11`. **Two are the proper noun *March***, at `263:19` and `269:3`, and both are *the Briar March*. Subtract *may* and subtract *Briar March* and the figure for a calendar is zero.
3. **THE PANEL COUNT: ZERO, and it is a count of panels and not of lines.** The volume's register is three; one is spent at `258:49`–`50`; the second belongs to Batch 0003 and the third to Batch 0005. **THIS BATCH SPENT NONE, as the cards and the hand-off both say.** A bold scan over the ten files returns **ZERO `**` marks anywhere**. **THE BATCH'S FIRST WRITING CARRIED TWELVE EMPHASIS-BOLD SPANS**, all of them in Chapters 265, 266 and 269, and **all twelve are converted to italic.** **The italic inventory is SEVENTY-ONE single-asterisk spans, measured after the conversion, re-measured after the repair of 27 September 2026, and never measured before it: 4 / 3 / 6 / 8 / 9 / 7 / 7 / 11 / 7 / 9 by chapter, and it is a measurement and not a limit. The delivery printed SEVENTY and a 10 in Chapter 268; the repair added the one span there. Neither figure is a limit.**
4. **THE DUPLICATE SCAN IS A SPAN SCAN AND NOT A LINE SCAN, AND THE UNIT MUST BE DECLARED.** Normalise whitespace, take every window of exactly *k* characters at every position, deduplicate within each chapter, then count. **At seventy characters, threshold and unit declared: 119,503 distinct spans in the batch, 61 repeated-within occurrences, and ZERO shared with Chapters 201 to 260. At forty characters: 118,674 distinct spans, 254 repeated-within occurrences, and 736 short carry-overs shared with Chapters 201 to 250 — connective tissue, below the threshold at which a span means anything.** **THE BATCH'S FIRST WRITING RETURNED TWO HUNDRED AND THIRTY-ONE shared seventy-character runs against Chapters 201 to 260, and every one of them has been reworded rather than left, and the re-scan returns zero.** The sources were thirteen and they are the useful part of the list, because a later batch will make the same thirteen: the day's-rise opening, the market-day woman with a stick and a board, the count-off-on-her-fingers, *because he has not written down what is said in a yard since a market town four hundred miles off*, *about nine hundred people who cannot pay a pound three shillings and sixpence for a day out of a village*, *whatever she decided about him happened in that second*, the hedge sentence of the fourth day of the fifty-eighth week, his own sentence of the same day about the first one when there are two, *for about as long as it takes to cross a yard*, and **the nine words of the recordwright, which are a refrain and are the one thing in the list that cannot be reworded without changing the volume, and were therefore broken at the attribution and not at the sentence. AND THE REPAIR OF 27 SEPTEMBER 2026 FOUND THAT ONE OF THE THIRTEEN WAS NEVER ACTUALLY REWORDED: *for about as long as it takes to cross a yard* stood five times, in five chapters, and this list has said since delivery that it had been handled. It is now twice, at `261:41` and `270:57`, and the seventy-character re-scan over the repaired batch returns ZERO against Chapters 201 to 260.**
5. **THE BOARD-COUNT, and it is a count of two different things and both are stated.** **The act of writing a figure in chalk at the foot of somebody else's board happened FIVE times and all five are in Batch 0001, at Chapters 251, 253, 256, 257 and 258. THIS BATCH ADDED NONE, and that is a decision and not an oversight: the third hand in that board is the figure he wrote on his first morning and nothing has been added to it in nineteen days, and that is what makes Chapter 270 legible.** The figure in the chalk is **sixty-three thousand five hundred and ten** and it is the figure on the sheet in Chapter 270. **THE BOARD'S MORNING FIGURE IS NAMED IN WORDS IN ALL TEN CHAPTERS — AND ON DELIVERY THAT WAS A FALSE CLAIM, AND IT WAS THREE OF TEN, at `261:9`, `262:5` and `270:29`, which left six mornings unverifiable in a volume whose engine is that a reader can check the board. The repair of 27 September 2026 put the missing six into 263, 264, 265, 266, 267 and 268, in six different shapes and not as a repeated line, and the ten now read 63,579 / 63,584 / 63,588 / 63,599 / 63,607 / 63,613 / 63,618 / 63,625 / 63,638 / 63,642, which closes the §1 chain exactly. A scan for four or five digits returns ZERO over the batch, as it did before and as it should.**
6. **THE DAY-PHRASE INSTRUMENT: TWENTY-THREE ON TWENTY-TWO LINES after the repair, and SEVENTEEN on delivery. All legal, none unparsed.** Pattern `([a-z]+(?:-[a-z]+)?)\s+day of the ([a-z]+(?:-[a-z]+)?)\s+week`, case-insensitive, and the variant is printed. **THE DELIVERY PRINTED, BY CHAPTER, 1 / 1 / 1 / 2 / 1 / 1 / 1 / 2 / 4 / 1 = 17; THE REPAIR MEASURES 1 / 1 / 2 / 2 / 5 / 1 / 1 / 1 / 5 / 4 = 23.** The rise is not new material and it is not an error: **the repair made six wrong-week back-references explicit instead of leaving them as *that week*, and *that week* is structurally incapable of matching this pattern, so phrases that were invisible to the instrument walked into it.** The instrument found every one of them legal on delivery. The back-reference clusters are Chapters 264, 268, 269 and 270 citing the fourth day of the fifty-eighth week, which is Chapter 254 and is where the hedge was, and Chapters 261, 262, 263, 269 and 270 citing the fourth and fifth day of the fifty-ninth week, which are Chapters 261 and 262.
7. **THE DAY-PHRASE SWEEP IS A LEGALITY CHECK AND NOT A TRUTH CHECK, and it has now returned zero illegal about three hundred and twenty phrases across two volumes and has still caught nothing, while the third question has caught eleven.** That ratio is the finding and it is the reason item 6 exists.
8. **THE FOUR SCANS THAT CAME BACK CLEAN AND ARE RECORDED AS HAVING BEEN RUN, because a sweep that reports zero is a fact about a range and the range is stated every time:** uppercase CORRECT, **zero**; *Remedy Drafter*, **zero**; the word *anchor*, **zero**, and a Volume 06 chapter may not use it in the fiction at all; the word *stone*, **zero**; the First Witness, **zero**; the phrase *right of refusal*, **zero**; any reference to the flat white nothing or to a System panel, **zero**, and the standing sentence the volume prints once at `258:47` is printed nowhere else. **AND TWO INSTRUMENTS THIS BLOCK DID NOT HAVE AND NOW HAS, both added because the review of 27 September 2026 found nine meta-narration sentences that every instrument above returned clean on. A META SWEEP, for `this (volume|project|batch|chapter|stretch|pass)` plus the named strings `two volumes`, `worth printing` and `in a book in`, which returns ZERO over the ten files and returned NINE on delivery. AND A TALLY, not a claim, of the four refrains this volume leans on — *man of thirty-one*, *not a wall*, *it takes to cross a yard*, *would like that/it noticed* — with the count and the chapter beside each, because a count in this file that carries no number beside it is a claim and not a measurement.**
9. **AND A COUNT OF THE PEOPLE WHO KNOW HE CANNOT READ IS NOT PRINTED IN THIS FILE OR IN ANY OTHER, AND NOBODY IN EITHER VILLAGE IS TOLD, AND NOBODY IN THE MARCH WORKS IT OUT.** The thread is carried as a habit and not as a disclosure for the second time: he asks for the sheet to be read out in the way he asks for things, in Chapter 260, Chapter 264 and Chapter 269, and in Chapter 269 he asks a woman of twenty-nine to read a figure back off the bottom of a sheet, and nobody in a press at Coldharrow-edge or in a yard at Coldharrow says one word about the asking, and about four people in Coldharrow have noticed it and stopped noticing it. Refer to `154:53` and `164:49` and to nothing else. **AND ONE THING A LATER PASS MUST NOT MISREAD: the string *cannot read* occurs TWICE in this batch and BOTH are Sable Venn's, in her own mouth, about her own twenty-five-year standing that she does not read other people's records, which is canon at `259:25` and is a different fact about a different person.**

### 3. THE CALENDAR, AND THE MARCH KEEPS THREE AND NONE OF THEM CONVERTS

**The Shelf: fifty-two weeks of seven days, day one the second day of the fortieth week. Volume 05 ran days 71 to 120, Batch 0001 ran 126 to 135, and this batch ran 136 to 145. Nothing turns the grain year in this batch and no file may say that it turns. The four hundred and eighty-five pounds is named in five chapters of Volume 05 and in none of these.**

**The bell: four a day, the first, the change, the noon, the last, and a day begins at the first and ends at the last and nobody in eleven villages has ever said what either is a height above.** **THE BOARD: thirty-one years in two hands, a figure and no column, and a day's figure is a WEIGHTED number.** **THE YEAR TURN: the first bell after the ewes come down off the fell.**

**AND THIS BATCH ADDED THE ARITHMETIC THAT MAKES FOUR MEAN SOMETHING FOR THE FIRST TIME, and it is the volume's finding stated as a sum, in a woman of forty-one's mouth at `261:99`: in twenty-five years the board has gone up fifty-four thousand seven hundred and fifty times; the four bells a day over twenty-five years is one thousand four hundred and sixty a year, which is thirty-six thousand five hundred; and the rest is eighteen thousand two hundred and fifty, and that is every burial and every boundary and every debt and every market and every stranger and every fire anybody in that village has rung for since she was sixteen. So about two rings a day sit on top of the clock, and two rings a day does not touch most days at all, and the days it does not touch are the days the board says four on. A day of four is not a rare day in this county. It is the ordinary one, and that is the whole trouble, and it is why a figure cannot be told from another figure and why a figure that anybody can move is not a record.**

### 4. THE MONEY, every figure recomputed and the working printed beside it, and the unit price printed in the sentence it belongs to

**Every figure this batch uses was in `outline/volume-06.md`'s table before a sentence of the batch was written, and the only NEW figure in the batch is the pair of dates, which the table carried. The instrument is the one this project has used since Volume 04: print the unit price in the sentence, find it, and multiply. A script checks the figures it is given and does not read the sentence they sit in, and this project has been bitten by that in three reviews running.**

| Figure | The working, and it is the whole of it |
|---|---|
| **A carter's day with a handcart on the lane = 18d, on 140 days = £10 10s** | 18 × 140 = **2,520d**; 2,520 − 2,400 = 120d = 10s ✓ |
| **The levy at a gate = 3d a load, at 9 loads a day, on 140 days = £15 15s a village** | 140 × 9 = 1,260 loads; × 3d = **3,780d**; 3,780 − 3,600 = 180d = 15s ✓ |
| **And the excess of the levy over the walking = £5 5s, and the keeper's wage at 9d × 140 days = £5 5s, TO THE PENNY** | 3,780 − 2,520 = **1,260d**; 140 × 9 = **1,260d** ✓ |
| **And the whole of the margin across eleven villages = £57 15s** | 11 × 1,260 = **13,860d**; 13,860 − 13,680 = 180d = 15s ✓ |
| **A bell, new, hung, with rope = £6 15s; and a bell is £1 10s more than a year of the woman** | 31 × 48d = **1,488d**; + 132d = **1,620d**; 1,620 − 1,260 = **360d = £1 10s** ✓ — and the bell is named in this batch once, in the argument about what a road wants, and the difference is not mistaken for three pounds |
| **A Bench date, bought = £1 3s 6d = fifteen days of the man who walks the road and one shilling** | 36 + 6 + 240 = **282d**; 282 ÷ 18 = 15 rem 12 ✓ |
| **AND THE BATCH'S OWN NEW FIGURE, TWICE BOUGHT: two dates = £2 7s exactly** | 2 × 282 = **564d**; 564 − 480 = 84d = 7s ✓ |
| **And two dates in the only money a March village has: thirty-one days of the man who walks the road and sixpence** | 31 × 18 = 558; 564 − 558 = **6d** ✓ |
| **And two dates against a year of the woman who keeps the count: a year of her less £2 18s** | 1,260 − 564 = **696d**; 696 − 480 = 216d = 18s ✓; and 5 pounds 5 shillings − 2 pounds 7 shillings = 2 pounds 18 shillings ✓ |
| **And four dates against a year of the keeper: her year less eleven shillings**, which is the price of using the lane and is named in Chapter 265 in a carter's mouth | 4 × 282 = 1,128d; 1,260 − 1,128 = **132d = 11s** ✓ |

| **AND THE TWENTY-FIVE-YEAR ARITHMETIC THAT MAKES FOUR MEANINGFUL, new in this batch and derived from the row above** | 25 × 2,190 = **54,750**; 25 × 1,460 = **36,500**; 54,750 − 36,500 = **18,250** ✓ |
