### 5. WHAT THE BATCH DELIVERED, **thirty-nine** findings, once each, with the chapter — **and the heading said twenty-eight on delivery and the delivery's own note below it said forty, and both were wrong. The list runs to thirty-nine and a later pass should count it rather than believe the heading or the note.**

1. **A village has held a true document for a day and a half and done nothing with it, and the reason is not inertia and not a decision anybody took: there is nothing in the yard to decide anything with, and the woman who read it out loud said in the same breath that she was not going to have it here.** `261:13`
2. **Three people pull one rope within two seconds and it is one ring, and the woman of forty-one can prove three reasons and one number, and the only thing on her list worth one is a death, and nobody in Coldharrow died on the fourth day of that week.** `261:39`
3. **The only figure on the list that has a name attached to it is the burial, and it works, and it works because a woman stands in a yard and shouts a name at two hundred and ten people.** `266:17`
4. **A day of four and a day of nothing and a day of nine strangers are the same number, and the finding that used to be a hard thing is the easiest thing in the county, because it has never cost anybody anything until now.** `262:55`
5. **THE FIRST USE OF ANYTHING HE HAS LEARNED IN THIS COUNTRY, in his own mouth and it is small: he can account for last night's bell because he has been standing in a yard doing nothing for ten mornings, and a man who is doing nothing watches the hands, and a person who has to ask what a ring is for ends up watching the hands.** `261:79`
6. **A number that anybody can move is not a record, and the count is the easiest thing in this county for a person to move and the hardest thing in it to argue with afterwards, and the only person it can be moved to is the woman who keeps it.** `261:87`
7. **AND THE HALF OF IT THAT IS WORSE, in her mouth: a number that anybody can move is a number that has to be asked about, and there is exactly one person in this county you can ask, and for twenty-five years that was a comfort and it is a door, and she heard somebody walk up to it this morning.** `262:59`
8. **The route-man's rule about a week of four is a rule and not a document and nobody can say where it comes from, and the test he set himself on the first day of that week — ask the first man in the lane what day it is — returns in about nine seconds the same answer four men from counties with roads in it have got out of that woman in twenty-five years.** `262:41`
9. **He is not the fifth hand on a board in a village he has been in eleven days, and the reason is that the only way to keep a thing in this country is to write it on somebody else's board.** `262:43`
10. **A boundary walked nine years ago is walked again and the seventh ring goes onto the board and it is the same seven, and the board cannot say they are the same walk and never will, and about eleven families are grazing inside it.** `263:5`
11. **A woman of about sixty in Ellerby has a name and it is said out loud in a lane for the first time in nine years, and the name is Wenna Skell, and she is the only living person who was standing on the strip when it was decided.** `263:7`
12. **Two people who bought the same day have not made a day twice; they have made one day with two people standing in front of it, and a room will take whichever of the two it likes, and it has never once in six years taken the one she would have chosen.** `263:29`
13. **AND THE FINDING HE SAID OUT LOUD ON A ROAD AND WROTE DOWN AFTERWARDS: he had a moment on a bench at a press at which he could have told the woman of forty-one what he was doing, and he bought the day instead, and a man with no answer has no business making an argument about it.** `263:51`
14. **A carter of thirty-eight asked a woman of twenty-nine for a date for a lane, and told her where it goes and how long he has walked it, and she said: tell me the day it was laid out. He said it was always there. She said: then I cannot help you. And that is the whole of a day in this county in one sentence from a woman of twenty-nine.** `263:65`
15. **A man of thirty-eight pays a pound three shillings and sixpence for a day that is no use to him whatever, so that a day of any kind exists in this county with his name at the foot of it, and he would do it again tomorrow, and he stood in a yard and said he would stand there and listen to what anybody had to say about it.** `263:67`
16. **THE WHOLE OF THE MONEY IN ONE SUBTRACTION, printed in a yard: two dates is two pounds and seven shillings, and it is thirty-one days of the man who walks the road and sixpence, and a year of the woman who keeps the count is five pounds five shillings, and two dates is a year of her less two pounds and eighteen shillings, and about four people in that yard understood that the figure was not a price at all and was the entire annual wage of a woman spent in an afternoon by three men.** `263:37`
17. **A market is the only day anything at all comes up the eleven-village lane, and that is a fact about the county that nobody in it had ever had cause to notice, and it is the reason a form arrived on the seventh day of that week and not before.** `264:5`
18. **THE CHAMBER'S ANSWER, and it is correct: a copy is not evidence, a copy is paper, an acknowledgment is evidence of one thing only, which is that a person was in a place and read a copy and is willing to say so afterwards. That is the only thing that makes a piece of paper worth carrying four hundred miles.** `264:37`
19. **AND IT IS WRONG ABOUT THE PLACE IN THREE WAYS, and in the same three ways in every country it has ever been in, and not one of the three is anybody being a villain: it asks for a room and the reading was in a yard; it asks for a day in the grain year of the seat and the fell year is not a day; and it says the acknowledgment will be entered in the district named in the copy, and that district is four hundred miles off, so this county would put the only name it has got into a record in a valley that has never heard of it.** `264:41`
20. **The fourth line of the form is a door that is built into it by somebody who was being fair: where no day can be shown, the acknowledgment may be entered without one, and the entry will be marked as made without a day. It is not a door anybody has to open.** `264:69`
21. **The form wants a person who was in a room when a copy was read out, and the county has exactly one, and she is the woman who read it out in a yard, and nobody can ask her because nobody in eleven villages has a person to ask.** `264:77`
22. **AND THE SENTENCE THAT IS THE FIRST HALF OF WHAT THE BATCH COSTS, said to a wall that cannot hold it: I have been the only one who can be asked in this county for twenty-five years and it has been a comfort, and I did not know until about a minute ago that a thing like that can be spent, and now I know, and I have not told anybody, and I would like it written down somewhere that is not a wall.** `264:79`
23. **A woman with a board sent for that form eight weeks ago, in the week she had a copy made, and told nobody, and is not thanked, and is on no paper, and there is no name in that yard that belongs to her.** `264:55`
24. **THE BATCH'S CENTRAL PRESSURE, SAID OUT LOUD IN FULL BY THREE ORDINARY PEOPLE IN ORDINARY MOUTHS BEFORE ANYBODY ASKED THEM: use it, take it out, tell the eleven families. And then the finding, said by the man who was in the middle of it and was not asked: they are not three positions, they are three prices. Using it costs the levy, which keeps nothing, and a road wants four dates a year, which is the woman's year less eleven shillings. Exposing it costs a date this county cannot make. Destroying it costs the eleven families the water off ninety acres they have had for nine years, and it is the only one of the three there is no figure for anywhere, and it is going to be the one somebody pays.** `265:61`
25. **A woman of sixty who is the only person alive who can say where a boundary started says she wants the hedge taken out, and gives the reason in her own mouth and it is not a principle: being the only one who can say where it started is not an honour, it is a job nobody gave her and nobody can take off her and she is about sixty.** `265:41`
26. **A woman of forty-one who has carried a fact about ninety acres for nine years because nobody ever asked her for it says she wants the eleven families told, in nine seconds, in a yard, as the plainest thing anybody said there — and THE ELEVEN FAMILIES ARE NOT TOLD ANYTHING IN THIS BATCH, and the reason is the volume's and not a convenience: the batch's problem is that nobody in eleven villages can produce a day, and a fact told to eleven families without a day on it is eleven families in a lane with nothing to be right about.** `265:53`
27. **ELLERBY'S BOARD IS TWO HUNDRED AND NINE NAMES AND NO FIGURES, nobody has ever struck a name out, about seventy of the two hundred and nine are people who live in four other villages and can be asked because a name is an address, and there is a name on it of a man in a blue coat. **THE DELIVERY ALSO SAID *about thirty-one people in the village are not on it*, WHICH DID NOT ADD UP AGAINST ELLERBY'S HUNDRED AND FORTY AND HAS BEEN REMOVED FROM THE CHAPTER, FROM `266:97` AND FROM THIS ITEM** that there is nobody in the village for, and they have left it there because a name with nobody at the end of it is still better than a number, and everybody in that village has understood that for about four hundred years without ever putting it in a sentence.** `266:67`
28. **THE DIFFERENCE BETWEEN THE TWO VILLAGES, in one sentence: a number says how many times and names nobody, and a name says who and can be asked; Coldharrow has the first and cannot produce the second, Ellerby has the second and does not know that is what it has; and the same four lines are worth nothing in Ellerby and are worth something again the moment a person in Ellerby stands in a doorway and hears them read out, which is the one thing he knows how to do and the one thing he cannot make anybody do.** `266:73`
29. **THE TECHNIQUE, DEFINED ONCE IN THE FICTION AND IN HIS OWN MOUTH, and it is not a lesson: you may put two things next to each other, you may not make them one, and the price of putting them next to each other is that the two people in the middle of them find out about each other, and neither of them was asked, and there is no instrument in this country that stops it happening, and he had looked for one, and he did it anyway, because the alternative is two things in two counties nine miles apart that are each worth nothing.** `267:17`
30. **The sentence he wrote down afterwards and gave to nobody: he was right about the mechanics, the mechanics were right, the human meaning of it arrived late as it always does, and it arrived on two women in a county of two hundred and ten people, and he did not find out that he had done it until the second of the two had already said it back to him; and he has had this before, twice, both times it was a hand on a table and both times he took the hand away again, and this is the same failure in a different medium and the only thing that has changed is the medium.** `267:69`
31. **And what a woman of forty-one said about it, and it is not anger: you have made me a person in a room and I did not ask to be one, and I have been one for nine years, and I did not know it was a room until a man of thirty-one stood in a doorway and put me in it in front of nine people. And she is not being magnanimous, she is being accurate, and she asks for it to be written down somewhere that is not a wall by somebody who is not in it, and does not say him.** `267:57`
32. **THE BAD WORK, WHICH IS THE METHOD WORKING: a person reads aloud and two people who are neither of her decide what it means, and because the two who decide cannot read they have to agree out loud in front of four witnesses, and there were eleven disagreements, and eleven disagreements are the reason the sentence that came out is the shape it is. And it is two hundred words with no name at the foot of it and no day on it, and the not putting of the day is the only protection anybody in that kitchen has got. **AND IT BELONGS TO SABLE VENN, NOT TO THE RECORDWRIGHT: THE DELIVERY ASSIGNED IT TO THE RECORDWRIGHT AT `268:51`, INSIDE A SPEECH THAT WAS NOT HERS AND THAT ALSO GAVE HER A FOURTH TELLING OF THE NINE WORDS, WHICH WOULD HAVE MADE `270:85` FALSE. SABLE VENN PUTS THE DAY THERE IN HER OWN MOUTH AT `268:51` NOW, AND THE RECORDWRIGHT SAYS THE NINE WORDS EXACTLY THREE TIMES** `268:25`, `268:51`
33. **AND THE THING SHE SAYS IN HER OWN MOUTH AND DOES NOT APOLOGISE FOR: I put four lines into this country and I did not read them, and you have made a woman of sixty read one out, twice, in a lane and in a kitchen, and she is not a document, and I know the difference, and it has taken me nine years and a copy to learn and it has taken you eighteen days, and it has cost her nothing at all because nobody asked her either, and I was one of the people who did not ask her, and I was one of them for nine years.** `268:61`
34. **AND THE THING BENEATH IT, which she had said she would say later and said in a lane: it worked four hundred times because of what she would not know and not because of what she could not do; there is a difference and she had never once thought about it; **A MAN OF THIRTY-ONE, AND THE DELIVERY PRINTED TWENTY-EIGHT, WHICH IS THE RECORDWRIGHT'S AGE AND NOT HIS, AND WHICH HAD BEEN PROMOTED TO CANON IN THIS ITEM** asked her what happens to a piece of paper and she could not tell him, and he said *and yet*, and the and yet has been in her head for ten days.** `269:71`
35. **THE BATCH'S COST, and it is in a man of thirty-one's own hand on a leaf he took out of a book: a woman of twenty-nine asked him for a number and he had nothing else to give, and the only figure he has ever put into this country in his own hand is the chalk on the foot of somebody else's board from the first morning, and he gave that, and a number with a hand in it is a person, and a person is what two hundred words and a woman of twenty-nine not putting a day on it were for. **THE DELIVERY SAID THE DOCUMENT SPENT ELEVEN DAYS AND TWO HUNDRED WORDS; IT SPENT ONE MORNING AND TWO HUNDRED WORDS, AND THE ELEVEN DAYS WAS A FIGURE OF THE ROUTE'S AGE MISTAKEN FOR A FIGURE OF THE WORK.** `269:35`, `269:43`
36. **He did not take it back, and the reason is the reason he did not offer to wipe the chalk off on the first morning: a man who takes a thing off a board in front of two hundred and ten people has admitted he had no business putting it there, and the only thing that would make it not a different act is if he had never done it, and he had, and he did it in four minutes on a first morning in a country where nobody had asked him anything.** `269:47`
37. **A number on a wall is a thing about a village and a number on a piece of paper is a thing about a person, and on the wall it is ours and on the paper it is his, and there is no column on that board that says whose hand anything was and there never was one and there is not going to be one — and she has been a person in a record for one morning and did not ask to be one and is not going to be able to undo it and is not going to be told she is sorry for it.** `270:65`
38. **A hundred and thirty-two, printed by a man of fifty-two on the side of a cart in a yard, is not a figure of anything: it is the shape of nineteen days, and a hundred and thirty-two is nineteen days or eleven burials or forty-four strangers or sixteen boundaries, and the last of the nineteen days was a day of four, and four is what the board says about most days in a year, so the very last day of the nineteen, the one nearest the sheet, is the one day out of the nineteen you could not find with a lamp and a boy. **AND THE FIGURE THIS ADDS TO IS 63,510 + 132 = 63,642, WHICH IS WHAT `270:29` NOW SAYS; THE DELIVERY PRINTED 63,542 THERE, WHICH WAS OUT BY A HUNDRED AND CONTRADICTED THIS VERY ITEM.** `270:29`, `270:41`
39. **And the volume's question, asked a second time, in a different mouth, in a different shape, and not answered: a boy of seventeen asks it in a doorway, to a person, and writes it in a line book that has never had a question in it because a question is not a thing that crossed, and it has a mark on the end of it. The man of thirty-one had the one sentence he had the standing to say and did not say it. Nothing came. He said: I do not know. And the boy said: that is two of us.** `270:97`, `270:101`, `270:113`

**AND THE COUNT IN THIS HEADING WAS WRONG THREE TIMES, WHICH IS THE ELEVENTH TIME THIS PROJECT HAS TYPED A COUNT INSTEAD OF COUNTING ONE. The heading said TWENTY-EIGHT, the note beneath it said FORTY, and the list runs to THIRTY-NINE. All three numbers are in the archive and none of them is rewritten, the corrected figure is in the heading, and the note stands because a state file in which every earlier count has been quietly replaced is a state file that has stopped being evidence.**

### 6. THE DEVICES THE BATCH SPENT, EACH ONCE

1. **The brass, spent once, at `262`.** Four bells, a rope, and anybody who can reach it, used the way a bell is used and not as a weapon and not as a plot device: three people and three reasons and one pull and one ring, and the count is the only record in the county, and it is the easiest thing in the county to move. **It is not a restage of device 1: a counter with no units names nothing, and this one names a number correctly and the number is wrong about the world.**
2. **The technique with its price, spent once, at `267`.** Two cases put side by side and not joined, in a doorway, in about nine minutes, and the price of it is that the two women at the centre of them come to know about each other, and there is no instrument in this country that stops that happening.
3. **A figure leaving a board inside a copy of four lines that were not read, spent once, at `269` and `270`.** The count leaves the board for the first time in thirty-one years, in his hand, on the copy, and he did not do it on purpose and cannot undo it and does not try to, and a woman of forty-one finds out in a yard in a village he was standing in while she was not looking, and **the chapter does not have him offer to wipe it.**

**AND THE TEN DEVICES BATCH 0001 SPENT WERE NOT RESTAGED. The five formulae Batch 0001's own span scan named as deliberate — the board's paint and grain and the size of a door, the list of a day's rings, *a market needs a day and Coldharrow does not have the kind of day a market needs*, *the man of thirty-one was in a yard at the second light for the express purpose of*, and *times a hundred and forty is one thousand two hundred and sixty pence* — and this batch's first writing used two of them (`the man of thirty-one was in a yard at the second light for the express purpose of`, at `266:3`, and the list of the day's rings) and **both have been reworded in the delivery, so the two formulae that this batch repeated out of Batch 0001 now appear in one chapter each and not in a third. THE REVIEW OF 27 SEPTEMBER 2026 FOUND THE LAST SENTENCE OF THAT CLAIM WAS ALSO WRONG AND IT WAS WRONG IN THE OPPOSITE DIRECTION: THE DAY'S-OPENING FORMULA WAS NOT IN ONE CHAPTER, IT WAS IN FOUR — 265, 267, 268 AND 269, THREE OF THEM THE SAME SHAPE. THE REPAIR LEAVES IT IN 268 ALONE, WHERE THIRTEEN IS THE LARGEST FIGURE IN THE BATCH, AND 265, 267 AND 269 NOW OPEN IN THREE DIFFERENT SHAPES. THE DAY'S LIST IS IN 262, ALONE. A DELIVERY THAT UNDER-REPORTS A REPETITION IS AS DANGEROUS AS ONE THAT OVER-REPORTS IT, AND BOTH HAVE NOW HAPPENED IN THIS VOLUME.**

### 7. THE THREADS IN AND THE THREADS OUT

**IN: twenty-two, being the eighteen Volume 05 handed on plus the four Batch 0001 produced. OUT: twenty-two, plus SIX produced, and NONE resolved.** The eighteen Volume 05 threads come out of this batch exactly as they went in and their full text is in the `## VOLUME 06 BATCH 0001` block of `state/open-threads.md`, which is now archive. **The four threads Batch 0001 produced are all still open and all were advanced: the unprovable boundary and the eleven families, advanced four times and still not told anything; the sealed copy and the man who carried it, which has now been to Coldharrow-edge and back twice and has a figure on it; the line book and a boy of seventeen, whose book has for the first time a question in it; and the question a carter asked in a yard, which is asked a second time and is still not answered.**

**THE SIX THIS BATCH PRODUCED, and a production is not a resolution:** the form of the Water-Seat of Sallowby and the person it wants; the three prices of the eleven-village lane; the two-hundred-and-nine names on a wall in Ellerby; the first route out of the county that anybody chose; a figure in a stranger's hand at the foot of a sheet; and the two women who now know about each other.

### 8. WHAT THIS BATCH DID NOT DO

**It did not deliver the volume's midpoint reversal**, which belongs at Chapter 271, 272, 273 or 274 and in the second half of Batch 0003, and which is the series' own sentence: *null zones are revealed as communities that preserved the original right of refusal after the Codification, not random magical failures.* **Chapters 261 to 270 treat a null zone as damage — a road that ends and a lane that does not, a name that drops out of a record and comes back — and a place that is named in one doorway and not in the next. No chapter here may be read as paying it, and no character in these ten chapters speculates it, and no character in these ten chapters is right about it.**

**It spent no panel. It did not resolve the split with Sera Quill.** She said the nine words a third time at `270:85` and it was not softened, and the split is not repaired, and the volume's engine is not a quarrel. **It did not tell the eleven families anything, and the record made by Batch 0001 that they would not be told in Batch 0002 has been kept and the reason is now printed in the fiction.** **It did not put the four lines into a room.** Nobody from any building with a yard came to Coldharrow; a thing came and it was a document. **It did not name a weekday, a month, an anchor, a stone, the First Witness, a position beneath anything, or his own inability to read.** **It did not undo anything and it did not relieve anybody.** **It did not restore the right of refusal, and the words are not in it.** **It did not compress the volume's new question into one word, and it did not answer it, and the civic form is now asked twice, once by a carter of thirty-eight in a yard to nobody without a mark and once by a boy of seventeen in a doorway to a person with one.**

### 9. THE FIFTEEN THINGS A LATER PASS MUST CHECK HARDEST IN THIS BATCH

1. **The board chain: 63,579 to 63,642 in nine exact steps and 63,647 on the first morning of Batch 0003.** Run it morning by morning against the table in `outline/volume-06.md` and do not believe a figure out of a block.
2. **The chapter's-own-day rule, and the FOUR figures this batch's first writing got wrong about how long a man or a woman has been in the country, plus the SIX wrong-week back-references the review of 27 September 2026 found on top of them.** Sable Venn's nine words are spoken in Chapter 259, which is the ninth day; on the eighteenth day it is nine days and on the nineteenth it is ten. **The six wrong-week back-references are the same class and they had survived a legality sweep: `265:5`, `265:7`, `265:61` and `265:73` read *that week* from inside the sixtieth week, `261:91` pointed at the following day, and `266:43` pointed three days ahead.** A later pass must read every back-reference against the chapter's own day by hand, because the instrument that checks legality is not the instrument that checks truth and this project has now been bitten by that eleven times.
3. **The span scan at seventy and at forty, and the unit declared, and the range declared.** The first writing returned two hundred and thirty-one shared runs against Chapters 201 to 260 and the delivery returns zero. A run shared with the previous batch is a defect and is reworded rather than left.
4. **The bold scan must return exactly zero marks in this batch, because the volume's second panel belongs to Batch 0003 and a bold instrument that counts two things is an instrument that does not work.** Seventy italic spans and no limit on them.
5. **The chalk count is FIVE for the whole volume so far and all five are in Batch 0001, and this batch added none.** The figure in the chalk is sixty-three thousand five hundred and ten and it is the figure on the sheet in Chapter 270; a later pass that finds a sixth has found a defect.
6. **The string *cannot read* appears twice in this batch and both are Sable Venn's, about her own standing, and both are canon.** Do not read either of them as a disclosure about him, and do not print a count of anybody who knows anything about him, in any file, for any reason.
7. **Every figure in these ten chapters is in `outline/volume-06.md`'s money table and the only new one is the pair of dates.** The twenty-five-year arithmetic at §3 and the hundred-and-thirty-two at §4 are derived in prose and the working is printed in the fiction itself.
8. **Ask the third question no regex can ask.** A clean legality sweep is not a clean chapter, and this batch's own first writing produced five defects that the sweep would not have seen.
9. **Do not let a later batch restage the day's opening or the day's list in a third chapter.** Two of this batch's formulae were reworded on delivery for exactly this reason.
10. **Do not let a later pass undo anything.** The form of the Water-Seat is unanswered, the eleven families are still not told, the copy is still going about under seats and in a satchel, the third hand is still in the chalk, and nobody is relieved.
11. **Do not let the midpoint reversal land in Batch 0003's first half.** It is at 271 to 274 and in the second half.
12. **Do not let the batch's own new material be read as the reversal.** Ellerby's board of names is an arithmetic difference, not a preserved right. Nothing in Chapters 261 to 270 is a history four hundred years old except a custom about who rings a bell, and that custom is about a bell.
13. **Do not name a weekday, a month-name, an anchor, a stone, the First Witness, or a position beneath anything.**
14. **Do not let a hand-off be wrong about a character's sex or age.** Sable Venn is a woman and the chapter files say so; Wenna Skell, Delya Craven and Marda Skell are named in this batch's own chapters and are the chapters' facts.
15. **Do not prune the state files inside a phase that is also writing prose**, and do not edit the archive blocks below the top block of any of the six.

**Shelf day one is the second day of the fortieth week. The fifty-seventh week is days 119–125, the fifty-eighth 126–132, the fifty-ninth 133–139, the sixtieth 140–146, the sixty-first 147–153. Volume 05 ended on Shelf day 120. Volume 06 opens on the FIRST day of the fifty-eighth week, day 126, and there is no gap printed anywhere: a day with no chapter is a thing the year does.**

**THERE IS NO FORMULA IN VOLUME 06, and that is a finding and not an omission. Volume 05's formula was basin day = Shelf day + 78. Volume 06's instrument is a counter with no units in it, and it does not convert into a Shelf day by any arithmetic available on earth, because a bell does not keep years. A later pass must not go looking for a +n.** The check that replaces the formula is the board chain, and it is the whole of the check: **the figure at the second light on a morning is the previous morning's figure plus that day's rings, and a day's rings are four bells plus what the village did.**

**THE BOARD CHAIN, run and printed so that it can be checked instead of believed. Every row is a morning count.**
63,510 + 5 = 63,515 ✓; + 11 = 63,526 ✓; + 5 = 63,531 ✓; + 6 = 63,537 ✓; + 8 = 63,545 ✓; + 5 = 63,550 ✓; + 7 = 63,557 ✓; + 4 = 63,561 ✓; + 13 = 63,574 ✓; + 5 = 63,579 ✓. **THE OPENING FIGURE IS CHECKABLE, AND THE CHECK IS THE ONE THE FICTION GIVES AND NOT THE ONE THIS BLOCK GAVE ON ITS FIRST WRITING: the board is six years of the man's hand and twenty-five years of the woman's, `251:37` and `251:57` and `253:39` and `258:39` all say so, and the four clock bells a day are 1,460 a year and about six a day is 2,190 a year, and the woman says the extra two a day has been about two a day for as long as she has been counting, which is her twenty-five years. So 6 × 1,460 = 8,760 and 25 × 2,190 = 54,750 and 8,760 + 54,750 = 63,510, which is thirty-one years in two hands. It is not a round number and nobody chose it, and that is the point. **THIS BLOCK PRINTED 50,370 AND A TWENTY-FOUR-AND-SEVEN SPLIT ON ITS FIRST WRITING AND NEITHER IS DERIVABLE FROM ANYTHING THE CHAPTERS SAY, and 6 × 1,460 + 25 × 2,190 = 63,510 while 25 × 2,190 = 54,750 on its own, which is already more than the figure it printed, so the number the state file called checkable was checked by nobody. The review of this phase caught it; every figure in the chain moved by 13,140; the ten mornings are re-printed above and in the outline's table; and the lesson is the one this project keeps learning in a new costume: a state file that prints the working beside a figure still does not check it, and a check nobody ran is not a check.**

**AND ASK THE THIRD QUESTION NO REGEX CAN ASK — is the named day the day the event happened on.** It found four defects in this batch's own first writing and all four are corrected in the prose before delivery. It is the only question that has ever caught anything in this project and it is the cheapest: ten chapter days against fourteen day-phrases, side by side, ten minutes.

### 3. THE CALENDAR, AND THE MARCH KEEPS THREE AND NONE OF THEM CONVERTS

**The Shelf: fifty-two weeks of seven days, Shelf day one the second day of the fortieth week. Volume 05 ran days 71 to 120 and this batch ran 126 to 135, and the five days between are not a gap and are not printed as one.** Nothing turns the grain year in this batch and no file may say that it turns. **Four hundred and eighty-five pounds is named in five chapters of Volume 05 and not in one, and it is named in none of these ten.**

**The March's own three, and it is a count and not a calendar.** **The bell: four bells a day are the clock — the first, the change, the noon, the last — and the first is a height above nothing and the last is a height above the first, and nobody in eleven villages has ever said what either is above.** **The board: thirty-one years in two hands, a figure and no column, and a day's figure is a WEIGHTED number because it is four plus what the village did.** **The year turn: the first bell after the ewes come down off the fell, which moves about three weeks in any year and is the only date in this country a stranger can be taught in a morning.** **And the count of four bells a day is 1,460 a year and about six a day is 2,190 a year, and 2,190 ÷ 11 goes in a hundred and ninety-nine times with one over, so a year of one board is a hundred and ninety-nine whole passages of a cart and one ring.**

### 4. THE MONEY, every figure recomputed in a script and the working printed beside it, and the unit price printed in the sentence it belongs to

**Twenty-two rows were recomputed and returned zero failures. The instrument is the one this project has used since Volume 04: print the unit price in the sentence, find it, and multiply. A script checks the figures it is given and does not read the sentence they sit in, and this project has been bitten by that in three reviews running.**

| Figure | The working, and it is the whole of it |
|---|---|
| **A carter's day with a handcart on the lane = 18d, on 140 days = £10 10s** | 18 × 140 = **2,520d**; 2,520 − 2,400 = 120d = 10s ✓ |
| **The levy at a gate = threepence a load, at nine loads a day, on 140 days = £15 15s a village** | 140 × 9 = 1,260 loads; × 3d = **3,780d**; 3,780 − 3,600 = 180d = 15s ✓ |
| **AND THE VOLUME'S FINDING IN ONE SUBTRACTION: the excess of the whole of the levy over the whole of the walking is £5 5s, and the bell-keeper's wage at ninepence a day for 140 days is £5 5s, TO THE PENNY** | 3,780 − 2,520 = **1,260d**; 1,260 − 1,200 = 60d = 5s ✓; and 140 × 9 = **1,260d** ✓ |
| **And the whole of the margin across eleven villages = £57 15s a year, which is the entire margin of a lane that eleven villages own and eleven people keep** | 11 × 1,260 = **13,860d**; 13,860 − 13,680 = 180d = 15s ✓ |
| **A bell, new, hung, with rope = £6 15s: metal at four shillings a pound on thirty-one pounds, plus frame and rope at eleven shillings** | 31 × 48d = **1,488d**; 1,488 − 1,440 = 48d = 4s ✓; + 132d = **1,620d**; 1,620 − 1,440 = 180d = 15s ✓ |
| **And a bell against a year of the woman who keeps the count: £1 10s, and the outline's table said THREE POUNDS on its first writing and was wrong by the exact class of error the table exists to catch** | 1,620 − 1,260 = **360d**; 360 − 240 = 120d = 10s ✓ |
| **A Bench date, bought = £1 3s 6d: two days' carrying at 18d, two nights at 3d, a clerk's fee of a pound** | 36 + 6 + 240 = **282d**; 282 − 240 = 42d = 3s 6d ✓ |
| **And a date in the only money a March village has: 282 ÷ 18 = 15 remainder 12, so a date is fifteen days of the man who walks the road and one shilling** | 15 × 18 = 270d; 282 − 270 = 12d ✓ |
| **And four dates against a year of the keeper: 4 × 282 = £4 14s, and the difference is 132d = eleven shillings** | 1,128 − 960 = 168d = 14s ✓; 1,260 − 1,128 = **132d = 11s** ✓ |
| **Nell Callow's year: about forty dates at £1 3s 6d = £47 exactly, which is about nine times a keeper's year** | 40 × 282 = **11,280d**; 11,280 ÷ 240 = 47 exactly ✓; 47 ÷ 5.25 = 8.95 ✓ |
| **The bell's own arithmetic, and the board's opening figure, and the split is the father's six and the woman's twenty-five** | 4 × 365 = 1,460 ✓; 6 × 365 = 2,190 ✓; 6 × 1,460 = 8,760 and 25 × 2,190 = 54,750 and 8,760 + 54,750 = **63,510** ✓; and 6 + 25 = 31 ✓ |
| **And what a carter's whole passage is against a year of one board** | 11 × 199 = 2,189; 2,190 − 2,189 = 1 ✓ |
| **The four pages of the hundred-and-fifty-fifth day, copied and sealed at Sallowby: threepence, which is the cheapest thing in this volume and the most expensive** | CANON, `state/continuity.md`, and a man of fifty-one was given five shillings for it and four days and carried it four hundred miles in a satchel under a seat and did not know what it was |

**AND THE INSTRUMENT CAUGHT ONE WRONG FIGURE BEFORE A SINGLE CHAPTER OF VOLUME 06 WAS WRITTEN, which is the whole reason every row above prints a working.** The difference between a bell and a keeper's year is one pound ten shillings and not three pounds, and the first writing of the money table had it wrong because it multiplied instead of subtracting. **A Batch 0002 agent that takes a figure from that table takes the working with it.**

### 5. WHAT THE BATCH DELIVERED, twenty-six findings, once each, with the chapter

1. **A village can prove how many times and can prove before or after, and cannot prove when, and the instrument that fails is a count with no units in it.** `252:45`
2. **A boundary is walked with seven rings, out loud, on a hedge, and the only living record of an afternoon is a woman saying a number while she walks.** `252:31`
3. **THE DEVICE THIS VOLUME IS BUILT ON, said by the man of thirty-one and agreed to by the woman in about four seconds: a day's figure is not how many things happened, it is how much they weighed.** `253`
4. **The list of a day's rings is complete and it is short, and we do not ring for a christening or a marriage, and the four a day are on top of it and are not in it.** `253`
5. **A village of two hundred and ten cannot be asked how many people died in it, or how many strangers, or how many people, and has been asked the last of those four times in twenty-five years and has given the same answer four times.** `252`
6. **A bell is a year of the woman who keeps the count plus one pound ten shillings, and no village has ever set one against the other because it would be a thing to say in a yard.** `253:91`
7. **The two villages with no bell have no board, and the reason nobody has a bell is that the last man in a village who wanted one went without, thirty years before the bell-founder was born, and the cause ran the other way and he has never worked out how.** `253:91`
8. **A board is a plank about the size of a door and a man of sixty-one and a great deal of paint, and that is not a metaphor, it is the whole of the labour.** `258:25`
9. **THE SPLIT, in nine words, from a recordwright, before she was asked, and she does not improve on it: a count that leaves the board is a count somebody can use.** `254:79`, `255:11`
10. **Her family claim of six years is unfiled, is not going to be filed, is mentioned once in this batch in about eleven words, and is given because he asked in front of a witness and would have got nothing in a lane.** `255:33`
11. **A March village's enforcement is a bell and two men at a door and two hundred and ten people in a lane, and it is more than a great many places manage, and a bell-count who is asked about it says we have a thing for it and not that we are a soft country.** `254:43`
12. **The finest thing a boy's line book can say is that a thing happened before this and after that, and that is not a date, and he has lost two pages in the ninth year and his father told him a book with a hole in it is a book with a hole in it.** `254:69`
13. **A formal appeal is a thing that is produced and a formal appeal costs money, and a family that cannot pay for one does not appeal, they walk — and a refusal made by a boy of seventeen against the man who is helping, and the boy is right, and the man does not ask again and says why in his own words.** `254:73`
14. **He knows he would hand the lane over for a good reason, and the reason he gives is that he decided in four days that the answer was to get a second person who can be asked, and he did not ask himself what happens to the first one when there are two.** `254:79`
15. **A place that cannot be dated is a place that cannot be named in a room, and a bench that is sent nothing does not write nothing down, it writes that it was sent nothing, and that is a sentence about a place and not about a person, and a place with no party is a place that can be done to.** `255:55`
16. **He says out loud in a yard that he is going to pay for a date himself and have somebody else pay it too so that it is not him, and that when there are two of them they will be able to be argued with, and that two is worse than one, and the recordwright does the sum in four minutes and says she has said her nine words twice and would like it noticed.** `255:75`, `255:81`
17. **A date is £1 3s 6d and is fifteen days of the man who walks the road and one shilling, and about half a month, and the man who said it has had nine such days a year for nineteen years.** `256:33`
18. **Four dates is a year of the woman who keeps the count less eleven shillings, and the yard went quiet in a way a yard in Coldharrow does not get quiet.** `256:45`
19. **A woman who sells days takes £47 a year, which is about nine times hers, and has not slept especially well about it for two years and has not stopped, because the alternative is not sleeping at all and there are thirty-one houses here that cannot pay a man with a cart to come up the lane once.** `256:57`
20. **THE INCOMPLETENESS, IN HER MOUTH: a date is for a thing that happened on a day; a boundary is a line and the line is there before you walk it and there after you come back; a man can walk a boundary twice and the second time is the one that counts; and she has made fourteen in six years and all fourteen went against the man who asked, and she did not stop to find out what the second walking was for, and she has not gone looking for the other four because she is twenty-nine and has a press and a woman and two children and has never done a thing in this county she could not do on a day she could name.** `256:77`
21. **Named by an ordinary person in a yard and not by the man: a boundary is a thing that can be walked twice, and that is the whole of why that girl is the way she is, and nobody has ever asked her to sell anything else because we are all waiting for somebody to come and make the room stop wanting them.** `256:87`
22. **The whole margin of a lane eleven villages own is fifty-seven pounds fifteen shillings, and it goes on at five pounds five each to eleven women with a cloth, and the other half of the year a bell goes four times a day for nothing, and nobody has ever noticed because nobody in the county is asking.** `257:43`
23. **What a gate is for, asked in four counties for nineteen years, and there is nobody who can answer it, and he has stopped expecting one and started keeping a list.** `257:83`
24. **THE PANEL, and the finding in her own mouth: a quiet day and a day with nine strangers on it are four rings apart, and I have been counting for twenty-five years and nobody has ever asked me which of them it was.** `258:49`, `258:60`
25. **A week of four is a week you do not talk about, and it is the same rule in nine counties and nobody can say where it comes from, and the route-man is not going to be the tenth thing that comes up that wall.** `258:94`
26. **A boundary walked in the twenty-second year of the board, by a man four years dead, over ninety acres about eleven families have grazed for nine years and paid nothing for and were never told about — and the only record that they were told anything is a woman's hand, and a hand is not a witness, a hand is a count — and the recordwright refuses to write it down and says so as a thing she has done and not as a thing she has refrained from, and a woman of forty-one says that is the correct answer and you are not going to get a better one out of her.** `258:70`, `258:78`

### 6. THE VOLUME'S OWN FIGURES AND THE THINGS A LATER PASS GETS WRONG IF IT DOES NOT MEASURE THEM

1. **Do not go looking for a formula in Volume 06.** There is none, and the reason there is none is the volume's central finding. The check is the board chain in §1.
2. **The board's opening figure is 63,510 and the check is 6 × 1,460 + 25 × 2,190, and the six and the twenty-five are what four separate lines of the fiction say the two hands are.** Do not believe it out of a block, and **do not believe the twenty-four and seven that this block printed on its first writing: it was not derivable and the review of this phase is what caught it.**
3. **The chalk happens five times, at 251, 253, 256, 257 and 258, and the morning figure is named in nine of ten chapters.** Both are counts of different things. Print what you are counting. **AND THE FIVE ARE THE FIRST, THIRD, FOURTH, FIFTH AND SIXTH MORNINGS, not five consecutive mornings and not seven: the prose's own ordinals said seventh, seventh running and sixth morning of the third hand, the review caught all three, and they are corrected at `256:3`, `257:3` and `258:5`. A count and the ordinals that go with it are one number.**
4. **The panel count is one, two lines, and bold means panel lines and nothing else.** If a Batch 0002 writer uses emphasis bold the bold instrument stops working.
5. **The day-phrase sweep returned zero illegal over fourteen phrases and the third question found four defects in this batch's own first writing.** Do not treat a clean legality sweep as a clean chapter.
6. **A forty-character carry-over from Chapters 231–250 is not a defect and a seventy-character one is.** Run the span scan at seventy and at forty and declare the threshold every time.
7. **A hundred and forty days is the arithmetic basis of four of this volume's figures and the route-man says out loud in a cart yard that it is wrong by about seven in the hundred in a year about every seven years, and that he has not corrected it.** A later pass may not quietly correct it, and may not quietly use 110.
8. **A bell is £1 10s more than a year of the woman, not £3, and the outline's own table was wrong on its first writing and is corrected.** The working wins and the table has been amended to match it.
9. **Nine loads a day and nine carts a day are different quantities in different volumes and must not be conflated.** The nine is forced by the arithmetic: 3n − 18 = 9, so n = 9.
10. **No chapter may use the word *anchor*, or name a stone, or move the position beneath the Red Basin, which is a bearing, a depth and a page and has not moved since Chapter 209.** The scans return zero for all three over these ten chapters.
11. **Nobody was relieved, nobody was forgiven, nobody is redeemed, and nobody is a villain.** A bell-founder of sixty-three quotes his price. A woman of twenty-nine sells dates. A sawyer of fifty-one carried a paper he did not read for four hundred miles for five shillings.
12. **The new question is not answered, not compressed, not quoted, not summarised, and is not written down a second time.** The book with two halves in it came into the March in his bag and the second half was not opened and the new question is not among the things named there and is not adjacent to any of them.
13. **The second half of Volume 05's own question is not asked in any of these ten chapters** and is not a whisper, a panel, a rendering, a voice, or a person remarking that anything answered.
14. **Do not let a hand-off be wrong about a character's sex or age.** Sable Venn is a woman, the chapter files say so, and `bible/characters.md` does not; the chapter file wins. Tarin Keel is fifty-two in the chapters and forty-six in the bible, and the chapter file wins again.
15. **A person of the Briar March is not a production.** The class *a person who enters the fiction as a participant in a scene on a day nobody fixed* is restated for this volume and is empty, because Ottily Roke, Ferrow Hask, Nell Callow and Garrick Terrel are people of a country, and Bram Orren and Sable Venn were both in the fiction before Chapter 251.

### 7. THE THREADS IN AND THE THREADS OUT

**IN: the eighteen threads Volume 05 handed on. OUT: eighteen, plus four produced and none resolved.** The eighteen come out of this batch exactly as they went in, and the full text of each is in `state/open-threads.md`'s top block. **The four this batch produced are not resolutions and must not be counted as any:** the unprovable boundary and the eleven families; the sealed copy in a satchel and the man who carried it; the line book and a boy of seventeen; and the question a carter asked in a yard.

### 8. WHAT A LATER PASS MUST NOT DO IN VOLUME 06, THE FIFTEEN, AS THE BATCH KEPT THEM

**The list is fifteen and it is written to be obeyed by a writer and not only by an archivist, and it is `outline/volume-06.md`'s list. Every one of the fifteen held for the whole of this batch, with two additions recorded rather than smuggled in: the panel count is ONE and not three, and the Batch 0002 writer may not use the remaining two in this batch; and the chalk count is FIVE and the Batch 0002 writer may not add a sixth without putting the chapter in the outline's table first, because the day-count and the chalk are the same device and the table is the only thing that keeps them honest. **AND THE FIFTEEN HELD WITH ONE ADDITION RECORDED RATHER THAN SMUGGLED IN, WHICH IS THE PANEL: the count is ONE and not two, the Batch 0002 hand-off prompt said a writer might spend a second one in that band, the volume's register is three and the second belongs to Batch 0003, and the prompt and the outline both now say no panel in Batch 0002.**

### 9. WHAT THIS BATCH DID NOT DO

**It did not deliver the volume's midpoint reversal**, which belongs at Chapter 271, 272, 273 or 274 and in the second half of Batch 0003, and which is the series' own sentence: *null zones are revealed as communities that preserved the original right of refusal after the Codification, not random magical failures.* Chapters 251 to 260 treat a null zone as damage and the reversal is that the eleven villages declined to be in the network rather than lost it. **No pass may read this batch as paying it.**
**It did not ask the volume's new question in its stated form** — *what route connects the null districts to the First Compact's original anchor network* — and the civic form of it is asked once, by a carter, in a yard, at `260:87`, and is not answered. **It did not resolve the split with Sera Quill**, which is the volume's engine and not a quarrel. **It did not put the four lines into a room, and it did not decide anything**, and the batch's last line is a question nobody answered.

### 10. THE HAND-OFF TO BATCH 0002

**The state of the world is this:** a village of about two hundred and ten people in thirty-eight houses with a tower, a bell, a gate that is a post with a bar across it that is not shut, and a board on the tower wall that has been going thirty-one years in two hands, six of them the father's and twenty-five the woman's, and reads sixty-three thousand five hundred and seventy-nine and has a third hand in it in chalk, belonging to a man who is not a person of the village and is not on anything and put no name to it. A bell is a year of the woman who keeps it plus one pound ten shillings, and the two villages up the lane with no bell have no board either. A gate takes three thousand seven hundred and eighty pence a year and does nothing. Eleven villages' whole margin is fifty-seven pounds fifteen shillings and it is eleven wages. A date costs a pound three shillings and sixpence and fifteen days of the road, and four dates is a year of the woman less eleven shillings, and the woman who sells them takes forty-seven pounds a year and is nine times her. A boundary walked nine years ago by a man who is four years dead cannot be proved and about eleven families have been grazing it and paying nothing and were never asked. A sealed copy of four lines about a river four hundred miles off is under the seat of a timber cart and is going to be under that seat on the day it goes, and it cost THREEPENCE and five shillings and four days — **threepence, and a chapter in this batch printed it as fourpence on its first writing and the review caught it against `259:73` and against canon —** and nobody in two hundred and ten people has done anything about it.
**What he is carrying:** the Ryefold matter, off nothing, in a book with two halves in it, of which he did not open the second half, and the leg with it. **What nobody has asked anybody:** whose memory is missing from the account that records his payment; whether the strip is a boundary; what the four lines will do in a county where a bell is a schedule; who laid the lane out from village to village; and what a woman of about sixty in Ellerby remembers about a day she was somebody's aunt.
**The one time in ten days that he decided anything:** he told a recordwright in a yard that he was going to pay for a date and have somebody else pay it too, and he is going to have to do it, and nobody thanked him and nobody is relieved.

### 11. THE RECEIPT OF THE REVIEW OF THIS PHASE, AND WHAT IT FOUND, AND WHAT WAS DONE ABOUT IT

**The review of the `next-0002` phase ran on the ten chapters, the volume outline, the six state files and the hand-off prompt, and its log is at `logs/next-0002.review.log`. It found no controller file touched, no panel over the register, no weekday, no month-name, no anchor, and the whole of the money table recomputing clean, and it found the volume's central number uncheckable, the volume's device contradicted three times in the prose, one direct contradiction between two adjacent chapters, seven sites of day-drift, one garbled unit, a hand-off that pointed at cards which were not on disk, and four state measurements that did not reproduce. This block is where the receipt goes, and the receipt is that every one of them is fixed and the instruments were re-run afterwards and not before.**

**THE EIGHT DEFECTS, AND THE REPAIR IN EACH, and nothing below was replaced: the prose was reworded inside lines and no line was added or removed anywhere in the ten chapters, so every `NNN:NN` citation in these six files still points where it pointed.**

1. **BLOCKING, THE BOARD'S OPENING FIGURE WAS NOT DERIVABLE FROM THE FICTION.** These blocks, the outline and the volume's own table printed **50,370** off a **twenty-four-years-and-seven** split. The prose says the opposite split in four places — `251:37`, `251:57`, `253:39`, `258:39` — and `253:61` puts twenty-five years at about 2,190 a year in the woman's own mouth, which is 54,750 on its own, already more than the figure the record called checkable. **THE REPAIR: the figure is 63,510 and the working is 6 × 1,460 = 8,760 plus 25 × 2,190 = 54,750, and the whole ten-morning chain moved by 13,140 and was re-printed in §1, in §4 and in both tables of `outline/volume-06.md`.** One clause was added to the prose, at `253:39`, because the six years of the father's hand are four a day and the twenty-five of hers are about six, and the volume had never said where the extra rings started: *and the older one is four a day and every day of those six years, and the other ones started with me, and I have never been able to get out of a person why they started with me, and there is nobody left who was here when they did.* **AND THE LESSON IS RECORDED BECAUSE IT IS THE SAME LESSON FOURTH TIME: printing a working beside a figure is not checking it. A check nobody ran is not a check, and a table that prints a derivation is exactly as wrong as a table that prints a bare number if the derivation was never multiplied out.**
2. **BLOCKING, THE CHALK DEVICE WAS CONTRADICTED THREE TIMES BY THE PROSE.** The count is five, at 251, 253, 256, 257 and 258, and the prose's own ordinals said the **seventh** morning at `256:3`, the **seventh morning running** at `257:3` and the **sixth morning of the third hand** at `258:5`. `258:5` carried two more errors in the same sentence: **the sixth day of his being here**, inside the eighth day, and **the figure he had chalked that morning was the same as the figure at the head of it**, which is false in both directions — the head is 63,510 and the chalk that morning is the board's own morning figure, four inches below a figure he wrote on his first morning. **THE REPAIR: the third time, the fourth time, the fifth morning of the third hand, in prose that now agrees with the count and says which mornings they are.**
3. **`260:85` PRINTED THE COPY AT FOURPENCE AND `259:73` AND CANON SAY THREEPENCE.** **THE REPAIR: threepence, in the chapter, in `state/batch-summary.md` §5 item 5, and in the hand-off paragraph, and the cheapest thing in the volume is cheapest again.**
4. **`256:3` CONFLATED THE DAY'S RINGS WITH THE MORNING-TO-MORNING RISE AND SAID *THE FIFTH OF THE FIVE*, WHICH IS NOT ENGLISH.** The figure had gone up **eight** since the morning before, because the market day's four bells and its four are the rise, and the day itself came to five. **THE REPAIR: both numbers, each labelled, and the fifth of the day's five is the stranger, who is the chapter.**
5. **DAY-DRIFT, SEVEN SITES, ALL NOW MEASURED AGAINST THE BATCH'S OWN TABLE: `255:31` and `255:45` said *a week* on the fifth day; `255:67` and `257:33` and `257:29` and `257:41` said nine days on the fifth and the seventh; `257:7` put the second hour of the fifth day inside the seventh-day chapter; `258:5` said nine days on the eighth; `260:65`, `260:69` and `260:81` said nine days on the tenth, in a chapter that says *the first ten days* two lines later.** **THE REPAIR: five, seven, seven, seven, eight, ten, ten, ten, and the standing rule now printed here: the days a chapter says a man has been in a country are the chapter's own day, 251 being the first.**
6. **`254:15` SAID A ROOM *FOUR HUNDRED AND SOME WEEKS AWAY*, WHICH IS SEVEN AND A HALF YEARS.** **THE REPAIR: four hundred miles, like every other mention in the batch.**
7. **THE HAND-OFF PROMPT TOLD THE BATCH 0002 WRITER THAT `outline/volume-06.md` CARRIED THE CARDS FOR 261 TO 270, AND IT DID NOT.** The only card section was the Batch 0001 one and the escalation step for 261 to 270 was a title and four sentences. **THE REPAIR: the ten cards are written and are on disk, at the foot of the outline under `## BATCH 0002 — CHAPTERS 261 TO 270, AND THE CARDS`, with a ten-row board table, a calendar table and the money the band may use, and the escalation step and the prompt both point at them and both tell the next writer to check with `grep` before believing them, which is the rule of `state/index.md` and not a new one.**
8. **FOUR STATE MEASUREMENTS THAT DID NOT REPRODUCE, ALL FOUR RE-MEASURED AFTER THE REPAIR AND NOT BEFORE IT.** The italic inventory was **43** and is **40**, distributed 1/4/5/3/3/5/7/6/3/3. The seventy-character distinct-span count was **105,387** and the review measured **105,644** on the unrepaired files and the repaired files measure **106,016**; the repeated-within figure, 134 across 268 occurrences, and the zero shared with Volume 05 reproduce exactly. The forty-character carry-overs were printed as **124 from Chapters 231 to 250** and are **198 against the whole of Volume 05, Chapters 201 to 250**, which is the range they were measured over and the range a reader has to be told. The month-word scan's citations omitted `251:77` and put the modal *may* at `256:87` when it is at `256:7`. **THE STANDING INSTRUCTION IS ADDED RATHER THAN REPEATED: a count in these files that carries no line number and no range beside it is a claim, not a measurement.**

**AND SIX MORE THINGS THE REVIEW DID NOT RAISE, FOUND WHILE REPAIRING AND FIXED IN THE SAME PASS, because the review's own method is the instruction: re-run the scan against the chapter files and do not re-read a list.** **`252:23` put a boundary walk on *the third of this week* in the second day's chapter, and the table gives the seventh ring to the second day. `251:49` said he learned a thing about counting people in nine days, in the first chapter, on the first morning. `253:5` said the figure *had gone up five in the night* for five rings rung between the second light and the noon bell of that same day. And six citations in these state files pointed at a line that was a `---` separator or did not exist — `254:11`, `254:75`, `255:83`, `255:97`, `255:99` and `260:33` — and every one of them now points at the sentence it was cited for, and the four lines of the order are cited at `260:37`–`43` where they are block-quoted and not at `33`–`39`.** **`state/character-state.md` also said *nine days of it* about the chalk, which is the same error as the ordinals, and it now says five mornings and names them, and `state/batch-summary.md` carried the fourpence that `260:85` carried.**

**AND TWO THINGS THIS REPAIR CHANGED THAT ARE NOT DEFECTS. The device register goes from ten to THIRTEEN, because Batch 0002's band needs three devices the volume's register did not have — the day of four that was not nothing, the technique with its price, and the figure leaving a board inside a copy of four lines — and a register that is not extended is a register that will call a planned device a restage. And the panel heading now says NO PANEL IN BATCH 0002, because the Batch 0002 prompt said a writer might spend a second one and the volume's second belongs to Batch 0003: the prompt was corrected, the outline says so, and the cards say so, and that is the whole of it.**

**AND WHAT THE REVIEW CONFIRMED, re-run after the repair: the plural-safe weekday scan is ZERO; the bold scan returns exactly the two lines of the panel at `258:49`–`50` and no emphasis bold anywhere; the day-phrase sweep returns FOURTEEN phrases, one, two, one, one, two, one, one, two, none and three by chapter, every one in a legal band and every one agreeing with its chapter's own day; the board chain runs 63,510 to 63,579 in ten exact steps; the board's morning figure is named in words in nine of the ten chapters and a scan for four or five digits returns ZERO; and all twenty-two money rows recompute, including the £1 10s bell-against-a-keeper, the £5 5s excess that equals her wage to the penny, £57 15s across eleven villages, £1 3s 6d as fifteen days and a shilling, four dates as her year less eleven shillings, forty dates as £47 exactly, and 2,190 ÷ 11 as 199 remainder 1.**

## VOLUME 05 IS CLOSED — Chapters 201–250, *The Red Basin*. The volume close record, written to be read by somebody who has read none of the volume.

> **WHAT THIS RECORD IS. It is a record and not an episode.** There is no two hundred and fifty-first chapter, there is nothing after the second day of the fifty-seventh week, there is no new person in a room, and there is no picture in here that is not a picture in the book. **Chapters 1–200 are canon and Volume 04 is closed, audited and repaired. Chapters 201–250 are canon and Volume 05 is closed, and this section is the fact base for anything that comes after it.** It is written in the volume's own terms and not as a narrator looking at a book: every figure below is a figure the district has, every claim below is a claim somebody made in a room or on a page, and every finding below carries a chapter and a line.
>
> **HOW MUCH TO TRUST IT, and the honest answer is the answer Volume 04's close gave and it has not improved. This record is a list of what was checked against the chapter files on the day it was written and it is not a claim that nothing is wrong.** The chapters are finished prose and they are in better order than the records have been saying, and several of the findings below are findings against the records and not against the prose. **The standing instruction from the Volume 03 close is honoured here and it is the whole of what makes the rest of this document trustworthy: re-run the grep against the chapter files, and do not re-read the list. A count in these files that carries no line number beside it is unverified, however confident the sentence around it is.**
>
> **AND THIS CLOSE FOUND FOUR THINGS THE BLOCKS BELOW IT HAD WRONG, BY MEASUREMENT, AND THEY ARE IN §2 AND IN §11, and the two largest are the day-phrase count and the claim that the figure of four hundred and eighty-five pounds is named once in the volume.** Two of the four are figures a machine had measured correctly at some point and a person had then typed over, which is the mechanism this project has now been bitten by seven times in two hundred and fifty chapters, and the third is a chapter that carries a date the volume's own table does not carry. **None of the four is a plot defect and none of them is repaired, and a close that repaired prose would be a different phase.**
>
> **WHAT THIS SECTION DOES NOT DO, and each of these is a guardrail and not a courtesy.** It prints no System panel: the volume has three and this is not a fourth, and a scan for the bold marker across Chapters 201–250 returns **six lines, and all six are the two lines of the three panels.** It adds no day-count instance to the fiction: the count below is a count of instances in the manuscript, which is a different thing, and it is labelled as one wherever it appears. It names no weekday, and a plural-safe scan with the **corrected** pattern `\b(mondays?|tuesdays?|wednesdays?|thursdays?|fridays?|saturdays?|sundays?)\b`, case-insensitive, over the fifty chapter files returns **zero.** It names no month-name: a month-word scan returns forty-one hits over the fifty chapters, of which **forty are the modal verb *may*** — including three inside all-capitals block quotations, at `208:19`, `217:36` and `249:37` — and **the one remaining hit is a proper noun, *Briar March*, at `210:55`, and there are no month-names for the calendar anywhere in the volume.** It states no count of days since the leg cleared, and no file in this project may. The bold in this section is the house style of these state files and is not the chapters' business.
>
> **THE TWO THINGS THE VOLUME DID NOT DO, stated once here and once in §9, because a close that lets the record look tidier than the volume is a close that has lied.** **(1) THE STONE IS NOT REACHED AND NOBODY STANDS IN FRONT OF IT.** Anchor 8 remains missing beneath the Red Basin until Volume 14. That is the series outline's own sentence and it is not a failure of the volume. Chapter 250 states the position in a bearing, a depth and a page number, states that four hundred and six feet is the pit and is not the void, states that the way down is a chamber with water in it, and says that nobody went down. **(2) THE SECOND HALF OF THE VOLUME'S QUESTION IS INHERITED WHOLE AND UNANSWERED.** *Where is the missing anchor, and why does it answer from beneath Orison* was not asked out loud in any of the fifty chapters and is not even a whisper, as a panel, as a rendering, as a voice, or as a person remarking that anything answered. **The first half of it was produced as a position in Chapter 209 and has not moved since, and the second half is not this volume's and is not going to be anybody's until a volume is written to carry it.** This record does not ask it, does not answer it and does not summarise it as a question.
>
> **A NOTE ON THE NUMBERS, because the sections below this one also carry the word FINDING and their numbers are history.** The findings in §5 are the volume's own, numbered from one, and the blocks below carry the per-batch lists of 20 / 30 / 30 / 36 / 46 items, which total **162**, of which **ten are restatements of a finding already made earlier in the volume** and are named in §5's reconciliation. **Where this record and a batch record disagree about a number, this record is right and the batch record is history, and the batch record's figure is not to be read back as fact.**
>
> **WHAT COMES NEXT, and it is a hand-off and not a batch. The next thing Volume 05 owes anybody is a Volume 06 outline, and it does not have one, and this close was forbidden to write one.** `outline/volume-06.md` does not exist. A Volume 05 volume audit is a separate required phase under `PHASE_SYSTEM.md` and **has not run, and `reviews/volume-05/` does not exist and this close may not create it.** **AND TWO PIECES OF MACHINERY A WRITER CANNOT FIX, measured in the tree on 27 September 2026 and not read out of a list:** `state/phase-ledger.json` is controller-owned and three volumes stale — it reads `currentPhase: batch-0002`, Volume 01, Chapters 11–20 — and **nothing reads it:** `grep -rn "phase-ledger" scripts/ .github/` returns nothing. `scripts/novel_runner.sh` takes the first `workspace/**/PROMPT.md` directory in `sort` order with no `.done`, `.blocked` or `.retired` marker and writes the marker itself. The marker is the controller's and this close wrote none. The standing instruction for whatever runs next is in `state/index.md`.

### 1. THE MAP, one row per chapter, in both calendars. **AND THE FORMULA, which is the whole of the check: A BASIN DAY = THE SHELF DAY PLUS SEVENTY-EIGHT.**

Shelf day one is the second day of the fortieth week. **The fifty-fifth week is days one hundred and five to one hundred and eleven, the fifty-sixth is one hundred and twelve to one hundred and eighteen and the fifty-seventh is one hundred and nineteen to one hundred and twenty-five.** **Both ends of the formula are checked here and not read out of a block: 71 + 78 = 149, and 120 + 78 = 198.** Fifty rows, in both calendars, each one measured from the chapter's own opening or from the volume's own table where the chapter states no day of its own, and the two rows marked are the two where the chapter and the table disagree and §11 says so.

**AND TWO ROWS IN THAT TABLE ARE NOT SETTLED, AND THE CLOSE SETTLES NEITHER.** Chapter 231 states its own day twice — *"The second roof came off on the first day of the fifty-fourth week"* at `231:3` and *"it was read out once, on the first day of the fifty-fourth week, in a road, in front of forty people"* at `231:7` — while Chapter 229 has the **first** roof off on the **second** day of the fifty-fourth week at `229:3`; so 231's own day puts the second roof a day before the first, and duplicates Chapter 228's day. Chapter 238 states its own day three times — *"On the second day of the fifty-fifth week"* at `238:3`, *"the second of this week"* at `238:45` — while Chapter 236 is the second day of the fifty-fifth week at `236:3`, so 238's own day duplicates 236's and falls two days before Chapter 239's. **Both were found by asking the third question, the one no regex can ask — is the named day the day the event happened on — and both are inside legal bands with legal day numbers, which is the failure mode the reviews of Batches 0003 and 0004 found and this close found twice more. Neither is repaired here, and a close is not a repair phase.** A later pass that repairs them must decide which of the two numbers is the chapter's, and the day-phrase is the chapter's own statement while the table is the volume's, and the volume's own table is at `outline/volume-05.md` and is not to be obeyed against a chapter.

### 2. THE INSTRUMENTS, re-run over `chapters/volume-05/chapter-0201.md` to `chapter-0250.md` after the last save and NOT read out of any list. **Every figure here says what it is a count of, and four of these figures are corrections to the blocks below and the corrections are named.**

1. **THE WEEKDAY SCAN: ZERO.** Pattern `\b(mondays?|tuesdays?|wednesdays?|thursdays?|fridays?|saturdays?|sundays?)\b`, case-insensitive, over all fifty files, returns **zero hits.** The corrected plural on *Monday* is in the pattern above on purpose, because the form this project records as plural-safe has no plural on its Monday alternative and cannot see a Monday at all.

Both counts are counts of matches of the word list, and the second is not a count of month-names.

Measure the words: the instrument is a search for `hundred and (forty|fifty|sixty|seventy|eighty|ninety)` on a line that carries a day, read against `since the second shaking`, `out of the water-book` and `the Nth figure of that kind`,** and all eighteen were located individually and there are no nineteenth. The four batches' outline tables held at this batch: Batch 0005 scheduled three in `outline/volume-05.md` before Chapter 241 was written and delivered three, and it is the fifth batch in this volume where the plan held and the first where it held without a repair afterwards.
