The lesson is the one this file has printed before and it is worth the two corrections: **a band figure that is exactly double the measured figure is a doubled count and a band figure that is short is an undercount, and neither is visible without the run.** And the third question is still the one that matters, because it is the only one that caught anything: **the legality sweep returned zero illegal across ninety-nine phrases and the side-by-side reading of fifty chapter days against ninety-nine phrases found the two conflicts in §1.**
6. **THE BOLD INVENTORY: six lines, zero of them emphasis. THE ITALIC INVENTORY: forty-four single-asterisk spans, and the split matters.** **Nineteen are his own hand** — `201:78`, the fourteen entries of the penny exercise book at `210:21`–`41`, `214:43`, `235:57` and `235:83`, and `236:27` — **and twenty-five are textual citation**, a phrase quoted out of an order, a bond, a settlement, a sheet on a wall or a person's mouth as written: `206:41`, `209:37` (twice), `211:43` (twice), `212:34`, `212:70`, `213:51`, `216:67` (twice), `226:33` (twice), `226:59`, `227:60`, `228:28` (twice), `228:30`, `228:36` (twice), `233:39`, `233:57`, `237:57`, `240:11`, `242:55`, `243:67`. **A count printed without that split is a count of a thing whose unit is not stated.**
7. **THE DUPLICATE SCAN, AND THE UNIT IS DECLARED BECAUSE THIS PROJECT HAS JUST BEEN BITTEN BY DECLARING THE WRONG ONE.** **A scan for duplicated LINES returns zero over this volume and always will, because a line in these files is a whole paragraph and this volume puts eighty-word sentences inside one line. The instrument that works is a SPAN scan, and it is run here in this exact form: normalise the whitespace of each chapter file, take every window of exactly *k* characters at every character position, deduplicate the windows within each chapter before counting, and then count the windows that occur in more than one chapter.** At **seventy characters**: over Chapters 201–250, **4,147 distinct repeated spans and 4,901 repeat occurrences**; over Batch 0005 alone, **519 distinct and 558 repeat occurrences**, and **1,067 of Batch 0005's spans are shared with Chapters 231–240** — the immediately preceding batch, which is the correct comparison and not Chapters 151–200. At **forty characters**: over the fifty chapters, **12,073 distinct and 15,856 repeat occurrences**; over Batch 0005, **1,698 distinct and 2,047 occurrences**, with **3,003 shared with 231–240**. **RUN THE SPAN INSTRUMENT, EXPECT THIS VOLUME'S DELIBERATE FORMULAE TO DOMINATE IT, AND READ WHAT IS LEFT, because that is the only useful part.** And what is left, at seventy characters, after the three formulae, is **nothing that is not a formula**: the settlement's standing figure *a hundred and seventy-eight pounds, thirteen shillings and fourpence a year* in **eleven** chapters (`223`, `228`, `229`, `235`, `236`, `238`, `239`, `240`, `242`, `248`, `249`); the tonnage chain *one thousand seven hundred and twenty-eight* in `217`, `223`, `229`, `238`, `239`, `242` and `249`; and the panel's standing sentence *in the flat white nothing that has put things in front of him in about a year and a half* at `207:67`, `217:35`, `227:53` and `240:25`, which is the same sentence four times in three panels and once in prose and is the volume's own method. **AND THE LAST SENTENCE OF THIS ITEM AS IT WAS FIRST WRITTEN — *there is no fourth cluster, and the one carry-over a cross-batch scan exists to catch, a line lifted from a chapter ten back, did not happen in this volume* — WAS FALSE BY MEASUREMENT, AND IT IS CORRECTED HERE BY A SECOND INSTRUMENT RUN AGAINST THE FIFTY FILES ON 27 SEPTEMBER 2026, AND NOT ONE OF THE FIFTY CHAPTERS IS REPAIRED, BECAUSE THE FIFTY CHAPTERS ARE RIGHT AND THE SENTENCE WAS NOT.** The first run counted duplicated SPANS at a fixed character threshold and read what was left. The second run is a different instrument and it pairs the fifty files, matches them word for word, and keeps the maximal shared runs of **twenty words or more**: **NINETY-ONE such runs, across SEVENTY-THREE distinct pairs of chapters, TWO THOUSAND FOUR HUNDRED AND EIGHT WORDS of them in all.** **The longest is SEVENTY WORDS and it stands at `208:7` and `233:7` — *I have no authority to open this. I am a clerk of a Seat. This is addressed to a chamber nine days away, and the chamber does not know there is a Seat* — which is twenty-five chapters apart and is therefore precisely the carry-over this instrument exists to catch. It is the volume's own device and not a defect: `233:9` names it, counts it and says *it has now been said four times*, and both chapters close the beat on the same short line — *nobody repeated it back to him afterwards* — at `208:9` and `233:11`.** Next, and every one of them a formula: the penny exercise book with two halves in it under a board with a stone on it, fifty-three words, at `220:59`, `221:67` and `230:33`; the second notice of four sheets, fifty words, at `210:27` and `220:37`; the chair put back at a table in a room with eleven seats, forty-nine words, at `240:41`, `241:67` and `250:61`; and the tonnage chain, forty-seven words, at `223:41`, `229:15` and `239:23`. **AND THE THREE THE CLOSE NAMED ARE STILL THREE OF THEM AND THEY ARE NOT THE ONLY THREE: *Ilyan Vester took out a penny exercise book and a stub of lead* stands at `201:76`, `204:93` and `211:87`; *nobody in that room said anything for about as long as it takes to* stands at `203:42`, `208:23` and `226:19`, which is a shape the volume uses on purpose; and *the four men and the barrow went down to the dam at the second light* stands at `205:88` and `220:51`.** So the honest form of this item is: **the instrument returns a volume dominated by deliberate formulae, the close named three of them, there are at least ninety-one maximal shared runs of twenty words or more, and not one of them is a prose defect** — every one is a figure, a debt, a document's own text, a named device, or the sentence the volume ends on. **A later pass that runs this instrument should expect the formulae, should read what is left, and what is left is nothing.**
8. **THE COUNT OF THE WORDS *NINE* AND *ELEVEN*, with the residue explained and not hidden.** ***Nine*: 466 whole-word occurrences over the fifty chapters, of which 443 are standalone and 23 stand inside a compound (nineteen, ninety, forty-nine, thirty-nine, twenty-nine, sixty-nine, fifty-nine).** ***Eleven*: 448 whole-word occurrences and every one of them is standalone, because *eleven* is not a component of any larger spelled number in this volume.** Both counts are counts of whole-word occurrences in prose, and the residue is the same in both: **443 and 448 quantities, and every one of them is a real quantity in the fiction and not a filler** — nine feet by fourteen, nine carts a day, nine shillings a week, nine days, nine people, nine hundred and forty forms, and the eleven houses, the eleven seats, the eleven feet, the eleven marks, the eleven hands and the eleven years. **The residue is not hidden and it is not a defect: a volume whose engine is a number will spend that number, and the fix is not to abbreviate the figure, which is what a writer reaches for, and the working is still printed in full and in order everywhere it appears.**
9. **THE COUNT OF PEOPLE WHO ARRIVED ON A DAY NOBODY FIXED IS SIX, and it is a count of persons and not of appearances.** Ewan Troke (`201:19`), Fen Tregerran (`202:5`), Alo Vearn (`209:5`), the man who comes through the north twice a year (`210:53`), Coll Ferren (`221:41`) and Sable Venn (`246:3`). The class is defined — *a self-arrived person is somebody who enters the fiction as a participant in a scene on a day nobody fixed* — and the sixth is the volume's climax and not a production.
10. **THE THREE SCANS THAT CAME BACK CLEAN AND ARE RECORDED AS HAVING BEEN RUN, because a sweep that reports zero is a fact about a range and not about the book and the range is stated every time: uppercase CORRECT, zero; *Remedy Drafter*, zero; a count of the people who know he cannot read, not printed in this record and not to be printed in any.** Refer to `154:53` and `164:49` and to nothing else.

### 3. THE THREE CLOCKS, unchanged, obeyed in every chapter, and what the volume did to them

**The Shelf's grain year is fifty-two weeks, a week is seven Shelf days, and Shelf day one is the second day of the fortieth week.** **The pan-book year of Hollow Sallow is fifty saltings, more or less, and a salting is a drying and has no length: seven weeks in a dry year and eleven in a wet one** (`202:43`, `212:21`). **The turn is twelve years from the first shaking, turn nought being the first shaking, and this is turn twelve** (`208:47`, `207:37`). **The day is a day out of the water-book since the second shaking, and it is the only date in the Red Basin that anybody keeps, and it has four hands in it and a fifth since the girl of seventeen was paid a penny a day for it** (`202:31`, `224:21`, `235:81`).

**AND THE VOLUME'S ENGINE WAS AND STILL IS ARITHMETIC AND NOT POLITICS: NONE OF THE THREE CLOCKS CONVERTS INTO ANY OTHER, AND THE VOLUME PROVED IT TWICE AND USED IT TWICE.** Once in a salt-pan's mouth at `202:45`, where a pan-book year is a year and nine is a year, so the sluice's standing order of the eleventh salting of the forty-ninth year and the bond's eleventh salting of the fortieth are the same salting nine years along, which is a *legal* order that arrives on the wrong day every time (`212:21`, `212:28`). And once in the volume's resolution, where the eleven feet had to be written twice, once above the Anner's own sill and once above the frame's, because the chamber's own second ruling of nine years ago says that **no order may name a height without naming the sill it is taken from** (`208:19`, `249:19`).

**WHAT THE VOLUME DID TO THE THREE CLOCKS, and the answer is very little: it gave the day a fifth hand, a penny a day, and a hundred and twelve days a year** (`224:21`, `228:28`). It did not turn the turn, it did not name a month, it did not put a weekday anywhere, and it did not convert one clock into another anywhere except in the two places where it had to and both are in the volume's own words.

### 4. THE MONEY, every figure recomputed in a script and the working printed beside it. **THE INSTRUMENT IS THE SAME ONE EVERY TIME: print the unit price in the sentence, find it, and multiply. A script checks the figures it is given and does not read the sentence they sit in, and this volume has now been bitten by that in three reviews running, so every row below was checked twice: once arithmetically and once by reading the sentence.**

| Figure | The working, and it is the whole of it |
|---|---|
| **A matter carried to the chamber at Orison and brought back = £5 6s, paid by the Anner out of the Anner's own cost of the high road, with the amount, the payer and the thing wanted written on the outside of the sheet in a clerk's hand** | 240d + 960d + 72d = **1,272d = £5 6s** ✓ (`205:7`, `234:39`, `242:7`, `242:53`) |
| **And what that price is against the two wages in this valley** | a pitman's week is 120d, and 1,272 ÷ 120 = **ten weeks and 72d over**; a sluice-keeper's week is 108d, and 1,272 ÷ 108 = **eleven weeks and 84d over, which is about three quarters of the sixteen weeks she is paid for** ✓ (`242:15`) |
| **And the price against the settlement** | 1,272 × 33 = 41,976d, and 42,880 − 41,976 = **904d over**, so the price goes into the settlement thirty-three whole times and not thirty-four ✓ (`242:35`) |
| **The settlement of this valley, recurring = £178 13s 4d a year** | 19 roofs at 2,160d = 41,040d; the whole wage 16 × 108d = 1,728d; the second hand 112d; 41,040 + 1,728 + 112 = **42,880d**, and 42,880 − 42,720 = 160d = 13s 4d ✓ (`229:15`, `238:41`, `239:23`) |
| **The whole wage = £7 4s a year; the second hand = 9s 4d; the gate = £5 18s; the three years owed and paid in an envelope = £10 16s; and the first year of the paper = £195 7s 4s** | 16 × 108d = 1,728d ✓; 112d ✓; 1,080d + 336d = **1,416d** ✓; 3 × 864d = **2,592d = £10 16s** ✓; 42,880 + 1,416 + 2,592 = **46,888d = £195 7s 4s** ✓ (`204:91`, `223:41`, `229:15`, `229:19`, `229:23`) |
| **The pit's half of the sluice-keeper's wage = £3 12s a year** | 16 × 54d = **864d**, and 864 − 720 = 144d = 12s ✓ (`246:43`) |
| **The chamber's tonnage = 1,040 tons; this basin's = about 1,100; the difference = 60 tons = £420 a year at £7 a ton, which is five and seven-tenths in the hundred of the trade** | 100,800 × 100 = 10,080,000 ÷ 1,747,200 = **5.77** ✓ (`223:41`, `243:37`). **And against the settlement, 100,800 ÷ 42,880 = 2.35, two and a third times** (`242:45`) |
| **The sixty tons as a hundredweight count, and it is a hundredweight count and NOT a day-count** | 60 × 20 = 1,200 cwt; £1 4s = 240 + 48 = **288d**; 1,200 × 288d = **345,600d = £1,440** ✓ (`243:33`) |
| **The tonnage chain, printed in a man's mouth with the working: at the mouth £4 a ton = £4,160; at the market £11 a ton = £11,440; the carrying between them is £7 a ton = £7,280** | 1,040 × 240d = 249,600d; × 4 = **998,400d = £4,160** ✓; × 11 = **2,745,600d = £11,440** ✓; × 7 = **1,747,200d = £7,280** ✓ (`239:19`) |
| **AND THE FINDING: the settlement of this valley and the cost of a second road come to £4,338 13s 4d a year, and the excess of that over the whole of the trade at the mouth is exactly the settlement, to the penny, with nothing behind it** | 998,400 + 42,880 = **1,041,280d**; 1,041,280 ÷ 240 = 4,338 with **160d over**, and 160d = 13s 4d ✓; and 4,338 13s 4d − 4,160 = **£178 13s 4d**, the settlement exactly ✓ (`239:33`, `239:41`) |
| **And the ceiling a second road would have to come in under to leave the settlement standing** | 998,400 − 42,880 = **955,520d = £3,981 6s 8s** ✓ (`239:51`) |
| **The cost of the Anner being bound in advance to a figure nobody has = 6s 8d a year, which is one and a third in the hundred of a pitman's paid year** | 120 ÷ 6 = 20d; 20 × 4 = **80d = 6s 8d**; 80 × 100 ÷ 6,000 = **1.33** ✓ (`248:51`) |
| **The third document, one sheet in three copies = 5d, and the Seat pays it out of the press** | 1d + 1d + 3d = **5d** ✓ (`249:21`) |
| **The charter, and its price, and the subtraction the resolution rests on: a roof wet through is £9 and not broken into parts; nineteen of them is £171 exactly; and the charter is worth the roofs and is not worth the wage, because a paper that can be ended cannot hold a wage** | 2,160d × 19 = **41,040d = £171 with no remainder** ✓; 42,880 − 41,040 = **1,840d = £7 13s 4d** ✓; and 1,840d = 1,728d + 112d, the whole wage plus the penny a day ✓ (`249:57`, `249:69`) |
| **AND THE SAME FIGURE IS ALSO WHAT THE FLOOD COST, to the penny, and one of the two things that figure is put water in nineteen houses** | `223:41`, `229:23`, `234:25` |
| **THE TWO SILLS, IN INCHES, and the difference of four feet in the third document. THE RULED GEOMETRY OF VOLUME 05, which the review of Batch 0005 settled on 27 September 2026 and which no pass may re-derive** | the Anner's sill is the dam's own sill and every number in this basin goes back to it; the frame's sill at Hollow Sallow is **NINE FEET = 108 inches ABOVE the Anner's**; the bed below the village is **THIRTEEN FEET = 156 inches below the frame's sill**, and is therefore **FOUR FEET = 48 inches below the Anner's**; the bond's line is **ELEVEN FEET = 132 inches above the Anner's**, and is therefore **TWO FEET above the frame's sill**, which is about the height of the floors of the houses on the upper half of that lane; the water's high mark in the return is **TWO FEET FOUR INCHES = 28 inches above the frame's sill**, which is **ELEVEN FEET FOUR INCHES = 136 inches above the Anner's, four inches above the bond's line**; **and therefore the difference between two lines that are both *eleven feet* is exactly the distance between the two sills the third document names, which is FOUR FEET, which is FORTY-EIGHT INCHES** ✓ (`206:45`, `216:45`, `247:29`, `247:31`, `249:19`) |
| **DEBTS AND NOT PRICES. £485 a year on £9,700 at the close of that grain year; a notice of 5s 4d unpaid; a claim of £45 2s 8d not producible; ninety-one numbered sheets; a box of about nine hundred and forty forms; the minute of the first of the forty-eighth week; a dead man in the fourth line of a list of two hundred and forty** | 9,700 × 12d = **116,400d = £485** ✓ (`250:47`); 4 × 16d = **64d = 5s 4d** ✓; 406 × 32d − 2,160d = **10,832d = £45 2s 8d** ✓ (`250:49`) |
| **AND THE FIGURE OF FOUR HUNDRED AND EIGHTY-FIVE POUNDS IS PRINTED IN FIVE CHAPTERS AND NOT IN ONE, AND §11 SAYS SO** | `206:101`, `220:45`, `230:35`, `239:63`, `250:47` |

### 5. EVERY FINDING THE VOLUME MADE, ONCE EACH, WITH THE CHAPTER AND THE LINE

**AND THE COUNT IS A COUNT OF ROWS IN THE LIST BELOW AND IT WAS MEASURED BY COUNTING THE ROWS, NOT BY ARITHMETIC, and the arithmetic then reconciles and the two agreeing is the only reason to believe either. The five batch records carry 20 + 30 + 30 + 36 + 46 = 162 numbered items between them. This list is ONE HUNDRED AND FORTY-EIGHT ROWS. The difference of fourteen is made up of EIGHT items that are a finding already made earlier in the volume and are named on the row where it was first made — `B2:29`, `B3:30`, `B4:33`, `B4:34`, `B4:36`, `B5:18`, `B5:41` and `B5:45` — and SIX items that share a row with a finding of their own, `B3:27` with `B3:28` on row 76 and `B5:38`, `B5:39`, `B5:40`, `B5:42`, `B5:44` and `B5:46` on row 148. 162 − 8 − 6 = 148, and the row count and the arithmetic agree.**

**THE EIGHT NAMED-ON-ANOTHER-ROW ITEMS ARE RESTATEMENTS, AND THE SIX SHARED ROWS ARE DELIBERATE, and both are named here so that nothing is silently dropped and so that a later pass does not go looking for a finding that is already on the page under its first site:** the System put nothing up and said nothing — `B2:29` and `B4:33` folded into `209:83`; nobody went down — `B4:34` and `B5:45` folded into `209:75`; a record perfectly kept and wrong about a place — `B5:18` folded into `208:21`; the volume ends on ordinary things and none of them is a ruling — `B3:30` and `B4:36` folded into `220:63`; the Anner's standing interest settled in public at a cost — `B5:41` folded into `236:33`; and `B3:28` folded into `230:19` on row 76 with `B3:27`, while row 148 carries the tenth device, the figure the charter cannot hold, the spent good name, the eighteenth figure, the position and the four ordinary things, because the volume's last civic finding and its last chapter are one thing and separating them would have made a row that is a list.

**This is the volume's findings and it is not a re-narration and it resolves nothing.**

**BATCH 0001, CHAPTERS 201–210 — findings 1 to 20.**

1. **THE ROAD IS NOT WHERE IT SET OUT**, and a man of fifty-two who has been in a country for nineteen years measures it twice, throws the first set away, and refuses to say which of three explanations it is — `201:9`, `201:13`.
2. **ONE WORD, *COMMON*, DEFINED IN THE MARGIN OF EACH DEED AND NOT IN THE BODY OF EITHER**, and the two definitions are incompatible, and both men were telling the truth — `201:39`, `201:46`, `201:50`.
3. **THE THING THAT ENDS CHAPTER 201 IS A FINDING ABOUT A ROOM AND NOT A RULING: there is nobody in this basin who can be in the room when four true pages are read** — `201:98`, `201:102`.
4. **THE CLOCKS DO NOT CONVERT**, said once by a man scraping salt, without knowing he had said anything — `202:43`, `202:45`.
5. **A DEED IN THIS BASIN IS NOT A PAPER; IT IS AN AGREEMENT BETWEEN TWO PAPERS**, because the two halves were written about eight years apart in two towns four miles apart and neither man saw the other — `203:30`, `203:32`, `203:70`.
6. **THE PERSON WHO HOLDS THE BURDEN IN THIS BASIN IS DOING THE MOST USEFUL THING ANYBODY DOES AND IS THE WORST PAID**, and has been opening the sluice eleven days early for eleven years — `204:73`, `204:77`, `204:91`.
7. **THE QUESTION NOBODY HAD ASKED HER WAS ASKED IN NINE WORDS, AND IT WAS NOT THE MAN WHO CAME NINE DAYS FOR A DIFFERENT THING** — `204:81`, `204:83`.
8. **A BOND KEEPS A THING TRUE BY COSTING SOMEBODY MONEY, AND THE MONEY STOPPED IN THE FOURTH YEAR AND THE THING DID NOT STOP, AND NOBODY NOTICED FOR THREE YEARS** — `205:11`, `205:32`.
9. **THE ELEVEN HOUSES ARE NOT A CATEGORY**, and the reason nobody has asked them is that asking eleven people costs three days and three days is not a figure — `206:57`.
10. **A HEIGHT IS A HEIGHT ABOVE SOMETHING**, and there are three sills inside four miles, and the same figure means two different things two rooms apart — `206:33`.
11. **THE QUICK RULING IS FOUR LINES AND ALL FOUR ARE COMPLETE AND THERE IS NO HOLE IN ANY OF THEM, AND HE SAYS SO OUT LOUD BEFORE ANYBODY ELSE HAS WORKED IT OUT, AND NOBODY NOTICES** — `207:45`, `207:49`, `207:57`.
12. **THE PARTY THE ORDER NAMES IS THE ONLY BODY IN THE BASIN WITH ENOUGH MEN TO HOLD A DAM, AND THE ORDER DOES NOT NAME THE PERSON WHO HOLDS THE HEIGHT ON THE NIGHT THE WATER COMES, BECAUSE THERE IS NO SUCH PERSON** — `207:39`, `207:82`.
13. **A RECORD CAN BE PERFECTLY KEPT AND STILL BE WRONG ABOUT A PLACE**, and the reason is on the head of the abstract, where the referral is signed by a man who is not of this basin — `208:21`, `208:49`. **Restated at `245:64` and `248:37`, the second time by a man of fifty-two about four people standing in a room, which is the better sentence and is in the worse voice.**
14. **THE CHAMBER'S SECOND RULING SAYS THAT A HEIGHT MARKED ON A DOORPOST IS NOT A MEASUREMENT AND THAT NO ORDER MAY NAME A HEIGHT WITHOUT NAMING THE SILL; IT IS NINE YEARS OLD, IT WAS NEVER IN THIS BASIN, AND THE FOUR PAGES BREAK IT** — `208:19`, `208:45`, `208:47`.
15. **A LETTER FROM A CLERK OF THIRTY-FOUR ABOUT A FOURTH HAND WAS IN THE BUNDLE AND NOBODY OPENED IT, AND THE CHAPTER SAYS SO OUT LOUD** — `208:63`, `208:65`, `208:69`, `208:73`, `208:75`.
16. **THE FIRST MISSING ANCHOR POSITION IS IDENTIFIED BY TWO ORDINARY RECORDS THAT DO NOT MEET**: a trench taken out in a straight line and not in a pit, six cuts in a floor, and a drain aimed along that line — `209:33`, `209:35`, `209:53`, `209:63`. **It has not moved since, and this record does not move it.**
17. **NOBODY GOES DOWN**, and the reason he gives is not the water and not the steps, and he tells nobody what the reason is — `209:75`, `209:79`. **Restated at `240:29` and `250:33`.**
18. **THE SYSTEM PUT NOTHING UP AND SAID NOTHING WHATEVER ABOUT IT, AND THE SECOND HALF OF THE VOLUME'S QUESTION WAS NOT ASKED OUT LOUD IN THAT CHAPTER AND IS NOT EVEN A WHISPER** — `209:83`. **Restated at `220:61`, `230:29`, `240:25`.**
19. **THE NEW QUESTION IS WRITTEN DOWN ONCE, IN HIS OWN HAND, WITH A PLACE AND A FORM OF WORDS, AND HE DOES NOT ASK, AND THE SENTENCE BREAKS OFF IN THE MIDDLE** — `210:39`, `210:41`, `210:43`. **IT IS NOT ASKED, NOT ANSWERED, NOT COMPRESSED INTO ONE WORD AND NOT SUMMARISED BY ANYBODY INCLUDING HIM, AND THIS RECORD DOES NOT SUMMARISE IT.**
20. **A WOMAN WHO MAPS A COUNTRY WITHOUT ASKING ANYBODY IS NAMED ONCE, IN A MOUTH, BY A MAN WHO HAS NOT MET HER, AND ONE PERSON IN THE ROOM REPEATS THE NAME WRONG** — `210:55`, `210:59`.

**BATCH 0002, CHAPTERS 211–220 — findings 21 to 49.**

21. **A GIRL OF SEVENTEEN, WHO HAS BEEN IN THAT LANE SINCE SHE WAS BORN, READS A FOUR-LINE ORDER OUT TO A LANE AND ASKS THE FIRST QUESTION ANYBODY IN THE RED BASIN HAS EVER ASKED ABOUT A DOCUMENT** — `211:29`.
22. **THE ORDER BREACHES THE CHAMBER'S OWN SECOND RULING ON ITS FACE**, and the under-manager says it is not void and he is right, because the ruling is about a mark on a doorpost and not about a height named in an order — `211:49`, `211:55`, `211:61`.
23. **A SECOND MATTER GOES TO ORISON IN A BAG WITH A KNOT CUT WITH A KNIFE, DATED ON THE OUTSIDE WITH A DAY OUT OF THE WATER-BOOK**, because the Shelf's grain year does not exist in this basin — `211:69`, `211:73`, `211:75`.
24. **THE SLOW HAS BEEN DOWN SIX DAYS AND THE ORDER HAS BEEN OBEEN EXACTLY**, and what it did on the ground in six days is a shut causeway, nine carts a day on a high road, and eleven feet held — `211:17`, `211:103`.
25. **THE STANDING ORDER OF THE SLUICE NAMES A WEATHER AND NOT A DAY**, and a salting is a drying, seven weeks in a dry year and eleven in a wet one — `212:21`, `212:34`.
26. **THE STANDING ORDER IS THE ELEVENTH SALTING OF THE FORTY-NINTH YEAR AND THE BOND IS THE ELEVENTH SALTING OF THE FORTIETH**, which is the same salting nine years along, and a pan-book year is a year and nine is a year — `212:28`.
27. **THE TURN, AND IT IS IN A BOOK AND NOT IN ANYBODY'S MOUTH: the sluice was shut and the water was in the hollow and nobody came, written by the woman who keeps the sluice three weeks and a day before it happened, and nobody has ever asked her how she knows** — `212:68`, `212:70`.
28. **A HEIGHT PUT ON TWO DOORPOSTS BY THE SAME WATER IS A FOOT ABOVE ONE OLD MARK AND A FOOT BELOW THE OTHER**, so the two old marks are two feet apart — `213:25`, `213:31`.
29. **THE LANE ASKS WHAT THE NUMBER IS A NUMBER OF, OUT LOUD, ON A BANK, AND IT IS THE FIRST QUESTION ANYBODY IN THAT VILLAGE HAS EVER ASKED ABOUT A DOCUMENT** — `213:49`, `213:53`, `213:55`.
30. **ASKING ELEVEN PEOPLE COSTS THREE DAYS AND THREE DAYS IS NOT A FIGURE**, and there is no form in this basin for asking a person a question that matters — `214:19`, `214:21`.
31. **A CLERK OF A SEAT READS A FOUR-LINE ORDER OUT IN A LANE HE HAS NO AUTHORITY TO READ IT IN, ON ELEVEN CHAIRS CARRIED DOWN A HILL BY NINE PEOPLE, AND NOBODY SIGNS ANYTHING** — `214:55`, `214:59`.
32. **A THING THAT IS VOID AND UNREAD IS NOT A PROTECTION, IT IS AN ABSENCE**, in the under-manager's own mouth, in ochre dust — `215:27`.
33. **THE HEIGHT IS HELD EXACTLY AND ON PURPOSE** — eleven feet and an inch at the low end and ten feet and eleven inches at the high end, and it took a man with a barrow and a string and most of a morning, because the paper says so — `215:11`.
34. **NINE WORDS, IN HIS OWN MOUTH, IN OCHRE DUST: I HAVE BEEN GIVEN A NUMBER AND NEVER A REASON. And a figure is not a decision** — `215:43`, `215:49`, `215:51`.
35. **THE ANNER IS THE ONLY BODY IN THIS BASIN WITH MEN ENOUGH TO HOLD A DAM, AND A MAN WITH MEN ENOUGH TO HOLD A DAM IS NOT LOOKING AT ELEVEN HOUSES, AND THAT IS THE ARITHMETIC AND NOT A FAULT** — `215:55`.
36. **THE MIDPOINT, IN THREE CHAPTERS. ONE: the height is held exactly, as instructed, lawfully, by the only body with enough men** — `216:3`, `216:7`, `216:17`, `216:31`.
37. **THE ARITHMETIC OF IT IS ONE SUBTRACTION AND IT IS PRINTED IN THE CHAPTER WHERE A READER CAN DO IT IN THEIR HEAD: the frame's sill is nine feet above the Anner's sill, so eleven feet above the Anner's sill is two feet above the frame's sill** — `216:45`, `216:49`, `218:49`.
38. **NOBODY DROWNED, AND THAT IS NOT A MITIGATION AND IS NOT WRITTEN AS ONE** — `216:55`, `216:57`.
39. **THE MIDPOINT, PART TWO: NINETEEN HOUSES AND NOT ELEVEN, and the eleven were right about the eleven, and the eight are a barn that became a house and seven others, and the man who turned the barn into a house cut no mark because nobody asked him for one** — `217:5`, `217:9`.
40. **NOBODY IN THIS VALLEY IS A VILLAIN, AND IT IS SAID PLAINLY: the bond's promise is its first two sentences and the promise was kept exactly by the party it names** — `217:13`.
41. **THE FINDING, IN HER OWN MOUTH, NINE WORDS: IT NAMED A DAM, IT NAMED A HEIGHT, IT NAMED A DAY, IT NEVER NAMED THE WATER** — `217:48`.
42. **THE MIDPOINT, PART THREE: HE SAYS THE CATEGORY IN HIS OWN MOUTH AND IT IS NOT THE SENTENCE HE SAID ONCE AND HE DOES NOT SAY IT AGAIN AND HE DOES NOT EXPLAIN IT AND NOBODY ASKS HIM TO: a rule that names no bystander is carried by whoever is nearest with hands on it** — `218:39`, `218:43`.
43. **THE FOUR PAGES STAY ON THE WALL, AND THE PERSON WHO SAYS WHY IS A WOMAN OF ABOUT FORTY WHO WASHES THE PEN**, and her reason is that a basin with no document on a wall has nothing for the next person to argue with — `218:51`, `218:57`.
44. **HIS NAME IS NOT ON THE FOUR PAGES AND NEVER WAS, AND IT IS NOT ON ANYTHING IN THIS VALLEY, AND THE SPACE AT THE FOOT IS NEITHER FILLED NOR DASHED** — `218:63`.
45. **THE MONEY IS NOT THE DIFFICULTY, PROVED BY ASKING THE WRONG QUESTION ON PURPOSE: not one of nineteen answers about nineteen houses is money** — `219:39`, `219:45`, `219:47`.
46. **THE LIST OF NINETEEN IS A DOCUMENT AND IT IS THE FIRST DOCUMENT ANYBODY IN THIS BASIN HAS EVER WRITTEN ABOUT ITSELF**, and it is the back of a leaf of a hundred-year-old water-book, and it has a mark under every answer — `219:5`, `219:37`, `219:61`, `219:67`.
47. **THE SECOND MATTER CAME BACK IN NINE DAYS AS A LETTER OF TWO PARAGRAPHS FROM A CLERK OF THE CHAMBER WHO DID HIS JOB PROPERLY, SAYING THE BENCH IS SEIZED OF THE MATTER AND WILL CONSIDER IT IN ITS OWN TIME AND GIVING NO TIME** — `220:21`, `220:23`, `220:29`.
48. **HE READS THE SHELF SIDE OF HIS OWN BOOK OUT LOUD ON A BANK TO NINE PEOPLE AND STOPS AT THE TENTH ITEM AND SAYS HE IS NOT GOING TO SAY IT, AND NOBODY ASKS HIM WHICH YEAR IT WAS, AND HE IS NOT RELIEVED OF THE NOT BEING ASKED** — `220:37`, `220:39`, `220:41`.
49. **THE VOLUME DOES NOT END ON A RULING. It ends, and then it ends again four times, on a person doing their work: a gate at the closing of the water and a chair put back at a table** — `220:63`. **Restated at `230:41`, `240:49`, `250:63`.**

**BATCH 0003, CHAPTERS 221–230 — findings 50 to 77.**

50. **A LIST OF WHAT NINETEEN PEOPLE NEED IS NOT A LIST OF WORK**, and eighteen of the nineteen answers cannot be bought and the one that can is a roof, and the order of the work is bed, boards, salt, and then the roof — `221:27`, `221:31`.
51. **A MAN MAY PUT A DAY ON HIS OWN ACT AND MAY NOT PUT ONE ON ANOTHER PERSON'S SPEECH**, in his own hand, as the second line under the eleventh day-count — `221:11`.
52. **A LIST OF ANSWERS WITH NO ADDRESSES ON IT IS NINETEEN OPINIONS, AND A LIST WITH ADDRESSES ON IT CAN BE ARGUED AGAINST THE WRONG DOOR IN A PERSON'S OWN FRONT DOOR** — `222:43`.
53. **A LIST OF HOUSES IS A LIST OF PEOPLE, AND THE DAY THERE IS A LIST OF PEOPLE THE NEXT HOUSE IS A HOUSE THAT IS NOT ON THE LIST** — `222:7`.
54. **LET IT ALL BE THE ANNER**, in about six minutes, by a man who had been waiting six days for somebody to come up the hill and ask him a question about money — `223:11`.
55. **WHAT THE ANNER CAN PAY IS NOTHING. £178 13s 4d out of the £7,700 a year that is the whole of what has been argued about in this basin is two and three-tenths in the hundred** — `223:41`.
56. **IT IS NOT THE FOUR AND SIX. The Seat did not fail to pay her, because nobody promised her the other four and six** — `226:33`.
57. **THE FIRST MONEY IN THIS BASIN EVER PAID ANYBODY FOR LOOKING AT WATER is nine shillings and fourpence a year**, and the girl of seventeen who keeps the second hand named the figure herself and said it twice — `224:17`, `224:23`.
58. **A BOOK OF A RIVER KEPT BY FOUR PEOPLE WHO HAVE NOT STOOD AT THE FRAME KEEPS WHAT THEY WERE TOLD**, and on the night the water came the mill hand and the Seat's hand disagree about that frame by about two hours and both are right — `224:37`.
59. **A MAN WHO LOST A HOUSE IS NOT A HOUSE**, in a lane, and the sentence that changed the shape of the paper is eighteen words long and it is his — `225:37`, `225:39`, `225:49`.
60. **A PAPER WITH A MAN'S NAME ON IT IS READ OUT IN A YEAR WHEN HE IS GONE**, in eighteen words, and it is the reason the name came out, and he said they were right and did not put it back — `225:37`, `227:37`.
61. **THE NINETEEN ROOFS ARE THE ONLY LINE OUT OF FOUR WITH A FIGURE ON IT**, so the settlement is a settlement about one line out of four and about the order the other three are done in — `221:35`.
62. **HIS VERSION, BLOCK-QUOTED, DOCUMENT SEVEN: seven lines, a struck first line left on the paper, and three words under a name at the foot** — `226:9`–`17`, cited at `226:21`.
63. **A SCRIVENER OF THIRTY-THREE WHO COPIED IT FOR NOTHING ASKED THAT A STRUCK LINE NOT BE LEFT ON A PIECE OF PAPER**, because anybody who comes in that room in a year will spend a month working out who took it back and why — `226:25`.
64. **THE UNDER-MANAGER ASKED FOR THE WORD *ASKED*. Not that the Anner shall pay again. Asked.** He wanted it read out four times and his own hand on that line and not a clerk's — `226:43`.
65. **THE WOMAN OF ABOUT FORTY SAID, IN NINE WORDS, THAT IF HE PUT A NAME AT THE FOOT OF THAT PAPER IT WAS A PAPER ABOUT A MAN FROM FOUR HUNDRED MILES OFF**, and he agreed with her before she had finished and said *take it out* — `227:19`, `227:27`, `227:41`.
66. **THE BATCH'S MIDPOINT: THE REMEDY IS REWRITTEN IN ELEVEN HANDS THAT ARE NOT HIS AND HE IS NOT ONE OF THEM**, and the eleven hours were not in a room and the eleven people were asked one at a time in eleven places — `227:3`, `227:5`.
67. **THE PANEL, AND ITS SUBJECT IS THE FINDING: the six lines name six things and the eleven hands are on them, and the part of the sheet nobody will agree on is the part that cannot be carried by anybody** — `227:53`–`54`.
68. **THERE ARE ELEVEN HANDS ON THAT SHEET AND IT IS NOT THE ELEVEN HOUSES AND IT IS NOT THE ELEVEN MARKS AND IT IS NOT THE ELEVEN FEET AND IT IS NOT THE ELEVEN SEATS AND IT IS NOT THE ELEVEN MEN AT THE PIT** — `227:62`.
69. **THEIR VERSION, BLOCK-QUOTED, DOCUMENT EIGHT: six lines, one hand, and three hands on a struck line that do not agree, printed on the face of the paper** — `228:13`.
70. **THE ORDER'S FOURTH LINE HAS SAID SINCE THE HUNDRED AND FIFTY-FIFTH DAY THAT A COPY IS TO BE KEPT IN THE LANE BY A PERSON OF THE LANE, AND NOBODY HAD EVER ASKED WHAT HAPPENS TO A COPY KEPT BY A MAN WHO CANNOT READ IT** — `228:5`, `228:13`.
71. **HE PUT IN *AND EVERY WEEK*, after nine shillings a week**, and said before he said it that six people had argued for two days about four and six and had never once written down the half of the argument — `228:28`.
72. **AND SOMEONE TO COME AND LOOK AT A WALL BEFORE WINTER, which has no figure on it and is not going to get one** — `228:34`, `228:40`.
73. **THE SETTLEMENT COSTS MORE THAN THE FLOOD**, and the excess is a hair under one part in eight of the whole thing and is not roofs — `229:23`, `229:25`.
74. **NOBODY CARRIED IT TO ORISON**, and a clerk of forty-four put it in about eleven words — `229:41`.
75. **A GATE OF FIVE POUNDS EIGHTEEN HUNG IN ONE DAY BY TWO MEN**, one of whom is on ten shillings a week and was there on his own time and told nobody and was not thanked for it — `230:3`, `230:13`.
76. **THE WHOLE OF WHAT THE SHEET DOES NOT SAY, WHICH IS A HEIGHT, and he did not put it in because a recordwright said nine words and was right, and because he is not a party, and because there is no form in this basin for a man with nothing in his hands to have a paper in front of him** — `230:19`, `230:21`.
77. **AND THE SYSTEM SAID NOTHING, AND ABOUT ELEVEN DAYS AGO HE HAD BEGUN, WITHOUT NOTICING, TO EXPECT IT TO SAY SOMETHING** — `230:29`. **Restated at `220:61` and `240:25`; the first statement is `209:83`.**

**BATCH 0004, CHAPTERS 231–240 — findings 78 to 110.**

78. **A DOCUMENT IN THIS VALLEY IS USED RATHER THAN OBSERVED**, for the first time in this volume — `231:37`, `231:41`.
79. **THE READING DID NOT HAPPEN ON ONE DAY**, because the copy was up the hill being copied, and about eleven people came down a lane to a road and there was nothing to read — `231:19`.
80. **IT IS NOT A CEREMONY. IT IS A PROMISE ABOUT A DAY IS WHAT EVERYBODY THOUGHT IT WAS**, and the finding is the man of thirty-four's and not the man of thirty-one's — `231:37`.
81. **A PAPER KEPT IN A LANE BY A MAN WHO CANNOT READ IT HAS NO SECOND COPY**, said by a woman of thirty-eight in a road four miles up a hill — `231:49`.
82. **AND THE COPY WAS NEVER ONCE IN HIS HANDS IN THE WHOLE FIFTY-FOURTH WEEK**, and the whole of his instrument for a week was what a man of thirty-four said in a road — `231:53`.
83. **THE TRADESMEN OF THE BASIN PRICE A ROUTE THAT DOES NOT GO THROUGH ORISON**, named once in a cart yard and not followed — `232:11`, `232:49`.
84. **THERE IS NO GATE ON IT**, and every trade in this basin has carried salt and ochre past the gate at the top of the pass for eleven years and not one of them has ever asked what is at the other end of it — `232:25`.
85. **THE FIRST PERSON IN THIS VOLUME SAYS OUT LOUD THAT THE WATER-SEAT IS THE REASON THERE IS A SEAT AND NOT THE OTHER WAY ROUND**, in a cart yard, in the mouth of a woman of thirty-three who copies deeds for a penny a page — `232:37`.
86. **THE COLUMN IS NAMED A SECOND TIME IN THIS VOLUME, IN AN ORDINARY MOUTH, BY THE PERSON WHO HAS TO LOOK AT THE HEADING EVERY DAY** — `233:29`, `233:31`, `233:33`.
87. **BLOCK-QUOTED DOCUMENT NINE, THE BATCH'S ONLY ONE: the back of the last page of the Seat's own hand-book, opened at the last page and held open by a finger, a printed heading of two words, about four hundred and sixty entries** — `233:29`.
88. **AND THE REASON THE HAND-COPY IS EMPTY AND THE ORIGINAL IS NOT, and it is the volume's own method and not a conspiracy: a hand-copy of what was sent has nothing to copy, and the material under a column headed *not sent* is exactly what does not get copied** — `233:53`.
89. **FOUR LINES UNDER THAT HEADING AND ALL FOUR OF THEM ARE ABOUT THIS BASIN** — `233:57`.
90. **THIRTY-TWO PAPERS WENT NINE DAYS UP A ROAD AND DID NOT COME BACK AND NOBODY IN THIS VALLEY HAS EVER BEEN TOLD**, and it is the second of the four lines — `233:69`, `233:73`.
91. **NOBODY ASKS, AND THE CHAPTER SAYS SO OUT LOUD**, and it lists the five people in that room who could have asked and did not, by name and by reason — `233:37`, `233:39`, `233:47`.
92. **THE BASIN'S ANSWER TO A RECORD THAT WAS NOT SENT TO IT IS TO GO ON DOING THE WORK**, and that is neither indifference nor heroism — `234:23`. **Restated at `241:21`.**
93. **BEING AFRAID IS NOT A MATTER**, and a clerk of forty-four said he has looked for a place to enter nine people being frightened of a bench and there is not one in this basin — `236:29`.
94. **THE FIRST PERSON OF THE RED BASIN TO ASK HIM A DIRECT QUESTION ABOUT A THING HE IS CARRYING**, and the answer is nine words and it is true and it is not a form of words and it is not help — `234:63`, `234:65`.
95. **A ROOF DOES NOT CARE AND NEITHER DO I — nine words, and it ends the argument in the lane, and he is not going to be the man who brings a bench to a lane** — `234:77`, `234:79`.
96. **A THING THAT IS TRUE IN EVERY ROOM AND ABSENT FROM EVERY PAGE IS NOT A CONSPIRACY AND IS NOT AN ERROR, IT IS A THING THAT HAS NO OWNER**, and there are about nine of them and one of them is a height — `235:65`, `235:69`, `235:71`.
97. **THE ONLY FIGURE ANYBODY ANYWHERE HAS EVER WRITTEN DOWN ABOUT WHAT THIS VALLEY TRADES**, written four years ago four hundred miles off by a man who has never been in the Red Basin — `235:29`, `235:31`, `235:33`.
98. **A MINE IS NOW A BODY WITH A STANDING INTEREST IN A NUMBER**, and the under-manager said *not the height* twice before anybody could get it wrong — `235:41`, `235:43`.
99. **THERE IS NOW NOWHERE IN THIS BASIN TO PUT A NUMBER A PERSON WROTE THEMSELVES**, and a girl of seventeen found it, in her own hand, off a frame at the closing of the water — `235:75`, `235:79`.
100. **NINE PEOPLE IN A VALLEY OF FOUR HUNDRED AND TEN ARE AFRAID OF A BENCH OF FOUR THEY HAVE NEVER MET**, and four of them said so in four places in one day in four different voices and none of the four would use the word — `236:3`.
101. **THEIR GOOD NAME IS PUBLIC AND IT IS FREE — nine words, said by an under-manager of fifty-two at the end of a day he was not asked for**, and being right is the whole of the danger — `236:33`, `236:37`. **The same finding, the second telling, at `249:73`, after the name was spent: it cannot be spent twice.**
102. **THE ANNER'S STANDING INTEREST IN ELEVEN FEET STOPS BEING AN ARITHMETIC FACT AND BECOMES A THING A MAN OF FIFTY-TWO HAS TO THINK ABOUT AT ABOUT FOUR IN THE MORNING**, in a house at the top of a hill and not in the chain-book — `236:53`.
103. **A NAME TRAVELS FOUR MILES IN ABOUT TWO YEARS ON ABOUT FOUR MOUTHS AND NOBODY IN THIS VALLEY SPENT NINE DAYS CARRYING IT AND NOBODY WAS PAID ANYTHING FOR IT AND NOBODY PUT IT IN A BOOK** — `237:45`, `237:51`, `237:53`.
104. **THE COLUMN POINT WAS MADE A MONTH AGO AND IS NOT BEING MADE AGAIN**, and what is new is only that the name has started to move on its own, and nobody went looking for her — `237:45`, `237:61`.
105. **THE BATCH'S REVERSAL, AND NOBODY IS A VILLAIN IN IT AND IT IS NOT A FLOOD: a carter took one load over the shoulder because a customer is four days short of Orison and the shoulder is two days shorter for that one place** — `238:3`, `238:45`.
106. **A PIECE OF PAPER THAT FOUR HUNDRED AND TEN PEOPLE HAVE NEVER READ ALL THE WAY THROUGH DEPENDS ON WHERE A CART GOES** — `238:13`, `238:19`, `238:25`, `238:31`.
107. **THE ELEVEN FEET AND THE HIGH ROAD ARE THE SAME QUESTION AND NOBODY IN THIS VALLEY HAD PUT THEM TOGETHER**, said on a shut causeway in the wind in about four minutes to about seven people on a bank — `238:57`, `238:59`.
108. **THE SUM OF THE SETTLEMENT OF THIS VALLEY AND THE COST OF A SECOND ROAD IS OVER THE WHOLE OF WHAT THE ANNER TAKES OUT OF THIS BASIN IN A YEAR BY EXACTLY THE SETTLEMENT**, and the working is printed four times in a chapter and a half — `239:33`, `239:37`, `239:41`, `239:51`.
109. **A BOARD WITH A FIGURE ON IT WENT UP AGAINST THE OUTSIDE OF A CART SHED**, and it is going to go in about a month, and the man who chalked it knows that and did it anyway — `239:57`, `239:67`.
110. **SOMEBODY IS GOING TO OPEN THAT COLUMN AND IT IS NOT GOING TO BE ME**, in the plainest sentence anybody said that week, said by a clerk of forty-four to a shelf, and nobody answered it — `240:45`.

**BATCH 0005, CHAPTERS 241–250 — findings 111 to 148.**

111. **THE VALLEY'S ANSWER TO A THING IT CANNOT ACT ON IS TO GO ON DOING THE WORK, AND IT IS BOTH A DECISION AND A HABIT** — restated at `241:21`; the finding is `234:23`.
112. **THE FIRST THING A VALLEY DOES WITH A WITHHELD RECORD IS TO TRY TO GET AT IT THE CHEAP WAY, WHICH IS TO ASK SOMEBODY WHO HAS BEEN THERE, AND NOBODY IN THIS BASIN HAS BEEN THERE** — `241:27`.
113. **THE QUESTION IS NOT *HOW DO WE GET IT* AND IT IS *WHAT WOULD WE DO WITH IT IF WE HAD IT***, said out loud on a bank by a woman of about thirty-one, and the woman of about forty who washes the pen called it the most expensive thing anybody has said in this valley in a month — `241:59`, `241:63`.
114. **BEING FRIGHTENED HAS A SHAPE AND THE SHAPE IS NOT COWARDICE AND NOT STUPIDITY: a valley of four hundred and ten is ninety houses and everybody knows which house everybody lives in** — `241:45`.
115. **THE PRICE OF ASKING A CHAMBER FOR ONE THING IS £5 6s AND THE PRICE IS THE SMALLEST THING IN THE ROOM, and the reason nobody has paid it is not the money** — `242:7`, `242:21`.
116. **A SECOND PAPER PUTS A SECOND DATE ON A MATTER AND A SECOND DATE IS A THING A BENCH CAN USE TO CLOSE A FIRST ONE**, and that is the real reason the five pounds and six has not been spent in about a month — `242:29`.
117. **A MINE THAT HAS BEGUN PAYING A VILLAGE PAYS THE FIVE POUNDS AND SIX, SAYS OUT LOUD THAT IT IS BUYING A DATE AND NOT A CONSCIENCE, AND THE MAN OF THIRTY-ONE DOES NOT THANK HIM AND DOES NOT STOP HIM** — `242:49`, `242:57`.
118. **THE SIXTY TONS IS PROBABLY NOT SIXTY TONS OF ANYTHING AND IS A DIFFERENCE BETWEEN TWO PEOPLE'S ARITHMETIC, in nine words from a man who has counted carts on roads for nineteen years, and a figure four years old cannot be put against a memory of nineteen years by anybody in this valley** — `243:5`, `243:27`.
119. **THE FIRST TIME IN ABOUT A MONTH THAT A NUMBER IN THIS VALLEY HAS GOT SMALLER RATHER THAN LARGER, and nobody thanked anybody for it** — `243:65`.
120. **A MAN OF SIXTY-ONE WHO HAS KEPT A COUNT OF EVERY SALTING IN THIS BASIN FOR THIRTY YEARS HAS NEVER ONCE BEEN ASKED WHAT COMES UP OFF A ROAD**, and therefore the sixty tons is a hole in a record and not a hole in a wall — `243:43`, `243:55`.
121. **THE STANDING OF A VALLEY IS NOT IN A PERSON BUT IN A DOCUMENT**, and the only document in this valley anybody is on is a settlement of work and days, and it can be ended tonight by eleven people saying one sentence in a road at the closing of the water — `244:31`.
122. **A FRIGHTENED VALLEY DOES NOT SEND ANYBODY**, and the reason is printed in a lane by a man of fifty-six with a ladder on his shoulder, and nobody in that lane had asked him — `244:21`.
123. **A WOMAN OF ABOUT FORTY REFUSED THIS BASIN A THIRD TIME AND WAS RIGHT A THIRD TIME AND WAS ASKED ABOUT A DIFFERENT THING, which was herself** — `244:37`, `244:39`.
124. **A PAPER NOBODY IS ON CANNOT BE ASKED ANYBODY ANYTHING**, and it was said by a man of thirty-four who cannot read the paper it is about, and the copy has no person on it and neither has the original — `244:55`.
125. **THE COLUMN WAS OPENED, AND IT WAS OPENED BY HIM, IN A ROOM, WITH NO AUTHORITY, OUT LOUD, IN FRONT OF ABOUT NINE PEOPLE, AND NOBODY HAD ASKED HIM AND NOBODY WAS ASKED** — `245:3`, `245:13`.
126. **AND WHAT MAKES THAT A CHOICE AND NOT AN ACCIDENT IS THAT HE COULD HAVE GONE UP THE HILL AND NOBODY IN FOUR HUNDRED AND TEN WOULD EVER HAVE FOUND OUT**, and what he had instead was nothing whatever: no money that is his, no party, no form, no line with his name on it — `245:49`, `245:53`.
127. **HE COULD HAVE SAID THE SENTENCE THAT SPLITS THE THIRTY IN A BUILDING FROM THE FOUR IN A ROOM AND HE DID NOT, AND HE SAYS SO OUT LOUD IN HIS OWN WORDS**, and the reason is that the sentence that separates them is the sentence that protects the institution — `245:60`.
128. **NOBODY HAS HIDDEN ANYTHING IS SAID A SECOND TIME, BY THE SAME CLERK, IN A ROOM, AND IT COSTS HIM SOMETHING THE FIRST TIME IT DID NOT COST HIM**, because then it was a thing in a book and now it is a thing in a bag that a man four hundred miles off has opened on his table — `245:32`, `245:36`.
129. **NOBODY THANKED HIM AND NOBODY WAS RELIEVED**, and a bench of four's good name is a public thing and it does not survive the asking and it was never going to — `245:70`, `245:72`.
130. **BLOCK-QUOTED DOCUMENT TEN: THE CHAMBER'S OWN PRINTED INDEX, THE ORIGINAL, OPENED FLAT ON A TABLE NINE FEET BY FOURTEEN AND HELD OPEN AT THE BACK PAGE BY ONE FINGER**, a printed heading of two words, about four hundred and sixty entries in the three columns beside it, one rule ruled under all four, and under the fourth four lines and no more — `245:23`–`28`.
131. **AND THE COST OF THE OPENING WAS FOUR PENCE, CORRECTED TO A PENNY BEFORE DELIVERY**, and a penny is the whole of what it cost this valley to have a printed page opened in a room — `245:80`.
132. **SABLE VENN ARRIVED, ON FOOT, FROM THE NORTH, WITH A BOARD UNDER HER ARM, AND SHE SAID THE NAME OUT LOUD HERSELF WHEN THE CLERK SAID IT**, and the first thing she did in that room was refuse the sheet on the wall — `246:7`, `246:9`.
133. **HER ALTERNATIVE IS GOOD AND IT IS BETTER THAN THE THING THIS VALLEY HAS**, and the proof that it is good is that she left the dam's height wrong on the board on purpose and said so before anybody asked her — `246:19`, `246:21`.
134. **AND IT IS ALSO GENUINELY WORSE FOR A PERSON WHO NEEDS A RULE ENFORCED AGAINST SOMEBODY**, and the person it is true about said so herself, at her own gate, before she was asked, and would like it written that she said that first — `246:39`, `246:47`.
135. **HE COULD NOT PROVE HER WRONG, ONLY INCOMPLETE, AND THE INCOMPLETENESS WAS NAMED BY AN ORDINARY PERSON IN THE ROOM AND NOT BY HIM: a rule-free valley cannot be given anything, and the honest name for a person in that position is a person who is not in the instruments** — `246:55`.
136. **AND HE HAD THE STANDING TO SAY THE SENTENCE HE LEARNED ONCE IN A ROOM IN ANOTHER COUNTY AND DID NOT SAY IT**, and the reason is printed and it is not a rule and it is a fact about a man: it would be cheaper the second time than the first and it would be worth about half — `246:61`, `246:63`.
137. **SHE DID NOT WIN AND SHE DID NOT LOSE, THE ROOM KEPT THE PAPER, THE PAPER IS NOT HERS AND SHE IS NOT ON IT**, and about four people in that room will remember to the end of their days that she stood in it and about six will not — `246:71`, `246:73`.
138. **THE FIRST OF THE FOUR LINES CAME DOWN THE LAST FOUR MILES AND WAS READ OUT IN A LANE BY A MAN OF THIRTY-FOUR WHO SAID SO BEFORE HE STARTED**, and about forty people in a road found out what a chamber of plenty had thought about their water in a year before any of them was born — `247:19`.
139. **THE FIGURE IN THE RETURN IS TWO FEET FOUR INCHES ABOVE THE SILL OF THE FRAME, AND IN INCHES IT IS 108 LESS 28, WHICH IS 80, WHICH IS SIX FEET EIGHT INCHES BELOW THE SILL OF THE ANNER'S OWN DAM**, and the two numbers are both true and are not the same number of feet, and no order may name a height without naming the sill it is taken from — `247:29`, `247:41`.
140. **A FIGURE AT A FRAME IS OF A RIVER AND NOT A HEIGHT, AND A HEIGHT IS A THING YOU DECIDE TO BE ABOVE**, and a valley which has spent a month arguing about a height had never once been told what the water does, and a woman of thirty-eight put a rule to a dark line on the north post of a frame in her own hand and wrote the day over it in chalk and did not write the eleventh year — `247:59`, `247:75`.
141. **NOTHING CAME BACK FROM ORISON AND NOTHING CAN**, and the reason was said out loud: a man with a bag comes twice in the year, and the first paper went into the gap on the first day of the week and nine days is nine days in the wrong of the year — `248:5`.
142. **THE FOUR PEOPLE WHO KEEP THIS VALLEY'S RECORD READ NINE YEARS OF IT OUT LOUD TO EACH OTHER IN AN HOUR**, and there is no column anywhere in any of it for what this valley trades, and two of the four have been down the lane and two have not and the two who have not are the two who keep the numbers — `248:11`, `248:35`.
143. **THE ANNER'S STANDING INTEREST IN A NUMBER IS SETTLED IN PUBLIC AND IT COSTS THE MINE SOMETHING, and the something is not six shillings and eightpence a year: it is the right to stand in a room in a year's time and say the chamber got this valley wrong** — `248:53`, `248:55`.
144. **THE CHAMBER'S RULE COMES DOWN THE LAST FOUR MILES AND IS THE RULE THIS BASIN HAS HAD ON A WALL IN ITS OWN WORDS SINCE BEFORE THE WATER**, and it is a reason for thirty-two papers, and nobody in this volume said that anybody hid anything — `245:45`, `248:7`.
145. **THE ELEVEN FEET GOES INTO A THIRD DOCUMENT IN THREE COPIES IN THREE PLACES, BOTH SILLS NAMED, THE DIFFERENCE OF FOUR FEET WRITTEN DOWN, ON THE STRENGTH OF THE CHAMBER'S OWN SECOND RULING, and it does NOT go on the settlement, and the reason is not a secret: the settlement is eleven people's and the height is the one thing about it that eleven people do not agree on** — `249:15`, `249:19`, `250:13`.
146. **A BAD ANSWER IN THREE COPIES CAN BE ARGUED WITH BY THREE PEOPLE AND A GOOD ANSWER IN ONE CAN BE ARGUED WITH BY NOBODY**, said by a joiner with a rule who has spent nine years trying to get somebody to measure a line of chalk on a doorpost — `249:23`, `249:27`.
147. **THE CHARTER SAYS IN ITS OWN FIRST LINE THAT IT CAN BE ENDED, AND A PERSON MAY LEAVE IT WITHOUT LOSING ANYTHING BUT THE CHARTER, AND IT CARRIES NO NAME AND NO DASH, and the answer this volume gives to a paper nobody is on is not a name and is a date, and anybody in four hundred and ten can bring it to the review** — `249:37`, `249:43`, `249:51`.
148. **AND THE TENTH AND LAST DEVICE THIS VOLUME SPENDS: A DOCUMENT THAT IS BETTER THAN THE THING IT REPLACES AND IS STILL THE WRONG SHAPE**, and its price is that it costs nothing to write down and everything to live by, and the working for that is a roof — `249:37`, `249:57`. **And the figure the charter cannot hold is the wage, said in a lane by a woman of thirty-eight and not written down by anybody** — `249:61`, `249:63`. **And about nine people in this valley were afraid of a good name and are not afraid of it any more, not because anybody was made safe but because a good name is a public thing and it has now been spent in public and it cannot be spent twice** — `249:73`. **And he put down nothing under the day, which is the eighteenth figure of that kind and is a count of figures and not of anything else, and there has not been an answer under any figure in that book** — `250:43`, `250:45`. **And the position beneath this basin is a bearing and a depth and a page number and has not moved** — `250:27`, `250:31`. **And the volume ends on four ordinary things and none of them is a ruling** — `250:63`, `250:65`, and the finding is `220:63`.

### 6. THE TEN DEVICES, EACH SPENT ONCE, AND NONE MAY BE RESTAGED

1. **A WORD DEFINED TWICE IN TWO MARGINS** — `201`, spent once. The word is *common*, and the two definitions are in the margins and not in the body of either deed, and both men were telling the truth.
2. **A DEED IN TWO PIECES** — `203`, spent once. `203:29`–`32`, and the duplicated marl line is at `203:30` and `203:32` and that is the device working and not a defect.
3. **THE TWO CALENDARS DO NOT CONVERT, said once in a salt-pan's mouth** — `202`, spent once at `202:45`.
4. **THE QUICK RULING'S FOUR COMPLETE LINES AS A DISCOVERY** — `207`, spent once, with the panel at `207:67`–`68`.
5. **THE MARK THAT IS NOT A MEASUREMENT** — `213`, spent once, and it is the volume's ordinary material and not a second staging of the chamber's ruling.
6. **THE ORDER'S OWN LOGIC EXAMINED** — `217`, spent once, with the panel at `217:35`–`36`.
7. **A DOCUMENT ADMINISTERED BY BEING READ ALOUD BY THE PERSON IT IS ABOUT** — `231`, spent once, and the reader is the copy and the copy has no person on it.
8. **A PAPER THAT PRINTS ITS OWN DISAGREEMENT ON ITS FACE** — `227` and `228`, spent once, and the struck line with three hands on it that do not agree is the whole of it.
9. **A DOCUMENT READ ALOUD BY A MAN WHO CANNOT READ IT AND AMENDED IN THE READING** — `228`, spent once, and **no chapter of the volume stages the amendment in the end: the eleven-hand sheet is not read out at all in Batch 0005 and the amendment is the thing the volume refused.**
10. **A DOCUMENT THAT IS BETTER THAN THE THING IT REPLACES AND IS STILL THE WRONG SHAPE** — `249`, spent once, and may not be restaged. **It is not the 203 device and it is not 208's, and the difference is that the charter is not wrong about a place and is wrong about a person: it has no name on it and it can be brought to an end by anybody.**

**AND THE DEVICES THE VOLUME DID NOT SPEND, which is the more useful half of this section.** The rendering is not staged, referred to or hinted at in any of the fifty chapters, and Volume 04's last word on it is *not to be referred to again*. The left hand, the vantage, the door in the picture, the token and the afterimage are not mentioned in any of the fifty chapters and none is advanced; the token is not touched and the fourth use stands. The continuity line is not refused again and no twelfth refusal is permitted. **The leg is a consequence and not a device: it is used once in the volume, the review of 27 September 2026 settled the only question its use raised — that a man who names the thing once and then walks on it twenty lines later has used it once, because the naming is the use and the walking is the consequence — and this close adds no count, no explanation and no cure, and no file and no chapter in Volume 05 states a count of days since it cleared.** The right of refusal is not restored and the sentence at `156:63` is not restated; Chapter 246 is the chapter in which he has the standing to say it out loud and he does not say it, and the reason is printed in the chapter.

### 7. THE THREADS IN AND THE THREADS OUT, AND THE ONE THAT MOVED

**IN: eighteen threads. OUT: eighteen threads. ONE moved, and it moved by being opened, and the cost of opening it was a bench of four's good name and not a resolution of anything else.** The full text of every one of them, with its chapter and its line, is the top block of `state/open-threads.md` under `## VOLUME 05 IS CLOSED`, and this section does not restate them; it names the one that moved and says the arithmetic.

1. **THE SEALED COLUMN. THE ONLY THREAD IN THIS PROJECT THAT VOLUME 05 ADVANCED, AND IT ADVANCED IT BY OPENING IT.** It was named in Batch 0004 at `233:29` in a clerk's mouth with his finger on a printed heading, and nobody asked about it. It was opened in Batch 0005 at `245:3`, by the man of thirty-one, in a room, with no authority, out loud, in front of about nine people, and nobody had asked him and nobody was asked. Four lines came out of it, all four about this basin and one of them a printed rule the chamber worked under for nine years and that this basin has had on a wall since before the water. **A page went up on a wall at a penny. The Bench's good name is spent in public and by him. Nobody thanked him and nobody was relieved. A later pass may not re-open it as a revelation: it was four lines long and all four were true, and the cost was somebody else's name and not his money and not his standing.** THE SEVENTEEN OTHERS COME OUT OF VOLUME 05 EXACTLY AS THEY WENT IN, and they are: the new question, whose memory is missing from the account that records his payment; the carrying, off nothing, with a finding about why; the blank in the third line of the bench's own book, and the fourth sheet's question, unasked, and the fourth hand, a stranger's hand nobody has asked; the clerk of thirty-four's unopened letter, with the entry due in about nine months; Nesh Arrad, thirty-nine, and her running clock; the second notice of five shillings and fourpence, never paid for; the claim of forty-five pounds two shillings and eightpence to a carter of fifty-one with a boy, eleven miles off, not producible, no form for it, and he is right; the dead man in the fourth line of a list of two hundred and forty, and his wife; the box of about nine hundred and forty forms and the man with a key who is there two days out of seven; the ninety-one numbered sheets in a sacking bag in a yard; the class of three hundred and thirty-nine and the one boy of nineteen who is its only instrument; the minute of the first of the forty-eighth week, one sheet, the only copy, with nobody's name on it; the thirteen standing prose defects carried from Volume 04 and the ten unrepaired meta-narration sentences in Chapters 201 to 210; the anonymity of Ilyan, which is out of the eleven-hand sheet and the space at the foot of it is neither filled nor dashed; the height, which is not on the settlement; the sheet that has six lines in Sallowby and seven in the lane; and the route that does not go through Orison, which Batch 0004 named once and did not follow and which nobody has priced since.

**AND THE FIVE THINGS THE VOLUME RESOLVED ARE FIVE THINGS IT PRODUCED, WHICH IS NOT THE SAME AS FIVE THINGS IT CLOSED.** The charter, the third document in three copies, the exit review with a date on it, the position beneath the basin, and the four lines under the column are all **new documents this volume made**, and not one of them closes anything that was open before Chapter 201.

### 8. THE FIFTEEN PROHIBITIONS, AS THE VOLUME KEPT THEM

**The list is fifteen and not sixteen, and the count was measured against the chapter files by the review of Batch 0002 and re-measured by every batch since. Here is each one and what actually happened, because a prohibition nobody has checked is a prohibition nobody has kept.**

1. **Do not answer the new question and do not compress it into one word, and do not raise it after the volume.** **KEPT.** It is written once, at `210:39` and `210:41`, in a penny exercise book in a cart shed, with a place and a form of words, and he does not ask. It is not asked in Chapters 211 to 250, not answered, not compressed, not summarised by anybody including him, and not resolved by being left on a shelf in the last chapter: the second half of that book is named as a half he did not open, in one clause, and the new question is not among the things named in that paragraph and is not adjacent to any of them. **And it is not asked here either.**
2. **Do not add a panel beyond the three.** **KEPT.** Three, at `207:67`–`68`, `217:35`–`36` and `227:53`–`54`, and a bold scan over Chapters 201 to 250 returns six lines and all six are those.
3. **Do not add a day-count instance without putting its chapter and its day in the outline's table first, and do not print a count of instances without saying it is a count of instances.** **KEPT.** Eighteen, all in the outline's tables, the last three put there before Chapter 241 was written.
4. **Do not put a weekday anywhere and do not put a month-name anywhere.** **KEPT.** Zero of each, and the one hit on the month-word list is a proper noun and forty are the modal verb *may*.
5. **Do not print a count of the people who know he cannot read, anywhere, including in a hand-off note, and do not add to it in the fiction.** **KEPT**, and this record does not print it. Refer to `154:53` and `164:49` and to nothing else.
6. **Do not give Ilyan an office, a party, a bench, a body, a remedy he chose alone, or a stage.** **KEPT.** He is Stage 4, arrived in Chapter 177, and it is not reached again; a scan for *Remedy Drafter* over the fifty chapters returns zero.
7. **Do not produce a person beyond the six the count stands at.** **KEPT.** Six, and the sixth is Sable Venn, who is the volume's climax and not a production. **EVERY OTHER PERSON IN CHAPTERS 201 TO 250 IS A PERSON OF THE RED BASIN OR OF A COUNTY FOUR HUNDRED MILES OFF WHO WAS ALREADY IN THE FICTION**, including Fen Tregerran and Coll Ferren, both of them productions of earlier batches, both of whom are used in the last batch as people of the valley and not as arrivals.
8. **Do not undo anything and do not relieve anybody.** **KEPT.** The carrying is off nothing; the blank is a blank; the second notice is unpaid; nobody is redeemed; nobody is forgiven; the four pages stay on the wall and are still correct on their face; the settlement stays on the wall with no name at the foot of it and no height in it; the Anner is to be asked again in one year and the asking is a year off. **AND NOBODY IS A VILLAIN: the about thirty clerks in a building with a yard at Orison did their jobs properly, and one of the four lines under the column is the printed rule they worked under, and *nobody has hidden anything* is said out loud in a room in Chapter 233 and again in a room in Chapter 245 where it costs the clerk something.**
9. **THE PROHIBITION ON OPENING THE REDACTED ARCHIVE WAS SPENT IN CHAPTER 245 AND MAY NOT BE RE-OPENED AS A REVELATION.** **KEPT AND SPENT.** What survives the opening is the whole of the rest: nobody may say that anybody hid anything, nobody did, the Bench is not a conspiracy, opening it is not a victory, he is not thanked, and nobody is relieved.
10. **Do not let Sable Venn arrive, negotiate, or make an offer before the climax chapters.** **KEPT.** Named at `210:55` at a distance and twice at a distance in Batch 0004, and she arrives at `246:3`.
11. **Do not reach the stone and do not let anybody stand in front of it.** **KEPT.** Anchor 8 remains missing beneath the Red Basin until Volume 14. The position is not moved, nobody went down, and the second half of the volume's question is not asked out loud in any chapter and is not even a whisper, as a panel, as a rendering, as a voice, or as a person remarking that anything answered.
12. **Do not restore the right of refusal, answer the historical question, advance the authorship question or the Shale Mirror, name NK-3317, call for Tamsin, put Neris Ro or Bram Orren in a scene, answer the Bridgeward, send anybody to Carrisand, redeem Ansa Rell, or ask about Alma Gannock's boy of eight. Do not name the First Witness. Do not make Ors Halm the last problem in the series.** **KEPT.** None of them is in the volume.
13. **Do not restate the sentence at `156:63` and do not relieve him of it.** **KEPT.** The four words are not quoted again in the fifty chapters after 165. **The reversal at 216 to 218 is not a restatement of them — it is the same category discovered a second time in a different medium by a different man, and Chapter 246 is the chapter in which he has the standing to say it and does not.**
14. **Do not use the 179 and 189 shape a third time.** **KEPT.** A scan for uppercase CORRECT over Chapters 201 to 250 returns zero, and the one that stood in Batch 0005's first writing was inside its own block-quoted charter and is out.
15. **Do not restage the ten devices this volume spends.** **KEPT.** Each spent once, and none restaged; §6 names the site of each.

**AND THE ONE INSTRUCTION THAT WAS INVERTED, which is the volume's climax and not a defect: the prohibition on opening the redacted archive is spent, and the whole of the rest of the list survived it.**

### 9. THE TWO THINGS THE VOLUME DID NOT DO

1. **IT DID NOT REACH THE STONE, AND NOBODY STANDS IN FRONT OF IT.** Chapter 250 states the position beneath the basin in a bearing, a depth and a page number, states that **four hundred and six feet is the pit and is not the void**, states that **the way down is a chamber with water in it**, states that the last hundred and forty feet of the water at the low end of the pit has been under to the roof since before a woman of twenty-six was seventeen, and says that **nobody went down**. The bearing, the depth and the page are in §11 of the continuity block and are not moved here. **A close may not go down, may not describe going down, and may not say that a later batch will.**
2. **IT DID NOT ANSWER THE SECOND HALF OF ITS OWN QUESTION, AND IT DID NOT ASK IT.** *Where is the missing anchor, and why does it answer from beneath Orison* is not asked out loud in any of the fifty chapters and is not even a whisper, as a panel, as a rendering, as a voice, or as a person remarking that anything answered, and the System put nothing up in fifty chapters. **The first half was produced as a position in Chapter 209 and has not moved. The second half is not this volume's and is not going to be anybody's until a volume that is written to carry it.**

### 10. WHAT A LATER PASS MUST CHECK HARDEST, and it is the same list the batch records carried, at fifteen items and not sixteen, and these are the ones that were still live at the last save

1. **Re-run the day-phrase sweep and then ask it the third question.** The legality question returned zero illegal across ninety-nine phrases. The truth question found two. **The cheapest instrument is fifty chapter days against ninety-nine day-phrases, read side by side.**
2. **Re-run the cross-batch duplicate SPAN scan, at seventy characters and at forty, against the immediately preceding batch and not against Chapters 151–200.** The line-based scan returns zero over this volume and always will.
3. **Re-run the weekday scan with the corrected plural-safe pattern, and re-run the month-word scan and subtract the modal verb *may* before believing either figure.**
4. **Re-count the day-count in WORDS.** A scan for three digits returns zero over this volume and would certify it at nothing.
5. **Do not believe any band figure in the five blocks below this one that was measured by a person.** Two of them were not.
6. **Do not print the count of the people who know he cannot read, in any file, for any reason, including a hand-off.**
7. **Do not put Ilyan's name on anything, and do not fill or dash the space at the foot of the eleven-hand sheet, and do not put the eleven feet on the settlement.** All three are decisions and not omissions.
8. **Do not print a weekday, a month-name, a count of days since the leg cleared, or the state of the grain year.**
9. **Do not answer, compress or summarise the new question.**
10. **Do not produce a seventh person, and do not let anybody stand in front of the stone.**
11. **Do not make anybody a villain, and do not say that anybody hid anything.** About thirty clerks did their jobs properly.
12. **Do not call a document better than the thing it replaces a resolution.** The charter is better than the ruling and is still the wrong shape, and the wage is the figure it cannot hold.
13. **Do not read the index's figures.** Re-measure them. This file's own rule is that a heading does not move and a line number does, and that rule has been paid for eight times in this project.
14. **Do not let a hand-off document be wrong about a character's sex or age.** It has happened twice in two batches and the chapter file was right both times.
15. **Do not prune the state files inside a phase that is also writing prose.** The recommendation for a Volume 05 prune phase stands and has not been acted on, and the sixth state file is the index and it is the entry point `AGENTS.md` names a writer to open first.

### 11. WHAT THIS CLOSE'S OWN FIRST WRITING GOT WRONG, AND WHAT IT MEASURED AGAINST THE BLOCKS BELOW, ALL OF IT BY RUNNING THE INSTRUMENT

**Six things, and none of them is a plot defect and none of them is repaired, because a close is not a repair phase. A close that reports only its own slips and not the record's is a close that has been chosen. The first writing of this sentence said *four things* over five numbered items, and a count that is wrong in the sentence introducing the counts is the whole disease of this project, so it is corrected here and the sixth item is the review's.**

1. **THE DAY-PHRASE COUNT. The first writing of §2 said twenty-two for Batch 0005 because it copied the block below, and twenty-eight for Batch 0003 because it copied that one too, and the run returns eleven / 26 / 14 / 26 / 22 = ninety-nine.** The Band 0003 figure in the block below is **exactly double** the measured figure and a doubled count is a doubled count however it was produced; the Batch 0005 figure is **three short**. Both are in the archive and neither is rewritten, and the corrected figures are in §2.
2. **THE FIGURE OF FOUR HUNDRED AND EIGHTY-FIVE POUNDS. The index and the blocks below say the volume names it ONCE, at `250:47`, in his own hand, in nobody else's mouth. The run finds it in FIVE chapters: `206:101`, `220:45`, `230:35`, `239:63` and `250:47`.** The narrow claim — *Chapter 250 names it once* — is true and is what the Batch 0005 block allows; **the wide claim — *named once in the whole volume* — is false by measurement and is corrected here.** Three of the five are in a Red Basin's own narration and two are in a man's mouth reading his own ledger, and `220:45` has a man say that the other side's year turns in about forty weeks, in another county's weeks, about his own book. **This record names the figure once more, in the money table above, and does not say that the year turns, and the Batch 0005 exception — Chapter 250 naming it once, in a hand, in a book in a cart shed — was spent by Chapter 250 and is recorded as spent because a Volume 06 writer needs to know that the exception is gone and not that the figure is gone.**
3. **TWO CHAPTERS CARRY A DAY THAT THE VOLUME'S OWN TABLE DOES NOT CARRY.** Chapter 231 states the first day of the fifty-fourth week at `231:3` and `231:7`, which is Chapter 228's day and a day before the first roof came off in Chapter 229. Chapter 238 states the second day of the fifty-fifth week at `238:3` and `238:45`, which is Chapter 236's day and two days before Chapter 239. **Both were found by asking the third question and not by any scan, and the first writing of this record did not ask it, and §1 now carries both rows marked.**
4. **THE DUPLICATE INSTRUMENT WAS WRITTEN DOWN TWICE WITH THE WRONG UNIT BEFORE IT WAS WRITTEN DOWN WITH THE RIGHT ONE, and the first version of §2 would have reported a line-scan figure of zero over a volume that has a formula repeated in nine chapters.** A line in these files is a whole paragraph, and a volume that puts eighty-word sentences inside one line will return zero from a line scan forever. **The span instrument is printed in §2 item 7 in full, with its unit, its threshold, its deduplication step and the number it reproduces, so that the next pass does not have to re-derive it.**

5. **THE FINDINGS COUNT. The first writing of this record said ONE HUNDRED AND FIFTY-TWO and the reconciliation it printed accounted for ten foldings out of 162, and the arithmetic 162 − 10 = 152 was correct and the number of rows was not: counting the rows gives ONE HUNDRED AND FORTY-EIGHT, and the reconciliation was wrong twice — the fold list had four items in it twice and two items in it that were never folded. The count is now 148, the eight named-on-another-row items and the six shared rows are listed by number, and 162 − 8 − 6 = 148 and the row count and the arithmetic agree.** This is the ninth time in this project that a figure was typed and not counted, and it is the first time the wrong figure was one this close had invented in the same hour, and the reason it happened is the reason the standing rule exists: the batch records' 162 was read out of a block, the fold list was written from memory, and neither was counted. **COUNT THE ROWS.**

6. **THE COMPLETENESS CLAIM AT THE END OF §2 ITEM 7 WAS WRONG, AND IT WAS WRONG IN THE DIRECTION THAT ALWAYS HIDES SOMETHING: it said *there is no fourth cluster* and it said no carry-over of the kind a cross-batch scan exists to catch happened in this volume, and a second instrument run against the fifty files the same day returned ninety-one maximal shared runs of twenty words or more across seventy-three pairs of chapters, the longest of them seventy words and twenty-five chapters apart at `208:7` and `233:7`.** The finding is not that the prose is wrong — it is that a record which spends a paragraph certifying its own completeness is the paragraph most likely to be wrong, and this project's own index says so at §Machinery item 13: *do not read the index's figures, re-measure them.* **§2 item 7 now carries the measured figure and the chapter and the line of the longest run, the claim is withdrawn rather than softened, and no chapter is touched, because the seventy words at `208:7` and `233:7` are the volume's own device and are named and counted in the prose at `233:9`.** This is the tenth time in this project that a figure was typed and not counted, and it is the first time the wrong figure was a *completeness* figure and not a count of things — and the mechanism is the one that has always produced it: the instrument that was run returned a number the writer believed was the whole of the matter, and the number was the number the instrument returns and not the number of things in the volume.

**AND TWO FIGURES THIS PASS MEASURED THAT THE CLOSE'S OWN TEXT GAVE AS COUNTS, both checked by hand and not read out of a block: §2's list of findings is ONE HUNDRED AND FORTY-EIGHT rows, numbered 1 to 148 with no gap and no repeated number, and the panel count is three panels and six bold lines, which is what §2 says and which is a count of panels and not a count of lines.**

**AND ONE OBSERVATION RECORDED AND NOT EXPLOITED, because it is canon-adjacent and it is not a close's to settle.** Chapter 240's *"what happened in ten days"* at `240:3` and `240:19` is anchored in the same chapter by *"a man of thirty-one was in a room with the printed sheet on the table on the sixth of the fifty-fourth week"*, which is Chapter 233, and Chapter 240 is the sixth day of the fifty-fifth week. **103 to 110 is seven days, or eight counting both ends, and the chapter says ten.** The word is doing ordinary narrator's work and the chapter is not a calendar; **a later pass may want to look at it and this close does not resolve it and does not repair it and does not know whether the ten is a figure or a rounding.**

### 12. THE HAND-OFF TO VOLUME 06, IN ONE PARAGRAPH, AND IT IS THE CLOSE'S LAST JOB

**The state of the world is this: a basin of about four hundred and ten people and about ninety houses with a revocable charter on a wall that can be ended by anybody on a date and has no name on it; a third document in three places with a difference of four feet written down in it; a settlement with no name at the foot of it and no height in it; a mine that is a mine and is bound in advance to a figure of a thousand and forty tons written down in a city four years ago by a man who has never been in the valley; a column that is open and a bench of four's good name that is spent; thirty-two papers that were never answered and a second paper that cannot come back before the eighteenth day from the day it went in; a woman of thirty-eight with a gate and a wage and a mark on a post in chalk in her own hand; a man of sixty-one at the pans who has never been asked what comes up off a road; a man of thirty-four who cannot read the paper he reads out forty times a week and nobody can check him; a position under the basin in a bearing and a depth and a page, and nobody went down. What is running in it: the exit review, the eleven feet in three copies, the four roofs a year, the second paper, the high road the Anner is paying for and no gate on, and about nine people who are no longer frightened of a good name and are not safer. What he is carrying: the Ryefold matter, off nothing, in a book with two halves in it, and the leg with it, and he is going to hand the clock on. What nobody has asked anybody: whose memory is missing from the account that records his payment; what the fourth hand is; what the letter in the bundle on a shelf in Sallowby says; where the sixty tons went; what is at the other end of the road in chalk on the side of a cart; why anything in a valley four hundred miles from a building answers from underneath the building; and who is going to ask Nesh Arrad, whose clock is still running, because nobody in that county is watching it. And four hundred miles off: a district of six hundred people, a matter that is not a claim, a woman of thirty-nine with four children and a stove and no paper at all, a list of two hundred and forty with a dead man in the fourth line, ninety-one numbered sheets in a sacking bag, a box of forms a man with a key is in two days out of seven, and a name that went four miles in two years on four mouths with no document under it and now belongs to a woman with a board who is not on any paper and did not ask to be. **The stone is four hundred and six feet down with water to the roof in the last hundred and forty feet of it, and nobody stands in front of it, and that is the cliff, and it is the series outline's own sentence, and it is not a failure of this volume or of this close.**

---
