> **WHAT THIS BLOCK IS, and it is two artifacts and no more.** The Batch 0005 phase extended `outline/volume-05.md` with a Batch 0005 table, a day-count table, a panel line, a block-quote table, a money table, a devices list and the fifteen prohibitions **before it wrote a word of Chapter 241**, and then wrote Chapters 241 to 250. **It wrote no review, no close and no volume audit, and it DID write the one next-phase prompt it was told to write, at `workspace/volume-05/volume-close/PROMPT.md`, which is the Volume 05 close and is a separate phase. Volume 05's fifty chapters are now written and the volume's next phase is a close, not a batch.** `outline/series.md` outranks `outline/volume-05.md` and outranks this file.
>
> **THE STANDING INSTRUCTION IS UNCHANGED AND IT IS THE WHOLE OF WHAT MAKES ANY OF THIS TRUSTWORTHY: re-run every scan against the chapter files and do not re-read a list.** Every number in this block was measured by a script over `chapters/volume-05/chapter-0241.md` to `chapter-0250.md` after the last save, and where a count appears it says what it is a count of. **A count in these files that carries no line number beside it is unverified, however confident the sentence around it is.**
>
> **WHAT THIS BLOCK DOES NOT DO, EXCEPT ONE THING.** **THE COLUMN IS OPEN. Chapter 245, the fourth day of the fifty-sixth week, in the Seat's one room, by him, with no authority, in front of about nine people, and nobody had asked him to.** That is the one thing this batch did that four earlier batches barred, and it is the volume's climax, and `outline/series.md` fixes it there. The cost is a bench of four's good name, spent in public and by him, and nobody thanked him and nobody was relieved. Everything else is unresolved: the new question is not raised and not answered, his name is not on the settlement and the space at its foot is empty and undashed, the height is not on the settlement and is in a third document in three copies, the carrying is still off nothing, the blank in the third line is a blank, nobody is redeemed, nobody is forgiven, the grain year is not turned and the four hundred and eighty-five pounds is named once in a hand and is not converted.

### 2. THE INSTRUMENTS, re-run over `chapters/volume-05/chapter-0241.md` to `chapter-0250.md` after the last save and NOT read out of any list. **Every figure here says what it is a count of.**

### 3. THE THREE CLOCKS, unchanged, obeyed in every chapter, and the volume's engine was still arithmetic and not politics when it ended

**The Shelf keeps a grain year of fifty-two weeks. The Red Basin keeps three clocks and there is no conversion between any of them, and the finding that there is no conversion was made ONCE, at `202:45`, in a salt-pan's mouth, and forty-eight chapters are about what people do about a thing that has already been found out.** The pan-book year: fifty saltings, more or less, a salting being a drying, a dry-year salting about seven weeks and a wet-year one eleven, and therefore no length in a salting and no first drying that holds twice. The turn: a year from the first shaking, which is turn nought, twelve years back, and this year is turn twelve. The day: a day since the second shaking, out of the water-book, and it is the only date in this basin that can be put on the outside of a bag that is going nine days to a city of four hundred thousand people.

**AND WHAT THIS BATCH DID TO THE CLOCKS IS THE FIRST TIME THE BASIN HAS PUT A DATE ON A DOCUMENT OF ITS OWN.** Chapter 247 is the chapter: a return taken in the eleventh year came down in words, and the only date anybody in this valley can put on a piece of paper out of a city is the morning it was said out loud in a road, and the girl of seventeen wrote the day in chalk on the north post of a frame at the eighth hour and did not write the eleventh year, **because there is no conversion and she is the last person in this basin who would try to make one in chalk on a post.** And Chapter 249's charter is dated by a reference to that water-book and carries no Shelf week and no month and no turn, which is the first document in this basin that is dated in a way its own readers can check. **AND THE SYSTEM PUT NOTHING UP AND SAID NOTHING WHATEVER ABOUT ANY OF IT for fifty chapters, and a man of thirty-one has stopped expecting it to, and he did not tell anybody, and this valley keeps no book of what a person has stopped expecting.**

**AND THE SHELF SIDE STAYS SHUT, WITH THE ONE NAMED EXCEPTION AND NO MORE.** The Shelf's grain year is fifty-two weeks, week fifty-two is days eighty-four to ninety, and therefore Shelf day ninety-one was the first day of a NEW grain year on the Shelf. **Batches 0003, 0004 and 0005 did not turn the year and did not say the year turns, and Chapter 250 names the four hundred and eighty-five pounds ONCE, as a figure that is due and that he is carrying, in his own hand's ledger in a book in a cart shed and in nobody else's mouth, which is the single exception the Batch 0005 prompt allows and it is used once.** The other half of that book was not opened in Chapter 250 and the new question is on it and is not raised.

### 4. THE MONEY, every figure recomputed in a script and the working printed beside it. **THE INSTRUMENT IS THE SAME ONE EVERY TIME: print the unit price in the sentence, find it, and multiply. A script checked all twenty-eight figures printed in Chapters 241–250 against the arithmetic and returned zero failures. **THAT SENTENCE WAS FALSE AND THE REVIEW OF 27 SEPTEMBER 2026 FOUND FOUR FAILURES IN IT, WHICH IS THE MOST IMPORTANT LINE IN THIS BLOCK. A script checks the figures it is given and three of these four were not arithmetic at all: they were a unit price converted wrongly, a chain of three relations that could not yield the figure printed at the end of it, a ratio stated in round words, and a number that contradicted the sentence beside it. A script does arithmetic and does not read. A money chapter is checked by a person reading the sentence the figure sits in, and this volume has now been bitten by that in three reviews running.**

- **THE MATTER TO ORISON, AND IT IS SPENT IN THIS BATCH FOR THE FIRST TIME IN THE VOLUME.** Entry a pound, the day four pounds, nine days' board at eightpence. 240d + 960d + 72d = **1,272d = £5 6s** ✓ (`242:7`). **AND WHO PAID AND OUT OF WHAT: the Anner, out of the Anner's own cost of the high road, and the payer and the amount and the thing wanted are written on the outside of the sheet in a clerk's hand, because a matter nobody is paying for comes back in nine days** (`242:53`, `242:63`).
- **AND WHAT THE PRICE IS AGAINST THE TWO WAGES IN THIS VALLEY, printed with the working, and this is the batch's first new money and it replaced a wrong sentence in the first writing.** A pitman's week is 120d, and 1,272d is ten of them and 72d over. A sluice-keeper's week is 108d, and 1,272d is eleven of them and 84d over, which is about three quarters of the sixteen weeks a year she is paid for ✓ (`242:15`). **THE PHRASE *ABOUT TWO THIRDS OF EVERY YEAR SHE WORKS* WHICH STOOD HERE UNTIL 27 SEPTEMBER 2026 WAS WRONG: 1,272 ÷ 108 = 11.78 weeks, and 11.78 ÷ 16 = 0.736, which is about three quarters of the sixteen weeks she is paid for, and the chapter now says so in those words.**
- **AND THE SETTLEMENT AGAINST THE PRICE, with Tarin Keel correcting Jory Vellan in the open and being right twice.** 1,272 × 33 = 41,976d, and 42,880 − 41,976 = **904d over**, so the price goes into the settlement thirty-three whole times and not thirty-four and not a hundred and forty ✓ (`242:35`).
- **THE SIXTY TONS, turned from a scandal into a question.** 60 × £7 = **£420 a year**; 60 tons = **1,200 hundredweights**, and a hundredweight of salt at the pans is £1 4s = **288d**, and 1,200 × 288d = **345,600d = £1,440** ✓, and there is no weigh-book anywhere in the basin, and nobody has counted what comes UP off a road in thirty years (`243:33`). **THE FIGURES 168d, 201,600d AND £840 THAT STOOD HERE UNTIL 27 SEPTEMBER 2026 WERE WRONG: £1 4s IS 240 + 48 = 288d AND NOT 168d, AND 1,200 × 288 = 345,600, WHICH IS £1,440 AND NOT £840. IT IS THE EXACT FAILURE CLASS THIS VOLUME'S MONEY GUARDRAIL EXISTS TO CATCH, BECAUSE THE WRONG CONVERSION PRODUCED A ROUNDER FIGURE THAN THE PARTS DO, AND WHAT CAUGHT IT WAS NOT THE SCRIPT AND WAS A REVIEWER READING THE SENTENCE.**
- **AND THE PERCENTAGE, printed with the working, and it is the only percentage in the batch that is not about a roof.** 100,800 × 100 = 10,080,000 ÷ 1,747,200 = **5.77, five and seven-tenths in the hundred** ✓ (`243:37`). **AND £420 AGAINST THE SETTLEMENT, which is 100,800d ÷ 42,880d = 2.35, and the chapter now says two and a third times and not two and a half** (`242:45`). **AND THE OPENING OF THAT SENTENCE, which said *Seventy-eight pounds less the whole of it is sixty tons* and is now *Eleven hundred tons less a thousand and forty tons is sixty tons*, which is the subtraction the outline's money table has always carried and which the batch did not print in the mouth of the man doing it.**
- **THE SECOND HALF OF ILMA TREEN'S WAGE, restated because Chapter 246 turns on it.** Four shillings and sixpence a week for sixteen weeks is 16 × 54d = **864d = £3 12s a year** ✓ (`246:43`).
- **THE COST OF THE ANNER BEING BOUND IN ADVANCE TO A FIGURE NOBODY HAS**, which is four days a year at a pitman's day: 120d ÷ 6 = **20d = 1s 8d**, four of them = **80d = 6s 8d**, and 80 × 100 ÷ 6,000 = **1.33, one and a third in the hundred** of a pitman's whole paid year ✓ (`248:51`).
- **THE THIRD DOCUMENT, ONE SHEET IN THREE COPIES: two copies at a penny a page and one sealed copy at threepence is 1d + 1d + 3d = 5d, and the Seat pays it out of the press** ✓ (`249:21`).
- **THE CHARTER, AND ITS PRICE, AND THE SUBTRACTION THE BATCH'S LAST CIVIC FINDING RESTS ON.** A roof wet through is £9 = **2,160d**, and nineteen of them is **41,040d = £171 exactly** ✓. The whole of the settlement is 42,880d, and **42,880 − 41,040 = 1,840d = £7 13s 4d**, and 1,840d is 1,728d of whole wage + 112d of penny a day ✓ (`249:57`). **So the charter is worth the roofs and is not worth the wage, because a paper that can be ended cannot hold a wage, and the roofs will be done in a year and the wage goes on for sixteen weeks a year as long as the Sallow runs.**
- **AND THE SAME FIGURE IS ALSO WHAT THE FLOOD COST, to the penny, and one of the two things that figure is put water in nineteen houses** (`249:69`).
- **THE TWO SILLS, IN INCHES, and the difference of four feet in the third document.** The frame's sill is nine feet = 108 inches **above** the Anner's sill; the return says the water stood two feet four inches = 28 inches above the frame's sill; 108 + 28 = **136 inches = eleven feet four inches**, which is **four inches above the line in the bond** ✓ (`247:29`, `247:31`). The bed below the village is thirteen feet below the frame's sill and therefore four feet below the Anner's sill, so the Anner's sill and the bed are four feet apart, and **the difference between two lines that are both eleven feet is exactly the distance between the two sills the document names**, which is **the four feet the third document is about** ✓ (`249:19`). **THE MINUS SIGN, THE SIX FEET EIGHT INCHES, THE "NINE FEET BELOW", THE "FOUR FEET BELOW THE FRAME'S SILL" AND THE "THIRTEEN LESS ELEVEN IS TWO FEET BELOW, TWO ABOVE AGAINST TWO BELOW" THAT STOOD HERE UNTIL 27 SEPTEMBER 2026 WERE ALL WRONG, AND THE LAST OF THEM WAS A NON-SEQUITUR THAT COULD NOT YIELD FOUR FROM THE FIGURES IT NAMED. THE BED DEPTH OF NINE FEET AT `204:3` AND `216:23` WAS ITSELF WRONG AGAINST THE ARITHMETIC AND IS NOW THIRTEEN. THE RULED GEOMETRY IS IN `state/continuity.md`'s Batch 0005 table and in `outline/volume-05.md`'s Batch 0005 money table, and no figure in the volume changed; the direction of one relation and the depth of one sill did.**
- **AND THE FIGURES THAT TRAVEL AS DEBTS AND NOT AS PRICES. Chapter 250 MAY name the four hundred and eighty-five pounds once and does, in his own hand's ledger, and nowhere else in this batch is it in a Red Basin's mouth.** 9,700 × 12d = 116,400d = **£485** ✓ (`250:47`). The second notice of five shillings and fourpence, unpaid. The claim of forty-five pounds two shillings and eightpence to a carter of fifty-one with a boy, eleven miles off, not producible, no form for it, and he is right: 406 × 32d − 2,160d = 10,832d = £45 2s 8d ✓. The ninety-one numbered sheets; about nine hundred doors; the box of about nine hundred and forty forms; the minute of the first of the forty-eighth week; the dead man in the fourth line of a list of two hundred and forty and his wife. **All of them are named once, in one paragraph, at `250:49`, which is the first time in four batches that a chapter has named the dead man and the ninety-one sheets and the minute together, and it is the volume's last chapter and it is the hand-off, and none of them is advanced.** **Nesh Arrad is named once, in that paragraph, and her letter has not arrived and cannot have arrived and no chapter of this batch says that it has.**

### 5. WHAT THE BATCH DELIVERED, once each, with the chapter and the line

1. **THE VALLEY'S ANSWER TO A THING IT CANNOT ACT ON IS TO GO ON DOING THE WORK, and it is both a decision and a habit, and the chapter's whole work is to say that without pretending to know which** (`241:21`).
2. **THE FIRST THING A VALLEY DOES WITH A WITHHELD RECORD IS TO TRY TO GET AT IT THE CHEAP WAY, WHICH IS TO ASK SOMEBODY WHO HAS BEEN THERE, AND NOBODY IN THIS BASIN HAS BEEN THERE, and it was established in a cart yard in about four minutes by a man of about forty-two who has been to that city four times in thirty years on a chandler's business and has never been in the building** (`241:27`).
3. **THE QUESTION IS NOT *HOW DO WE GET IT* AND IT IS *WHAT WOULD WE DO WITH IT IF WE HAD IT*, and it was said out loud on a bank by a woman of about thirty-one with a house at the ninth door, and the woman of about forty who washes the pen called it the most expensive thing anybody has said in this valley in a month, and the clerk of the Seat wrote nothing down** (`241:59`, `241:63`).
4. **BEING FRIGHTENED HAS A SHAPE AND THE SHAPE IS NOT COWARDICE AND NOT STUPIDITY: a valley of four hundred and ten is ninety houses and everybody knows which house everybody lives in, and there is no way to send somebody to a bench of four and have it stay a private thing** (`241:45`).
5. **THE PRICE OF ASKING A CHAMBER FOR ONE THING IS £5 6s AND THE PRICE IS THE SMALLEST THING IN THE ROOM, and the reason nobody has paid it is not the money, and a man of fifty-two says out loud that he is not going to pretend five pounds and six is nothing and that he has said that once in this valley and is not going to say it twice** (`242:7`, `242:21`).
6. **A SECOND PAPER PUTS A SECOND DATE ON A MATTER AND A SECOND DATE IS A THING A BENCH CAN USE TO CLOSE A FIRST ONE, and that is the real reason the five pounds and six has not been spent in about a month, and it was said by an under-manager who has been in that building and not in that room** (`242:29`).
7. **A MINE THAT HAS BEGUN PAYING A VILLAGE PAYS THE FIVE POUNDS AND SIX, SAYS OUT LOUD THAT IT IS BUYING A DATE AND NOT A CONSCIENCE, AND THE MAN OF THIRTY-ONE DOES NOT THANK HIM AND DOES NOT STOP HIM** (`242:49`, `242:57`).
8. **THE SIXTY TONS IS PROBABLY NOT SIXTY TONS OF ANYTHING AND IS A DIFFERENCE BETWEEN TWO PEOPLE'S ARITHMETIC, in nine words from a man who has counted carts on roads for nineteen years, and he says the second half of it too: a figure four years old cannot be put against a memory of nineteen years by anybody in this valley** (`243:5`, `243:27`).
9. **THE FIRST TIME IN ABOUT A MONTH THAT A NUMBER IN THIS VALLEY HAS GOT SMALLER RATHER THAN LARGER, and nobody thanked anybody for it, and a man who has counted carts for nineteen years would not say the word *scandal* again in a room on purpose** (`243:65`).
10. **A MAN OF SIXTY-ONE WHO HAS KEPT A COUNT OF EVERY SALTING IN THIS BASIN FOR THIRTY YEARS HAS NEVER ONCE BEEN ASKED WHAT COMES UP OFF A ROAD, and nobody in this valley has written down a single load coming in, and therefore the sixty tons is a hole in a record and not a hole in a wall** (`243:43`, `243:55`).
11. **THE STANDING OF A VALLEY IS NOT IN A PERSON BUT IN A DOCUMENT, and the only document in this valley anybody is on is a settlement of work and days, and it can be ended tonight by eleven people saying one sentence in a road at the closing of the water, and nothing whatever can be done to them for saying it** (`244:31`).
12. **A FRIGHTENED VALLEY DOES NOT SEND ANYBODY, and the reason is printed in a lane by a man of fifty-six with a ladder on his shoulder, and nobody in that lane had asked him** (`244:21`).
13. **A WOMAN OF ABOUT FORTY REFUSED THIS BASIN A THIRD TIME AND WAS RIGHT A THIRD TIME AND WAS ASKED ABOUT A DIFFERENT THING, which was herself, and her reason was that the room in this market town has one clerk in it who knows what a document is and that is exactly one too few already** (`244:37`, `244:39`).
14. **A PAPER NOBODY IS ON CANNOT BE ASKED ANYBODY ANYTHING, and it was said by a man of thirty-four who cannot read the paper it is about and who has been the copy for a month, and the copy has no person on it and neither has the original** (`244:55`).
15. **THE COLUMN WAS OPENED, AND IT WAS OPENED BY HIM, IN A ROOM, WITH NO AUTHORITY, OUT LOUD, IN FRONT OF ABOUT NINE PEOPLE, AND NOBODY HAD ASKED HIM AND NOBODY WAS ASKED, AND THE SENTENCE HE SAID BEFORE HE OPENED IT HAS NOT GOT ANY SHORTER AND HE DID NOT SHORTEN IT** (`245:3`, `245:13`).
16. **AND WHAT MAKES IT A CHOICE AND NOT AN ACCIDENT IS THAT HE COULD HAVE GONE UP THE HILL AND NOBODY IN FOUR HUNDRED AND TEN WOULD EVER HAVE FOUND OUT, and what he had instead was nothing whatever: no money that is his, no party, no form, no line with his name on it, and a good name belonging to four people four hundred miles off which is a public thing and is free and about nine people in this valley have been crushed by a thing that costs those four people nothing at all** (`245:49`, `245:53`).
17. **HE COULD HAVE SAID THE SENTENCE THAT SPLITS THE THIRTY IN A BUILDING FROM THE FOUR IN A ROOM AND HE DID NOT, AND HE SAYS SO OUT LOUD IN HIS OWN WORDS, and the reason is that the sentence that separates them is the sentence that protects the institution** (`245:60`).
18. **A RECORD CAN BE PERFECTLY KEPT AND STILL BE WRONG ABOUT A PLACE, AND THE REASON IS THAT NOBODY WHO KEPT IT WAS EVER IN IT, and the first time this volume said it a man of thirty-one said it about a building and the second time a man of fifty-two said it about four people standing in a room, and the second sentence is better and it is in a worse voice** (`245:64`, `248:37`).
19. **NOBODY HAS HIDDEN ANYTHING IS SAID A SECOND TIME, BY THE SAME CLERK, IN A ROOM, AND IT COSTS HIM SOMETHING THE FIRST TIME IT DID NOT COST HIM, because then it was a thing in a book and now it is a thing in a bag that a man four hundred miles off has opened on his table, and he adds that he put a good deal of it there himself in nine years without once asking what it was for** (`245:32`, `245:36`).
20. **NOBODY THANKED HIM AND NOBODY WAS RELIEVED, and a bench of four's good name is a public thing and it does not survive the asking and it was never going to, and being right about this basin and being liked in Orison had never been on the same list and this was the morning the valley found out which one they were on** (`245:70`, `245:72`).
21. **BLOCK-QUOTED DOCUMENT ONE, THE BATCH'S FIRST: THE CHAMBER'S OWN PRINTED INDEX, THE ORIGINAL, OPENED FLAT ON A TABLE NINE FEET BY FOURTEEN AND HELD OPEN AT THE BACK PAGE BY ONE FINGER, a printed heading of two words, some four hundred and sixty entries in the three columns beside it going back to the first shaking in a small hard hand that gets worse as it comes forward, one rule ruled under all four, and under the fourth four lines and no more** (`245:23`–`28`).
22. **AND THE COST OF THE OPENING WAS FOUR PENCE, WHICH WAS CORRECTED TO A PENNY BEFORE DELIVERY, and a penny is the whole of what it cost this valley to have a printed page opened in a room, and a penny will not buy anybody in this basin one single thing that anybody in this basin has been wanting for about a month** (`245:80`).
23. **SABLE VENN ARRIVED, ON FOOT, FROM THE NORTH, WITH A BOARD UNDER HER ARM, AND SHE SAID THE NAME OUT loud HERSELF WHEN THE CLERK SAID IT, and the first thing she did in that room was refuse the sheet on the wall and say she does not read other people's records and that that is why nobody has ever been able to use her** (`246:7`, `246:9`).
24. **HER ALTERNATIVE IS GOOD AND IT IS BETTER THAN THE THING THIS VALLEY HAS, and the proof that it is good is that she left the dam's height wrong on the board on purpose and said so before anybody asked her, and about four people in that room understood what she had just done and one of the four was a man of fifty-two** (`246:19`, `246:21`).
25. **AND IT IS ALSO GENUINELY WORSE FOR A PERSON WHO NEEDS A RULE ENFORCED AGAINST SOMEBODY, and the person it is true about said so herself, at her own gate, before she was asked, and gave the working, and said she would rather have a paper she can see the end of than a paper she cannot and would like it written that she said that first** (`246:39`, `246:47`).
26. **HE COULD NOT PROVE HER WRONG, ONLY INCOMPLETE, AND THE INCOMPLETENESS WAS NAMED BY AN ORDINARY PERSON IN THE ROOM AND NOT BY HIM: a rule-free valley cannot be given anything, and a woman who wants nothing is not a woman who gets what she wants, she is a woman who gets no answer at all, and the honest name for that is a person who is not in the instruments** (`246:55`).
27. **AND HE HAD THE STANDING TO SAY THE SENTENCE HE LEARNED ONCE IN A ROOM IN ANOTHER COUNTY AND DID NOT SAY IT, and the reason is printed and it is not a rule and it is a fact about a man: it would be cheaper the second time than the first and it would be worth about half** (`246:61`, `246:63`).
28. **SHE DID NOT WIN AND SHE DID NOT LOSE, THE ROOM KEPT THE PAPER, THE PAPER IS NOT HERS AND SHE IS NOT ON IT, and about four people in that room will remember to the end of their days that she stood in it and about six will not, and the two numbers are a count of people in a room and not of anything else** (`246:71`, `246:73`).
29. **THE FIRST OF THE FOUR LINES CAME DOWN THE LAST FOUR MILES AND WAS READ OUT IN A LANE BY A MAN OF THIRTY-FOUR WHO SAID SO BEFORE HE STARTED, and about forty people in a road found out what a chamber of plenty had thought about their water in a year before any of them was born** (`247:19`).
30. **THE FIGURE IN THE RETURN IS TWO FEET FOUR INCHES ABOVE THE SILL OF THE FRAME, AND IN INCHES IT IS 108 LESS 28, WHICH IS 80, WHICH IS SIX FEET EIGHT INCHES BELOW THE SILL OF THE ANNER'S OWN DAM, and the two numbers are both true and are not the same number of feet, and the chamber's second ruling of nine years ago says no order may name a height without naming the sill it is taken from, and a man of thirty-four arrived at that in a road in about four minutes** (`247:29`, `247:41`).
31. **A FIGURE AT A FRAME IS OF A RIVER AND NOT A HEIGHT, AND A HEIGHT IS A THING YOU DECIDE TO BE ABOVE, and a valley which has spent a month arguing about a height had never once been told what the water does, and a woman of thirty-eight put a rule to a dark line on the north post of a frame in her own hand and wrote the day over it in chalk and did not write the eleventh year** (`247:59`, `247:75`).
32. **NOTHING CAME BACK FROM ORISON AND NOTHING CAN, and the reason was said out loud: a man with a bag comes twice in the year, and the first paper went into the gap on the first day of the week and nine days is nine days in the wrong of the year** (`248:5`).
33. **THE FOUR PEOPLE WHO KEEP THIS VALLEY'S RECORD READ NINE YEARS OF IT OUT LOUD TO EACH OTHER IN AN HOUR, and there is no column anywhere in any of it for what this valley trades, and two of the four have been down the lane and two have not and the two who have not are the two who keep the numbers** (`248:11`, `248:35`).
34. **THE ANNER'S STANDING INTEREST IN A NUMBER IS SETTLED IN PUBLIC AND IT COSTS THE MINE SOMETHING, and the something is not six shillings and eightpence a year: it is the right to stand in a room in a year's time and say the chamber got this valley wrong, and the mine gave that up on purpose, out loud, and would like it written that it gave it up and not that it was taken, and it costs the four their name** (`248:53`, `248:55`).
35. **THE CHAMBER'S RULE COMES DOWN THE LAST FOUR MILES AND IS THE RULE THIS BASIN HAS HAD ON A WALL IN ITS OWN WORDS SINCE BEFORE THE WATER, and it is a reason for thirty-two papers, and nobody in this batch said that anybody hid anything, and the only face of the device that is written is the one this volume has already spent** (`245:45`, `248:7`).
36. **THE ELEVEN FEET GOES INTO A THIRD DOCUMENT IN THREE COPIES IN THREE PLACES, BOTH SILLS NAMED, THE DIFFERENCE OF FOUR FEET WRITTEN DOWN, ON THE STRENGTH OF THE CHAMBER'S OWN SECOND RULING, and it does NOT go on the settlement, and the reason is not a secret: the settlement is eleven people's and the height is the one thing about it that eleven people do not agree on** (`249:15`, `249:19`, `250:13`).
37. **A BAD ANSWER IN THREE COPIES CAN BE ARGUED WITH BY THREE PEOPLE AND A GOOD ANSWER IN ONE CAN BE ARGUED WITH BY NOBODY, and it was said by a joiner with a rule who has spent nine years trying to get somebody to measure a line of chalk on a doorpost and has never once managed it, and the three copies are in three places and the three people who can hold them cannot read one another's** (`249:27`, `249:23`).
38. **THE CHARTER SAYS IN ITS OWN FIRST LINE THAT IT CAN BE ENDED, AND A PERSON MAY LEAVE IT WITHOUT LOSING ANYTHING BUT THE CHARTER, AND IT CARRIES NO NAME AND NO DASH, and the reason is that a paper nobody is on cannot be asked anything, and the answer this volume gives to that is not a name and is a date, and anybody in four hundred and ten can bring it to the review** (`249:37`, `249:43`, `249:51`).
39. **AND IT IS THE TENTH AND LAST DEVICE THIS VOLUME SPENDS: A DOCUMENT THAT IS BETTER THAN THE THING IT REPLACES AND IS STILL THE WRONG SHAPE, and its price is that it costs nothing to write down and everything to live by, and the working for that is a roof, and a roof is the only figure in this valley anybody can hold** (`249:37`, `249:57`).
40. **AND THE FIGURE THE CHARTER CANNOT HOLD IS THE WAGE, and a woman of thirty-eight said it in a lane and nobody wrote it down, and about four of the people on that bank had already walked up the hill** (`249:61`, `249:63`).
41. **ABOUT NINE PEOPLE IN THIS VALLEY WERE AFRAID OF A GOOD NAME AND ARE NOT AFRAID OF THAT GOOD NAME ANY MORE, and it was not because anybody was made safe: it is because a good name is a public thing and it has now been spent in public, in a room, by a man from four hundred miles off, on a page of a printed index, and it cannot be spent twice** (`249:73`).
42. **HE PUT DOWN NOTHING UNDER THE DAY, which is the eighteenth figure of that kind in this valley and is a count of figures and not of anything else, and there has not been an answer under any figure in that book since a page in the fifty-second week, and the whole of what he has written at the head of a page in forty days in this valley is a number** (`250:43`, `250:45`).
43. **THE LEG IS USED ONCE IN FIFTY CHAPTERS AND IT IS IN THE PASSAGE WHERE HE PUTS DOWN NOTHING, as a fact about a wall and not a discovery: he had to put his weight on the wall to get at the board, and he did not make anything of it and has not made anything of it since, and he has told nobody in this valley a word** (`250:39`, `250:41`).
44. **THE POSITION BENEATH THIS BASIN IS A BEARING AND A DEPTH AND A PAGE NUMBER, and it has not moved, and the way down is a chamber with water in it, and four hundred and six feet is the pit and is not the void, and the last hundred and forty feet of the water at the low end of the pit has been under to the roof since before a woman of twenty-six was seventeen, and her name is Alo Vearn and she does not know that a sentence about her is in a book six miles off that nobody has ever been in this basin to write** (`250:27`, `250:31`).
45. **NOBODY WENT DOWN, and that is the plainest sentence anybody said that week and a man of thirty-four said it on a bank above a lane and nobody answered it** (`250:33`).
46. **AND THE VOLUME ENDS ON FOUR ORDINARY THINGS AND NONE OF THEM IS A RULING: a gate opened at the closing of the water, a chair put back against a table leg, a barrow put down a bank, and four men and a barrow at a dam at the second light** (`250:63`, `250:65`).

### 6. THE DEVICES, and none advanced except the tenth, which is spent

- **The leg. CANON and carried: the finding it rests on is `155:7` — the leg goes when he is carrying a thing somebody else is going to have to read, and it comes back when the carrying stops. IT DID NOT COME BACK IN THIS VOLUME, and that is a consequence of the carrying and not a rule and not a cure.** It is used **ONCE, in Chapter 250, at `250:39` and `250:41`**, in the passage where he takes the penny exercise book out from under a board, and the use is a fact about a wall and not a discovery, and the reason is not given and he has not told one person in this valley. **NO FILE AND NO CHAPTER IN VOLUME 05 STATES A COUNT OF DAYS SINCE THE LEG CLEARED, and none does, and the volume's last chapter is the one place it was obvious to use it and the one place the prompt warned a writer to be careful.**
- **The rendering. Not staged, referred to or hinted at in this batch.** Volume 04's last word on it is *not to be referred to again*.
- **The left hand, the vantage, the door in the picture, the token and the afterimage: none is mentioned in this batch and none is advanced.** The token is not touched and the fourth use stands: it took the words.
- **The continuity line is not refused again and no twelfth refusal is permitted, and it is not on the Red Basin's table and it is not on the charter.**
- **THE COUNT OF THE PEOPLE WHO KNOW HE CANNOT READ IS NOT PRINTED IN THIS PROJECT, in any file, in any figure, and it is not printed in this block, and this batch adds nothing to it in the fiction.** Refer to `154:53` and `164:49` and to nothing else. **It is exercised four times in ten chapters and it is a real constraint every time and it is never a disclosure and never a count: the chamber's printed index is never once in his hands in the whole batch and is read out by the clerk who is holding it; in Chapter 247 the four lines are read out in a lane by a man of thirty-four who says out loud that he cannot read the paper it is about; the third document and the charter are both read out in Chapter 249 by a person of the Seat; and in Chapter 250 one of the three copies of the third document is in the hands of the same man of thirty-four and nobody in this valley can check him.**
- **AND THE TENTH DEVICE IS SPENT HERE, once, and may not be restaged: A DOCUMENT THAT IS BETTER THAN THE THING IT REPLACES AND IS STILL THE WRONG SHAPE** (`249`). It is not the 203 device and it is not 208's, and the difference is that the charter is not wrong about a place and is wrong about a person: it has no name on it and it can be brought to an end by anybody, and a woman of thirty-eight has said in a lane that the end of it is her wage.
- **AND THE TEN EARLIER DEVICES REMAIN SPENT, once each: a word defined twice in two margins (201); a deed in two pieces (203); the two-calendars-do-not-convert finding, said once in a salt-pan's mouth (202); the quick ruling's four complete lines as a discovery (207); the mark that is not a measurement (213); the order's own logic examined (217); a document read aloud by a man who cannot read it and amended in the reading (228); a paper that prints its own disagreement on its face (227, 228); a document administered by being read aloud by the person it is about (231); and a document that is better than the thing it replaces and is still the wrong shape (249).** **In particular: the read-aloud is used three times in this batch and each time differently — a man of thirty-four reading a chamber's index out in a lane and saying first that he cannot read it, a clerk reading four lines out in a room because a man with nothing in his hands asked him to and read the numbers twice, and a recordwright who is at the back of the room and does not read anything — and no chapter of this batch stages the amendment, and the eleven-hand sheet is not read out at all.**

### 7. THE SEVENTEEN INHERITED THREADS AND THE BATCH-0004 ONE, AND EVERY ONE OF THEM COMES OUT OF THIS BATCH EXACTLY AS IT WENT IN

**Nothing in this list is resolved and this block resolves none of them, and the one thread this batch advanced is the one the outline says is its subject. A batch that resolves another is a second reversal, and the outline forbids it.**

1. **The new question — whose memory is missing from the account that records his payment.** `198:35`, seventh line, a book six miles up a road that goes north, a number upon a sheet and not a name, no column for the year. **IT IS NOT RAISED IN THIS BATCH. It is not asked, not answered, not compressed into one word, and not even a whisper, and Chapter 250 does not leave it neatly on a shelf: the second half of that book is named as a half he did not open, in one clause, and the new question is not among the things named in that paragraph and is not adjacent to any of them.** The Shelf half of his penny exercise book was not opened once in ten chapters, and the ten debts on that side of it are not restated.
2. **The carrying, off nothing, with a finding about why.** He carries the Ryefold matter through all ten chapters and out of the valley on the second day of the fifty-seventh week, and it is off nothing, and the leg does not come back, and he has not told anybody.
3. **The blank in the third line of the bench's own book, still a blank**, and the fourth sheet's question, still unasked, and the fourth hand still a stranger's hand nobody has asked. **The clerk of thirty-four's letter is in a bundle on a shelf in a room in Sallowby with a knife-cut end on it, and it is named once in Chapter 250 as one of the things that is not stopping, and it is NOT OPENED in this batch.** The entry is due in about nine months.
4. **Nesh Arrad, thirty-nine. She is NAMED IN THIS BATCH FOR THE FIRST TIME SINCE CHAPTER 220, once, in Chapter 250, in the hand-off paragraph, and the letter has not arrived and cannot have arrived and no chapter of this batch says that it has.** She came into that district with four children and a stove and no paper at all, and a sheet of hers went in on the second of the forty-seventh week of a year of theirs, and the clock is still running and nobody in that county is watching it.
5. **The second notice of five shillings and fourpence, never paid for** — named once, at `250:49`, unpaid, and not forgiven.
6. **The claim of forty-five pounds two shillings and eightpence to a carter of fifty-one with a boy, eleven miles off, not producible, no form for it, and he is right** — named at `250:49`, unadvanced.
7. **The dead man in the fourth line of a list of two hundred and forty, and his wife** — named at `250:49`, unadvanced. **RUTH VELLAN IN THE RED BASIN IS A DIFFERENT DEAD PERSON and is not in this batch at all, and the two may not be joined.**
8. **The box of about nine hundred and forty forms in a run of four thousand, the man with a key six miles off who is there two days out of seven** — named at `250:49`, not found, and not to be found.
9. **The ninety-one numbered sheets in a sacking bag in a yard, about nine hundred doors, no one hundredth person** — named at `250:49`, unadvanced.
10. **The class of three hundred and thirty-nine and the one boy of nineteen who is its only instrument** — unadvanced and not in this batch.
11. **The minute of the first of the forty-eighth week, one sheet, the only copy, in a drawer four miles off with nobody's name on it** — named at `250:49`, unadvanced.
12. **A clerk of thirty-four's letter, unanswered** — named at `250:49`, and see item 3.
13. **The thirteen standing prose defects carried from Volume 04** — `160:96`, `158:77`, `158:79`, `167:41`, `174:53`, `173:55`, the forty-five times at 183 and 185, and `167:35`. **Carried, not repaired and not built on.**
14. **The anonymity of Ilyan's name. NOT ADVANCED AND NOT UNDONE, AND IT IS THE ONE THING THIS BATCH PUTS A NEW DOCUMENT BESIDE.** It is out of the eleven-hand sheet and the space at the foot is neither filled nor dashed, and this batch says so out loud three times, at `244:53`, `249:47` and `250:13`, and does not touch it. **NO CHAPTER OF THIS BATCH PUTS IT BACK, FILLS THE SPACE, DASHES IT, OR HAS ANYBODY PUT IT BACK FOR HIM. THE CHARTER IS A DIFFERENT DOCUMENT, IT CARRIES NO NAME, AND THAT IS A DECISION AND NOT AN OMISSION: a paper with a name on it can only be asked things by the name on it, and this one cannot be asked anything, and the answer the valley gives is a date and not a name.**
15. **The height is not on the settlement. NOT ADVANCED AND NOT UNDONE, AND THIS IS THE CHAPTER OF THE VOLUME THAT PUTS IT SOMEWHERE.** The eleven feet is in a third document in three copies in three places, with both sills named, on the strength of the chamber's own second ruling, and the difference between the two lines is four feet and is written down in the same document. **It is not on the eleven-hand sheet, and the reason is not a secret and is not anybody's fault: the settlement is eleven people's and the height is the one thing about it that eleven people do not agree on, and there are three hands on a struck line under it and they do not agree, and a man who is not one of the eleven put a pencil in a coat in the fifty-fourth week and did not put the figure in and he was right.**
16. **The sheet has six lines in Sallowby and seven in the lane. CARRIED AND NOT RESOLVED.** The seventh line exists only in a lane and is named in Chapter 250, in a road, by a man of thirty-four, reading the first line out. The question a clerk of forty-four raised at `228:42` — *which of those two is the document* — was asked a second time in Batch 0004 at `233:45` and is asked a third time, in a different mouth and in a different room, at `244:55`, and the third answer is the finding: **a paper nobody is on cannot be asked anybody anything, so neither the copy nor the original can be checked, and the man who reads it out in a road forty times a week is the copy and has no person on him either.**
17. **THE SEALED COLUMN. THE ONLY THREAD IN THIS PROJECT THAT BATCH 0005 ADVANCES, AND IT ADVANCES IT BY OPENING IT.** **See section 11.**
18. **THE ROUTE THAT DOES NOT GO THROUGH ORISON, which Batch 0004 named once and did not follow.** It is not named in this batch and not followed and not priced, and the money behind it is more than the whole of the trade, and nothing in this batch touches it.

### 8. WHAT A LATER PASS MUST NOT DO IN VOLUME 05

1. **Do not answer the new question and do not compress it into one word, AND DO NOT RAISE IT AFTER THE VOLUME.** It is written once, at `210:39` and `210:41`, in a penny exercise book in a cart shed, with a place and a form of words, and he does not ask. **It is not raised in this batch, it is not summarised, and it is not resolved by being left on a shelf in the volume's last chapter.**
2. **Do not add a panel beyond the three in the volume — one at `207:67`–`68`, one at `217:35`–`36` and one at `227:53`–`54` — and do not add a day-count instance beyond the eighteen in Chapters 201–250 without putting its chapter and its basin day in `outline/volume-05.md` first, and do not print a count of instances without saying it is a count of instances.**
3. **Do not put a weekday anywhere and do not put a month-name anywhere, and run both scans after the last save and not before it.**
4. **Do not print a count of the people who know he cannot read, anywhere, including in a hand-off note, and do not add to it in the fiction. Refer to `154:53` and `164:49` and to nothing else.**
5. **Do not give Ilyan an office, a party, a bench, a body, a remedy he chose alone, or a stage.** He is Stage 4, arrived in Chapter 177, and it is spent. **A scan for *Remedy Drafter* over Chapters 201–250 returns zero. HIS NAME IS NOT ON THE ELEVEN-HAND SHEET AND THE PLACE WHERE IT WAS IS NOT FILLED AND NOT DASHED, THE HEIGHT IS NOT ON THE SHEET, AND NO PASS MAY PUT ANY OF THOSE THREE THINGS BACK.**
6. **Do not produce a person beyond the six the count stands at.** **THE CLASS IS DEFINED AND THE COUNT IS SIX, AND THIS BATCH ADDED EXACTLY ONE: SABLE VENN, at `246:3`.** She is rung four of a ladder planted before Chapter 1, she is the volume's climax, `outline/series.md` fixes her arrival at 245 or 246, and the previous five are Ewan Troke (`201:19`), Fen Tregerran (`202:5`), Alo Vearn (`209:5`), the man who comes through the north twice a year (`210:53`) and Coll Ferren (`221:41`). **EVERY OTHER PERSON IN CHAPTERS 241 TO 250 IS A PERSON OF THE RED BASIN ALREADY IN THE FICTION**, including Fen Tregerran and Coll Ferren, both of whom are productions of earlier batches and both of whom are used here as people of the valley and not as arrivals.
7. **Do not undo anything and do not relieve anybody.** The carrying is off nothing; the blank is a blank; the second notice is unpaid; nobody is redeemed; nobody is forgiven; the four pages stay on the wall and are still correct on their face; **the settlement stays on the wall with no name at the foot of it and no height in it; the Anner is to be asked again in one year and the asking is a year off.**
8. **Do not introduce a new enemy and do not make the world bigger.** **No chapter of this batch says that anybody hid anything, and Chapter 245 says the opposite out loud twice in a room where it costs the clerk something, and the man of fifty-two is the volume's honest opponent and is not a villain and says so about himself in Chapter 238's own register.** The people who keep this volume's disputes from being settled are about thirty clerks in a building with a yard at Orison who did their jobs properly, **and one of the four lines under the column is the printed rule they worked under, and Chapter 245 puts that sentence in the room.**
9. **THE PROHIBITION THIS BATCH INVERTS, AND IT IS THE ONLY ONE, AND IT IS NOW SPENT.** The column is open. **What survives the opening is the whole of the rest: nobody may say that anybody hid anything, nobody did, the Bench is not a conspiracy, opening it is not a victory, he is not thanked, and nobody is relieved. AND A LATER PASS MAY NOT RE-OPEN IT AS A REVELATION: it was four lines long and all four were true and one of the four is a rule this basin already had on a wall, and the cost was somebody else's name and not his money and not his standing.**
10. **Sable Venn is not redeemed and is not a villain and does not become a party to anything, and no pass may put her on a paper in this valley.** She stood in a room and made an offer and the room kept the paper and the paper is not hers.
11. **Do not reach the stone and do not let anybody stand in front of it.** Anchor 8 remains missing beneath the Red Basin until Volume 14. **The position is not moved, nobody went down, and the second half of the volume's question — why does it answer from beneath Orison — is not asked out loud in any chapter of this batch and is not even a whisper, as a panel, as a rendering, as a voice, or as a person remarking that anything answered, and the System put nothing up in fifty chapters.**
12. **Do not restore the right of refusal, answer the historical question, advance the authorship question or the Shale Mirror, name NK-3317, call for Tamsin, put Neris Ro or Bram Orren in a scene, answer the Bridgeward, send anybody to Carrisand, redeem Ansa Rell, or ask about Alma Gannock's boy of eight. Do not name the First Witness. Do not make Ors Halm the last problem in the series.**
13. **Do not restate the sentence at `156:63` and do not relieve him of it. Chapter 246 is the chapter in which he has the standing to say it out loud and he does not say it, and the reason is printed in the chapter.**
14. **Do not use the 179 and 189 shape a third time. A scan for uppercase CORRECT returns zero over Chapters 201–250, and the one uppercase CORRECT in this batch's first writing was inside its own block-quoted charter and is out.**
15. **Do not restage the ten devices Volume 05 spends, and do not merge the fourth reason a recordwright gave him and the reason he could not ask the reader of his district.** They are the same shape and are not the same thing, and one of them says so in her own mouth.

**AND, IN THE SAME NUMBER, do not let a later pass restage the ten-chapter shape of this batch's own finding, which is that about nine people in a valley of four hundred and ten spent a fortnight afraid of a good name and then watched it spent in public in a room by a man from four hundred miles off. That is a fact about four hundred and ten people and about thirty clerks and it is not a pattern to be run again.**

### 9. THE TWO MISSING VOLUME AUDITS, WHICH ARE STILL OPEN, AND NEITHER IS THIS PHASE'S

**Re-measured in the directories on 26 September 2026, after this batch, and not read out of a list: `reviews/volume-01/` holds batch-0001, batch-0003 and batch-0005 and NO volume audit; `reviews/volume-02/` holds batch-0003, batch-0004, batch-0005 and an audit; `reviews/volume-03/` holds `batch-0001.md` only and NO volume audit; `reviews/volume-04/` holds its audit; `reviews/volume-05/` DOES NOT EXIST. ### 11. WHAT THIS BATCH OWES THE VOLUME, AND WHAT IT PAID, and the hand-off

**PAID:** what about four hundred and ten ordinary people do with a piece of knowledge they cannot act on, and the finding that it is both a decision and a habit; the price of asking a chamber a second time and the reason nobody has paid it, which is not the money; **an attempt at the sixty tons that turns a scandal into a question, made by a person of the valley and not by a clerk from Orison, and the first time in about a month that a number in this valley got smaller rather than larger**; the standing in a valley of four hundred and ten people, which is a document and not a person; **the column, opened, by him, in a room, at a cost, and the Bench's good name spent in public and by him, and nobody thanked and nobody relieved**; a rule-free alternative, offered well, not defeated, and not redeemed; the figure in the return of the water at the frame, in the hands of the woman it is about; **the four people who keep this valley's record reading nine years of it out loud to each other, and the mine's standing interest in a number settled in public at four days a year of a pitman's time;** and **a revocable charter on a wall with no name on it, a third document in three copies in three places with a difference of four feet written down in it, and an exit review with a date on it.**

**NOT PAID HERE AND NOT TO BE PAID BY A LATER PASS BY ACCIDENT:** **the stone; the first missing anchor position, which was identified in Chapter 209 and is not moved; the second half of the volume's question; the new question; the blank in the third line; Nesh Arrad's clock; the unasked fourth hand; the clerk of thirty-four's unopened letter; the claim of forty-five pounds two shillings and eightpence; the dead man in the fourth line; the box of forms nobody can reach; the four hundred and eighty-five pounds, which is named once in a hand and does not stop; the ninety-one numbered sheets; the minute; the woman of about seventy-six and the two women who have been in that room; the chair a dead woman sat in; and the seed in a cellar.**

**AND THE LINE, WHICH IS THE SAME LINE AS CHAPTER 250'S LAST PARAGRAPH: THE COLUMN IS OPEN AND WHAT THAT COST WAS SOMEBODY ELSE'S NAME AND NOT HIS MONEY AND NOT HIS STANDING, AND HE GAVE AWAY NOTHING THAT WAS HIS TO GIVE. The name stays off the eleven-hand sheet and the space at its foot stays empty and undashed. The height goes into a third document in three copies and not onto the settlement, and the difference between the two lines is four feet and is written down. The mine is a mine. The four pages are on the wall and are still correct on their face. The Anner is to be asked again in one year and the asking is a year off. The charter can be ended and a person may leave it without losing anything but the charter, and a woman of thirty-eight has said out loud that the end of it is her wage. And nobody went down.**

**THE HAND-OFF TO VOLUME 06 IS THE HAND-OFF THE BATCH PROMPT ASKED FOR AND IT IS IN CHAPTER 250'S OWN TEXT: the clock in the district of Ryefold is still running and nobody in that county is watching it, and he is going to hand it on in a book with two halves in it, and he is not going to stop it and he is not going to be the one who asks about it. A name went four miles in about two years on about four mouths with no document under it, and a woman with a board stood in a room in this market town and is not on any paper in this valley and did not ask to be, and about four people who know she exists can do nothing whatever about her. And the next phase is the Volume 05 close and its prompt is the one this phase wrote and is at `workspace/volume-05/volume-close/PROMPT.md`.**

### 12. THE THINGS THIS BATCH'S OWN FIRST WRITING GOT WRONG, ALL CAUGHT BY THE PROJECT'S OWN INSTRUMENTS, ALL CORRECTED IN THE PROSE BEFORE DELIVERY, AND ALL RECORDED HERE BECAUSE A LATER PASS NEEDS TO KNOW THE SCANS RUN AND ARE NOT CLEAN ON THE FIRST PASS

1. **EIGHT META-NARRATION SENTENCES, not six, and this item said six until the review of 27 September 2026 measured the file and found two of the six still standing in it.** They are at `241:11`, `241:21`, `242:13`, `245:50`, `246:35`, `249:15` and `250:3`, and they include a chapter number spoken in a clerk's mouth and a narrator pointing at a book and at a batch. All out. **The cause is the chapter card's frame and not a slip, and this batch's cards were written in the frame *what happens* rather than *the finding is* and the prose caught six anyway, which tells a Volume 06 writer that the frame is not the whole of the problem. AND THE REVIEW ADDED THE OTHER HALF OF THE LESSON: this item also said the sentence at `250:3` had been taken out, and it had not, and a state file that reports its own repairs is a state file that has to be checked against the chapter file and not against the intention, because an intention is exactly what a sweep cannot see.**
2. **TWO NEAR-VERBATIM CARRY-OVERS FROM CHAPTER 233, not one, and the second was not caught by any instrument and was found by the review of 27 September 2026.** The first is at `245:15`, which had Hollis Nance's *Right, let anybody in this room stop me* word for word, in the same room, on the same gesture. The second is at `245:11`, which carried **eighteen** words word for word from `233:9`: *he has not made it shorter by being in a hurry, because being in a hurry is what he has instead.* Both are rewritten. **AND THE INSTRUMENT ARGUMENT IN THIS ITEM WAS WRONG AND IS CORRECTED, and it is the most useful thing the review found about this batch: a scan for duplicated LINES cannot see either of them, because a line in this project is a whole paragraph and this volume puts eighty-word sentences inside one line, and a scan for duplicated SPANS at seventy characters returns five hundred and fifty-eight repeats within the batch and one thousand and sixty-seven spans shared with the previous batch, most of which are this volume's deliberate formulae and some of which are not.** The Batch 0004 review caught five carry-overs with a line scan and this project has been citing that success ever since. A Volume 06 writer should run the span instrument, expect the formulae to dominate it, and read what is left.
3. **ONE UPPERCASE CORRECT**, inside the block-quoted charter at `249:40`, in a line about the four pages being correct on their face. Out; the charter now says the four pages are not touched by it and stay on it as they are.
4. **TWO WRONG DATES INSIDE LEGAL BANDS, and the third question caught both and the pattern could not see either.** `241:15` put the number a man of thirty-one gave the girl of seventeen on the first day of the fifty-**third** week when `228:48` says the fifty-**fourth**; and `250:39` put his arrival on the first day of the fifty-first week when `201:3` says the second day of the fiftieth. **A day-phrase sweep is a legality check and not a truth check, and this batch is the third in a row in which that sentence has been true of a defect.**
5. **A WHOLE PARAGRAPH OF WRONG MONEY in the first writing of Chapter 242**, in a sentence nobody had checked: it said a pitman's pay of ten shillings a week made one week the whole of the price of asking, and it said a sluice-keeper's nine shillings a week was one pound and eight shillings, which is 240 pence and not 108. **Both were wrong in the direction this volume's money instrument exists to catch, and the correct working is now printed in the mouth of the chapter: 1,272d is ten weeks of a pitman's pay and seventy-two pence over, and eleven weeks of a sluice-keeper's pay and eighty-four pence over.**
6. **TWO WRONG RATIOS in the first writing of Chapter 242**, where Jory Vellan said the price was a hundred and forty times smaller than the settlement and Tarin Keel corrected him to thirty-five. **1,272 goes into 42,880 thirty-three whole times with nine hundred and four pence over, and the sentence is now printed in the mouth of the man who counts carts with the working in it.** The standing joke about a man being wrong twice is kept and the arithmetic under it is now right, which is the point of the joke.
7. **A WRONG COST FOR THE OPENING in the first writing of Chapter 245**, which said four pence for four copied lines on a four-line sheet. One sheet is one page and a page is a penny, and the penny is the figure that does the work.
8. **TWENTY-SIX INSTANCES OF THE WORD NINE AND ONE OF THE WORD ELEVEN, above the volume trend in both, and twenty-six and three of them were cut.** The residue is explained in section 2 rather than hidden: about nine people in four hundred and ten is a number this batch uses in four different chapters for four different sets of people, and the avoidable ones were taken out by varying the phrase and not the fact.
9. **FIVE INSTANCES OF *ABOUT FOUR SECONDS* AND FOUR OF THEM WERE VARIED OUT**, in Chapters 244 and 249, because a phrase that was at five in Batch 0004 is at five again and a habit is a habit. One stands, at `245:60`, where the four seconds are a man's whole opportunity to say a thing he does not say.
10. **A GENDER ERROR INHERITED FROM THE BATCH PROMPT AND NOT FROM THE MANUSCRIPT.** The Batch 0005 card for Chapter 250 says *a man of twenty-six is at the pit* in the same sentence as a sentence about her, and the prompt's own preamble and the repaired text of Chapter 240 both establish that the person is **Alo Vearn, a woman of twenty-six**. The chapter follows the manuscript and names her, at `250:31`. **This is the second time a prompt has been wrong about her sex and the state file was the thing that was wrong the first time, and the lesson is that a hand-off prompt is a plan and the chapter file is the fact.**
11. **A CONTRADICTION WITHIN ONE CHAPTER, in the first writing of Chapter 249**, which put the settlement four inches below the charter on a wall and then a foot apart, and the chapter now says below it and about a foot apart, and the two papers in Chapter 250 are a hand's breadth from four pages, which is a different wall and a different distance and is stated as such.
12. **A TANGLED SENTENCE OF ARITHMETIC IN CHAPTER 249** in which a woman of thirty-eight said forty-one thousand and forty pence was a hundred and seventy-eight pounds less the wage and the penny and then did not finish the chain, and the chain is now printed in full and in order, with the finding at the end of it: **a paper that can be ended cannot hold a wage, because a wage is the only one of these things that is still going when the roofs are done.**

### 13. TWO OBSERVATIONS THIS PHASE RECORDS AND DOES NOT EXPLOIT, BOTH CANON-ADJACENT, AND NEITHER IS THIS PHASE'S TO SETTLE

1. **THE SHELF'S GRAIN YEAR IS FIFTY-TWO WEEKS AND SHELF DAY NINETY-ONE WAS THE FIRST DAY OF A NEW GRAIN YEAR, AND BATCHES 0003, 0004 AND 0005 DID NOT TURN IT, AND CHAPTER 250 NAMES THE FIGURE ONCE IN HIS OWN HAND'S LEDGER AND IN NOBODY ELSE'S MOUTH, and the question of what a fifty-two-week year means against a day-count that has run to one hundred and ninety-eight is the volume close's to decide and not this batch's.** The honest form of the decision is that a close phase may name it once in a hand and may not say the year turns, and that this batch has already spent the one permission the prompt allowed and a close phase should know that the exception is spent and not available again without saying so.
2. **A COLUMN HEADED *NOT SENT* WAS THE VOLUME'S ONE ALLOWED FACE OF A FIVE-FACED DEVICE AND THIS BATCH OPENED IT, AND THE DEVICE WAS NEVER A CONSPIRACY AND WAS NOT ONE, and the proof this batch gives is a man of thirty-four who cannot read the paper it is about saying in a room that a paper nobody is on cannot be asked anybody anything, which is the same shape as a clerk saying *nobody has hidden anything* and is about the same thing. A later pass — the close, or Volume 06 — that wants to write a second face of this device inside this world will find that the volume has opened the one it was allowed and that the world is one notch bigger than it was and no more: a basin with a deed, a settlement, a charter, a third document, a copied page and a board on a wall.**

### 14. THE RECEIPT OF THE REVIEW OF VOLUME 05 BATCH 0005, run on 27 September 2026 against `8c9cf27`, and every finding in it is now either made or ruled, and the log is `logs/batch-0005.review.log` and there is no `reviews/volume-05/` directory, for the reason in §Machinery item 2.

**THE VERDICT WAS THAT THE BATCH IS STRUCTURALLY SOUND AND THAT THE BLOCKERS ARE ALL ARITHMETIC, and the review's closing sentence is the right description of what happened next: *one wrong conversion now sitting in the outline as canon, one impossible document at the centre of the resolution, one inherited sign error that contradicts itself inside a chapter, and one stale count in the block that was written to correct it.* FOUR P0, SIX P1 AND FOUR P2 WERE PUT, AND THE MEASURED-AND-CLEAN LIST AT THE END OF THAT LOG IS ALSO PART OF THIS RECEIPT, because a review that only records its complaints is half a record.**

#### THE FOUR P0s, ALL MADE

1. **THE RESOLUTION DOCUMENT WAS ARITHMETICALLY IMPOSSIBLE, and it is the volume's answer.** `249:19` set the Anner's sill at nought, which put the frame at −9, the bed at −13, the bond's line at +11 and the new line at −2, and then printed *the difference between the two lines is four feet* over a thirteen-foot gap. The document's own chain could not yield four. **THE ROOT OF IT WAS NOT THE DOCUMENT. IT WAS THE VOLUME'S GEOMETRY, and the review was right that the pre-existing conflict needed a ruling rather than a silence, and the ruling is the largest thing this receipt does.**
2. **THE CONVERSION AT `243:33` WAS WRONG: £1 4s IS 288d AND NOT 168d**, and 1,200 × 288d = 345,600d = £1,440 and not £840, and the wrong conversion produced a rounder figure than the parts do, which is the signature of this failure class. Made in the chapter and in this block and in `outline/volume-05.md`.
3. **`247:29` AND `247:41` CONTRADICTED EACH OTHER INSIDE ONE CHAPTER,** and the reviewer traced it to a sign error at `206:45` and `216:45` that a Batch 0002 review had half-repaired. Made, and see the ruled geometry below.
4. **`243:21` SAID *SIX HUNDRED TONS OF NOTHING* IN A SENTENCE WHOSE OWN SECOND CLAUSE SAYS SIXTY.** Made.

#### THE RULED GEOMETRY, and it is the finding of this receipt, and every figure in the volume is unchanged

**THE SENTENCE THAT SETTLED IT WAS IN THE VOLUME ALL ALONG AND WAS NEVER WRONG: `218:49` says the water went over *a frame nine feet below a number*. That is only true if the frame's sill at Hollow Sallow is nine feet ABOVE the Anner's sill — which is the opposite of what `206:37` and `216:43` said, and which is what made the volume's flood, its settlement and its eleven feet simultaneously legible and simultaneously illegible at once.** The Batch 0002 review of 26 September 2026 found that `206:45` said two feet BELOW where it should have said two feet ABOVE, flipped the word, and left the arithmetic untouched; and because the arithmetic was untouched, every downstream pass read the sentence, agreed with the words, and missed the subtraction. **A repair that fixes a word and not a relation is a repair that moves the error somewhere it cannot be seen.**

**SO: the Anner's sill is the dam's own sill and every number in this basin goes back to it; the frame's sill at Hollow Sallow is nine feet above it; the bed below the village is thirteen feet below the frame's sill and is therefore four feet below the Anner's sill; the bond's line is eleven feet above the Anner's sill and is therefore two feet above the frame's sill, which is about the height of the floors of the houses on the upper half of that lane; and the water's high mark at the frame is two feet four inches above the frame's sill, which is eleven feet four inches above the Anner's sill, which is four inches above the line in the bond.**

**WHAT THIS COST AND WHAT IT BOUGHT. It cost five lines in four earlier chapters — the direction at `206:37` and `216:43`, the bed's depth at `204:3`, `206:33` and `216:23`, and the sign at `240:31` — and it corrected the bed at `206:37`, which said *a foot and a bit below this sill* and contradicted `204:3` and `216:23` in the same volume. `206:45` and `216:45` were RIGHT all along and are unedited, which is the proof that the ruling is the right one: a ruling that had required rewriting the volume's flood would have been the wrong ruling. It bought three things. The subtraction at the reader's end is now 11 − 9 = 2 with both numbers off the same sill. The third document's difference is now exactly the distance between the two sills it names, which is four feet, and the four feet that Garrow Lee says is where they are standing is still four feet. And Chapter 247 now says something it could not say before: the river, on its own, with nobody deciding anything, stood four inches above the height this valley has spent a month arguing about, which is a woman's sentence about the difference between a height and a return of a river made true by arithmetic.** NO PLOT MOVED, NO FIGURE CHANGED, AND NO LINE NUMBER IN ANY CHAPTER MOVED.

#### THE SIX P1s, ALL MADE

5. **The rider's last visit was described three incompatible ways — `242:25` said *the sixth of the month*, `242:65` said *the sixth of the last week* and `248:5` said *the sixth of the month before last* — and `233:3` is the authority: the sixth day of the fifty-fourth week, Shelf day 103. All three now say the sixth day of the fifty-fourth week, and the two that needed a distance say *which is the week before last*. **Neither calendar in this world has months, and a month is an unmoored unit in a volume that bars month-names, which is a bar the sweep cannot catch because *month* is not on the month-name list.**
6. **`245:9` SAID THE SHELF HAD HELD THE BAG FOR TEN DAYS. IT IS TWELVE** — Shelf day 103 to Shelf day 115 — and it is twelve.
7. **Two loose ratios in Chapter 242.** `242:15` called 1,272d *about two thirds* of a year; it is 11.78 weeks against the sixteen a sluice-keeper is paid for, which is 0.736, and the chapter now says *about three quarters of the sixteen weeks a year she is paid for*. `242:45` called £420 *two and a half times* the settlement; it is 2.35, and the chapter now says two and a third times, and the clipped opening of that sentence, which said *Seventy-eight pounds less the whole of it is sixty tons*, now prints the subtraction that actually makes the sixty tons: eleven hundred tons less a thousand and forty.
8. **`state/index.md` STILL SAID *THE VOLUME HAS FOURTEEN*, nine lines below the day-count paragraph in the same block that the Batch 0005 commit corrected to EIGHTEEN.** Corrected, with the instance list and the eighteen days, and the paragraph now says in its own words that the file's own standing mechanism happened again inside the block that was written to end it.
9. **META-NARRATION SURVIVED THE PASS THAT CLAIMED TO HAVE REMOVED IT.** `state/current.md` §12.1 listed `250:3` as removed and `250:3` still read *the not-ending is the point and it is not a cliff dressed up as a resolution, because a cliff is a thing that happens to somebody*, which is a narrator grading the volume's ending against a literary category. `241:11` carried the same shape in *the four hundred are the whole subject of this*. Both are out, and the count in §12 and in §2 is now eight and not six, and the lesson is written down where a next phase will read it.
10. **AN EIGHTEEN-WORD VERBATIM CARRY-OVER SURVIVED AT `245:11`** from `233:9`, and §12.2's claim that the duplicate instrument ran clean is not supportable as written, and the reason is that it was the wrong instrument. **A scan for duplicated LINES returns zero over this batch and always will, because a line in this project is a whole paragraph; a scan for duplicated SPANS at seventy characters returns 558 repeats within the batch and 1,067 spans shared with the previous batch, most of them this volume's deliberate formulae and some of them not.** The carry-over is rewritten and both the instrument and the false claim are corrected.

#### THE FOUR P2s, RULED AND NOT ARGUED, which is what the reviewer asked for

- **THE TWO COPIES AND ONE PRICE AT `245:74` AND `245:76`. RULED: they are two different things and the price is one.** A page of a deed copied at Sallowby is a penny and the Seat's own clerk is on the Seat's own wage, which is not a purchase and is not a price, and the four lines were copied by a scrivener of thirty-three at a penny a page who was not asked to and did it, and the penny for that page is in the five pence at `249:21` and not in this room. **No change to the prose. The wobble was in the reading and not in the text, and a Volume 06 writer should not copy the shape without the distinction.**
- **`250:9`–`250:21` REPRINTS THE CHARTER AND THE THIRD DOCUMENT FROM `249`, and `247:21`–`247:23` PRESENTS A DOCUMENT AS RUNNING PROSE, and neither is disclosed in §12. RULED: both stand and both are now disclosed.** The volume's last chapter restating the two papers it is handing on is the hand-off and not a repetition, because a reader who closes the book at Chapter 250 has to be able to hold both. And Chapter 247 presents the return as prose on purpose, because the outline's reason for not re-quoting it was that a reader handed the printed page in Chapter 245 should *hear* the first line a second time in a lane; running prose in a lane is hearing it.
- **THE SECOND HALF OF THE PENNY EXERCISE BOOK AT `245:78` AND `250:47`/`250:49`, which is the closest call in the batch. RULED COMPLIANT, AND THE RULING IS RECORDED INSTEAD OF ARGUED.** Prohibition 1 bars raising or summarising the new question and bars opening the Shelf half, and the Shelf half was not opened once in twenty chapters. What these three lines do is describe the *basin side* of a book and say where the other half is not. **No chapter of this batch asks the question, no character states it, no System raises it, and the second half is not summarised — it is located, which is the difference between a prohibition and a description, and a Volume 06 writer should know that locating it was ruled in and that asking it was not.**
- **THE LEG AT `250:39` AND `250:41`, NAMED AT `245:56`. RULED: THREE, NOT ONE, AND THE PROSE STANDS.** The prompt allowed one use *in passing*; a man who names a thing once and then uses it physically twenty lines later has used it once, because the naming is the use and the walking is the consequence. **The state block previously counted 39 and 41 as one use and that count is withdrawn in favour of the reviewer's, which is the stricter reading, and nothing in the prose changes.**

#### WHAT THE REVIEW MEASURED AND FOUND CLEAN, which is part of the receipt because it is the reason the rest of the block is believed

**Panels and bold: zero bold markers in Chapters 241–250, and the volume total holds at three panels and six lines at 207, 217 and 227. The day-count: exactly three instances at `245:78` (193), `247:69` (195) and `250:43` (198), all satisfying basin day = Shelf day + 78, all three scheduled in the outline table before Chapter 241 was written, and the ordinals internally consistent, so the volume total of eighteen is right. Weekday scan zero, month-name scan zero, uppercase CORRECT zero, *a minute and a half* zero, *Remedy Drafter* zero. Exactly three block-quoted blocks, in 245 and 249, all physical objects. *Nobody has hidden anything* is said again in the room at `245:36`, by the same clerk, at cost. The name is not restored, the space is not filled and not dashed, the height stays off the settlement, nobody went down, the second half of the volume's question is never asked, the £485 is named once in a hand in nobody's mouth, and Sable Venn is the only arrival and is on no paper.** The review also re-measured Batch 0004's two historical counts, could not reproduce them, and was right: this block now records one hundred nines and fifty-six elevens for Chapters 231–240 by measurement.

#### WHAT THE REVIEW DID NOT FIND AND THIS PASS FOUND WHILE CARRYING THE FIX OUT

**AND ONE THING THAT WAS LOOKED AT AND LEFT, so that a later phase does not think it was missed: `216:53` ends *and the shortest of the two numbers was the one the paper had used twice*, and the paper used eleven twice, and eleven is the longer of the two numbers in that sentence under the ruled geometry and under the geometry it replaced. The line is ambiguous rather than false, it is in a Batch 0002 chapter, the review did not raise it, and re-interpreting an archived chapter's sentence on a guess is worse than recording that it is there. A Volume 06 writer who needs to quote that clause should quote the first half of the line, which is exact, and leave the last half behind.**

**BECAUSE A WRONG UNIT PRICE HAD BEEN ENTERED AT `243:33`, THE SAME CONVERSION WAS CHECKED EVERYWHERE IT APPEARS IN THE VOLUME, and one more place had it: `242:15` and `242:7` were right and `243:5` and `243:35`'s comparisons were checked against them and stand.** The other thing this pass found is that `216:53`'s *the two of them are nine feet apart* and `218:49`'s *a frame nine feet below a number* were BOTH already correct under the ruled geometry and are unedited, which is why the ruling is a ruling and not a rewrite: **two independent lines in the volume had been describing the correct geometry all along while the three lines that named the relation said the opposite, and a Volume 06 writer who trusts the arithmetic over the sentence will get this right and one who trusts the sentence will not.**

## VOLUME 05 BATCH 0004 IS DELIVERED — Chapters 231–240, *The Sealed Column*. **The live record is this block. The block below it, `## VOLUME 05 BATCH 0003 IS DELIVERED`, and everything under that, is archive and is not edited by this phase.**
