### 3. THE THREE CLOCKS, unchanged, obeyed in every chapter, and the engine of the volume is still arithmetic and not politics

**The Shelf keeps a grain year of fifty-two weeks. The Red Basin keeps three clocks and there is no conversion between any of them, and the finding that there is no conversion was made ONCE, at `202:45`, in a salt-pan's mouth, and forty-eight chapters are about what people do about a thing that has already been found out.** The pan-book year: fifty saltings, more or less, a salting being a drying, a dry-year salting about seven weeks and a wet-year one eleven, and therefore no length in a salting and no first drying that holds twice. The turn: a year from the first shaking, which is turn nought, twelve years back, and this year is turn twelve, and the first eight were numbered on a door frame by a man four short who corrected it himself in the front of his own book. **The day: a day since the second shaking, out of the water-book, and it is the only date in this basin that can be put on the outside of a bag that is going nine days to a city of four hundred thousand people, which is what Chapter 211 does with it.** Inside a day: the first light, the noon light, the closing of the water, and a sluice-keeper's day is the closing of the water.

**AND TWO FIGURES OF THE SLOW WERE FIXED IN THIS BATCH AND THE WORKING IS BESIDE THEM, because the first writing of this record had no figure for either.** The slow came up out of the flats on the evening of the sixth day of the fiftieth week, which is basin day one hundred and fifty-three, and at the closing of the water on day one hundred and fifty-nine six days and six nights of it had gone by: 159 − 153 = **6** ✓. The water-book's last mill-hand entry is *of the ninth*, and at Chapter 212's day of one hundred and sixty that entry is three weeks and a day old: 160 − 139 = **21** ✓, and the slow came up fourteen days after she wrote it, which is a fortnight, and **those are two different questions and the water-book has no column for which day a thing was written on, and Chapter 212 says so. RE-MEASURED ON THE BATCH 0002 REVIEW: the sentence above originally read *fifteen days* and *a fortnight and a day*, carrying a ✓ beside a wrong subtraction, and 153 − 139 = 14. The prose at `212:70` said the same wrong thing and is now *a fortnight*. Both are corrected; the prose figure wins, as the standing instruction requires.**

### 4. THE MONEY, every figure recomputed in a script and the working printed beside it. **THE INSTRUMENT IS THE SAME ONE EVERY TIME: print the unit price in the sentence, find it, and multiply.**

- **A matter carried to the chamber of plenty at Orison and brought back is £5 6s** — entry a pound, the day four pounds, nine days' board at eightpence. 240d + 960d + 72d = **1,272d = £5 6s** ✓ (`211:69`, `220:23`).
- **TWO matters is £10 12s, and the second one is the one that does not come back in time.** 2 × 1,272d = **2,544d**; 2,544 − 2,400 = 144d; 144 ÷ 12 = **12s with no remainder**; 2,400d = £10 ✓. **Five of it was Ilyan's and seven of it was the clerk of the Seat out of his own money and out of a column that does not exist, and the chapter says both of those out loud and says that neither of them is going to be thanked (`211:71`, `220:27`).**
- **Ilma Treen: nine shillings a week for the sixteen weeks the Sallow runs, which is a hundred and forty-four shillings, which is seven pounds four, and nothing the rest of the year** (`212:46`). 16 × 108d = **1,728d = £7 4s** ✓.
- **The pit gives four and six of the nine and the Seat owes four and six, and three years of that is ten pounds sixteen** (`212:46`). 16 × 54d = 864d = **£3 12s** ✓; 3 × 864d = 2,592d = **£10 16s** ✓.
- **She works the water a hundred and forty days a year and is paid for a hundred and twelve of them; the unpaid twenty-eight is four more weeks, and four weeks at four and six is eighteen shillings exactly** (`212:48`). 16 × 7 = 112; 140 − 112 = 28; 28 ÷ 7 = 4; 4 × 54d = **216d = 18s**, and 216 ÷ 12 = 18 with no remainder ✓. **These are the corrected figures and they are the figures in the chapter.**
- **The Seat is said to have four hundred and eleven pounds a year and it is a kitchen figure: nobody in Hollow Sallow can produce it out loud, two people who were asked gave two different numbers and one said she did not know it, and the chapter strikes out of the record the one comparison the number permits** (`212:54`, `212:56`, `219:25`).
- **A sluice gate: the frame £4 10s and the oak £1 8s, together £5 18s, and the whole of what anybody has spent on this structure in three years is a gate in front of a frame that does nothing** (`212:9`, `212:88`). 1,080d + 336d = **1,416d = £5 18s** ✓. **The lath with the figure on it is in the frame at the end of the batch and nobody has bought anything.**
- **The dam's ordinary year: forty loads of clay at ninepence is one pound ten, and a hundred and forty of rubble at threepence is one pound fifteen, and the two together are three pounds and five** (`215:15`). 360d + 420d = **780d = £3 5s** ✓.
- **The night dressing, AND THIS ONE WAS WRONG IN THE FIRST WRITING AND THE WORKING WON, and it is the second time in this one batch that shillings were added to pounds to make a rounder figure.** Twenty loads of clay at 9d is **180d, which is fifteen shillings exactly and not one pound four**; thirty loads of rubble at 3d is **90d, which is seven shillings and sixpence and not one pound ten**; the two together are **270d, which is one pound two and six** (`216:9`). 270 ÷ 780 = **0.346, about a third of a year** ✓, and the chapter says a third out loud. **£2 6s is nowhere in the volume and the outline's money table was corrected at the site rather than in a banner.**
- **Four men on the Anner wage for a week is forty shillings, which is two pounds, and a man is on ten shillings a week for fifty weeks, which is twenty-five pounds a year** (`215:15`, `215:19`). 4 × 120d = **480d = £2** ✓; 50 × 120d = **6,000d = £25** ✓. **So the whole week's labour of the four men who hold the height costs about twice what a year of material costs, and neither figure is in a document.**
- **A roof, wet through, is about £9 to put back, and it is NOT broken into thatch and battens and nails, because the man who does roofs has never given anybody a price in parts in his life and says why** (`217:23`, `217:29`). £9 = **2,160d** ✓.
- **Nineteen houses at about £9 a roof is a hundred and seventy-one pounds** (`217:62`). 2,160d × 19 = **41,040d**; 41,040 ÷ 240 = **171 with no remainder** ✓. **The prompt's own working for this figure was wrong and said £9 is 180d; the answer was right and the multiplication was not, and the corrected working is what the chapter prints and what the outline prints.**
- **£171 is 41,040d and the whole year of a sluice-keeper is 1,728d, so £171 is 41,040 ÷ 1,728 = 23.75, which is about twenty-four years, and she is standing in it and has been for eleven years** (`217:64`). 1,728 × 23 = 39,744; 41,040 − 39,744 = 1,296; 1,296 ÷ 1,728 = 0.75 ✓.
- **A quarter of the Seat's four hundred and eleven pounds a year is a hundred and two pounds and fifteen shillings, and the flood is sixty-eight pounds and five shillings MORE than a quarter, and the four hundred and eleven is a number that came out of a kitchen** (`219:25`). 98,640d ÷ 4 = 24,660d; 24,660 − 24,480 = 180d = 15s; 24,480d = £102 ✓. 41,040 − 24,660 = 16,380d; 16,380 ÷ 240 = 68 pounds remainder 60d = **£68 5s** ✓.
- **Forty-five times a hundred and seventy-one pounds is seven thousand six hundred and ninety-five, and the whole of what is being argued about in this basin is seven thousand seven hundred a year, so nineteen roofs are a forty-fifth of the difference between what a ton costs at the pit's mouth and at Sallowby, and five pounds short of even that** (`219:13`). 171 × 45 = **7,695**; 7,700 − 7,695 = **5** ✓. And 1,100 × 4 = 4,400; 1,100 × 11 = 12,100; 1,100 × 7 = **7,700** ✓.
- **A four-page order of the water-seat: four pages at 3d and 6d for the seal is one shilling and six** — and it is not in the four pages, because the Seat does not charge itself. 12d + 6d = **18d** ✓. **Not restated in this batch.**
- **A pitman is on ten shillings a week for fifty weeks, which is £25 a year exactly** (`215:15`). ✓
- **AND THE FIGURES THAT TRAVEL AS DEBTS AND NOT AS PRICES, and none of them is converted and none of them stops.** Four hundred and eighty-five pounds a year on nine thousand seven hundred at the close of that grain year, **named once in this batch, in his own hand, at `220:45`, and he says the other side's year turns in about forty weeks and that he is not printing the figure, because the figure is a figure of the other side of the book and there is no column in this basin for it.** Check: 9,700 × 12d = 116,400d; 116,400 ÷ 240 = **485** ✓. The second notice of five shillings and fourpence, unpaid and not going to be forgiven (`220:39`). Check: 4 × 16d = **64d = 5s 4d** ✓. The claim of forty-five pounds two shillings and eightpence to a carter of fifty-one with a boy, eleven miles off, not producible, no form for asking, **and he is right** (`220:39`). Check: 406 × 32d − 2,160d = 10,832d; 10,832 ÷ 240 = 45 pounds remainder 32d = **2s 8d** ✓. The ninety-one numbered sheets in a sacking bag in a yard; the box of about nine hundred and forty forms; the minute of the first of the forty-eighth week; the dead man in the fourth line of a list of two hundred and forty and his wife. **Nesh Arrad's name is on that list at `220:39` and no day figure is printed for her, for the reason in his own hand in Chapter 210, and her letter has not arrived and cannot have arrived and no chapter of this batch says that it has.**

### 5. WHAT THE BATCH DELIVERED, once each, with the chapter and the line

1. **A GIRL OF SEVENTEEN, WHO HAS BEEN IN THAT LANE SINCE SHE WAS BORN, READS A FOUR-LINE ORDER OUT TO A LANE AND ASKS THE FIRST QUESTION ANYBODY IN THE RED BASIN HAS EVER ASKED ABOUT A DOCUMENT, and she is not a lawyer and has never been in a room: *it says how high the water is, and it does not say high as from where*** (`211:29`).
2. **THE ORDER BREACHES THE CHAMBER'S OWN SECOND RULING ON ITS FACE, and the under-manager says it is not void and he is right, because the ruling is about a mark on a doorpost and not about a height named in an order, and the finding is that there is no form for asking a chamber whether its own order breaks its own ruling** (`211:49`, `211:55`, `211:61`).
3. **A SECOND MATTER GOES TO ORISON IN A BAG WITH A KNOT CUT WITH A KNIFE, DATED ON THE OUTSIDE WITH A DAY OUT OF THE WATER-BOOK, because the Shelf's grain year does not exist in this basin and a deed that arrives with one on it is a deed about nothing — and the clerk of the Seat pays the five pounds and six out of his own money out of a column that does not exist and says so first** (`211:69`, `211:73`, `211:75`).
4. **THE SLOW HAS BEEN DOWN SIX DAYS AND THE ORDER HAS BEEN OBEYED EXACTLY, and what it did on the ground in six days is a shut causeway, nine carts a day on a high road at a morning and a half each way, and eleven feet held above a sill with four men and a barrow** (`211:17`, `211:103`).
5. **THE STANDING ORDER OF THE SLUICE NAMES A WEATHER AND NOT A DAY — it says *the day the pans are turned at Coldbeck* — and a salting is a drying, seven weeks in a dry year and eleven in a wet one, and the only person in the basin who could say in advance which year it is has never been asked** (`212:21`–`24`, `212:34`).
6. **THE STANDING ORDER IS THE ELEVENTH SALTING OF THE FORTY-NINTH YEAR AND THE BOND IS THE ELEVENTH SALTING OF THE FORTIETH, and that is the same salting of a year nine along, and a pan-book year is a year and nine is a year, and that is the whole of the arithmetic anybody in this basin can do on it and nobody has ever done it** (`212:28`).
7. **THE TURN, and it is in a book and not in anybody's mouth: on the ninth the sluice was shut and the water was in the hollow and nobody came, in the mill hand, three weeks and a day before this chapter, written by the woman who has been the gate for eleven years, on the Seat's own book, in forty leaves of it, and nobody in nine years has ever asked her what is on them** (`212:70`, `212:68`).
8. **A HEIGHT PUT ON TWO DOORPOSTS BY THE SAME WATER IS A FOOT ABOVE ONE OLD MARK AND A FOOT BELOW THE OTHER, so the two old marks are two feet apart — and a joiner of forty-one has had his own shoulder against both of them for nine years and says they are a foot apart, and neither man is lying, and one of them is wrong by a foot, and nobody can say which, because neither of them has had water on both marks in the same hour** (`213:25`, `213:31`).
9. **THE LANE ASKS WHAT THE NUMBER IS A NUMBER OF, OUT LOUD, ON A BANK, AND IT IS THE FIRST QUESTION ANYBODY IN THAT VILLAGE HAS EVER ASKED ABOUT A DOCUMENT, AND IT IS ASKED OF A WOMAN WHO IS NOT A LAWYER AND CANNOT ANSWER IT** (`213:49`, `213:53`, `213:55`).
10. **ASKING ELEVEN PEOPLE COSTS THREE DAYS AND THREE DAYS IS NOT A FIGURE, and there is no form in this basin for asking a person a question that matters, and the person who keeps the sluice has not been in the Seat's room in eleven years for exactly that reason** (`214:21`, `214:19`).
11. **A CLERK OF A SEAT READS A FOUR-LINE ORDER OUT IN A LANE HE HAS NO AUTHORITY TO READ IT IN, ON ELEVEN CHAIRS CARRIED DOWN A HILL BY NINE PEOPLE, AND NOBODY SIGNS ANYTHING, AND IT IS THE THIRD THING HE HAS DONE IN ELEVEN DAYS THAT HE HAS NO AUTHORITY TO DO AND IT IS THE WORSE OF THE THREE** (`214:55`, `214:59`).
12. **A THING THAT IS VOID AND UNREAD IS NOT A PROTECTION, IT IS AN ABSENCE, in the under-manager's own mouth, in ochre dust, and it is the volume's argument about contracts in a stone said again by a different man and it is still true** (`215:27`).
13. **THE HEIGHT IS HELD EXACTLY AND ON PURPOSE, and the under-manager made it eleven feet and an inch at the low end and ten feet and eleven inches at the high end with a string and a barrow and most of a morning, because the paper says eleven feet and does not say where along the dam** (`215:11`).
14. **NINE WORDS, IN HIS OWN MOUTH, IN OCHRE DUST: I HAVE BEEN GIVEN A NUMBER AND NEVER A REASON. He is right. And a thing held exactly is a thing held to a figure, and a figure is not a decision, and correctly is the whole of the danger** (`215:43`, `215:49`, `215:51`).
15. **THE ANNER IS THE ONLY BODY IN THIS BASIN WITH MEN ENOUGH TO HOLD A DAM, AND A MAN WITH MEN ENOUGH TO HOLD A DAM IS NOT LOOKING AT ELEVEN HOUSES, AND THAT IS NOT A FAULT, THAT IS THE ARITHMETIC** (`215:55`).
16. **THE MIDPOINT, IN THREE CHAPTERS. ONE: the height is held exactly, as instructed, lawfully, by the only body with enough men, and four men and a barrow do what the paper says because that is the whole of what anybody has ever asked them to do, and the Sallow comes up backwards because its somewhere has been taken off it, and the sluice does its job, and the sluice's job ends at the frame** (`216:3`, `216:7`, `216:17`, `216:31`).
17. **THE ARITHMETIC OF IT IS ONE SUBTRACTION AND IT IS PRINTED IN THE CHAPTER WHERE A READER CAN DO IT IN THEIR HEAD: the frame's sill is nine feet above the Anner's sill; eleven feet above the Anner's sill is therefore two feet above the frame's sill; two feet above the frame's sill is about the height of the floors of the houses on the upper half of that lane. **THE FIRST CLAUSE OF THIS ITEM SAID *THE ANNER'S SILL IS NINE FEET ABOVE THE FRAME'S SILL* UNTIL 27 SEPTEMBER 2026 AND WAS INVERTED; THE SUBTRACTION IS ONLY LEGIBLE WITH THE DIRECTION AS IT NOW STANDS, AND `218:49` HAS ALWAYS SAID IT CORRECTLY.** And the whole of the finding in nine words: the eleven feet is the dam's number and not the lane's** (`216:45`, `216:49`).
18. **NOBODY DROWNED, AND THAT IS NOT A MITIGATION AND IS NOT WRITTEN AS ONE, and the nineteen that are under water are the nineteen that are under water for the next four months, and the third line of the order — that no person of Hollow Sallow is to be asked to leave any house — is the only line anybody in that lane could have used and it is why they were standing in it** (`216:55`, `216:57`).
19. **THE MIDPOINT, PART TWO: NINETEEN HOUSES AND NOT ELEVEN, and the eleven were right about the eleven, and the eight are a barn that became a house and seven others, and the man who turned the barn into a house cut no mark because nobody alive in that lane had been told to, and he is forty-four and did not know for four days that his house was in anything** (`217:5`, `217:9`).
20. **NOBODY IN THIS VALLEY IS A VILLAIN, AND IT IS SAID PLAINLY, AND THE BOND'S THREE SENTENCES ARE SET OUT AND THE PROMISE IS THE FIRST TWO OF THEM AND THE PROMISE WAS KEPT EXACTLY BY THE PARTY IT NAMES: the village the mine had promised to protect is flooded by the fulfilment of the mine's promise, and that is not a sentence anybody in this basin is going to be able to improve on this year** (`217:13`).
21. **THE FINDING, IN HER OWN MOUTH, NINE WORDS, AND SHE IS NOT A LAWYER AND IS NOT A LAWYER BECAUSE THERE IS NO LAWYER IN THIS BASIN: it named a dam, it named a height, it named a day, it never named the water** (`217:48`).
22. **THE MIDPOINT, PART THREE: HE SAYS THE CATEGORY IN HIS OWN MOUTH AND IT IS NOT THE SENTENCE HE SAID ONCE AND HE DOES NOT SAY IT AGAIN AND HE DOES NOT EXPLAIN IT AND NOBODY ASKS HIM TO: a rule that names no bystander is carried by the nearest body with hands** (`218:39`, `218:43`).
23. **THE FOUR PAGES STAY ON THE WALL, AND THE PERSON WHO SAYS WHY IS A WOMAN OF ABOUT FORTY WHO WASHES THE PEN AND TURNS THE BOARD OVER, and her reason is that a basin with no document on a wall has nothing for the next person to put her hand on, and the chapter says that in about nine days somebody will come in and ask what the four pages were for, and that there is no minute of any of it because the Seat keeps no minutes** (`218:51`, `218:57`).
24. **HIS NAME IS NOT ON THE FOUR PAGES AND NEVER WAS, AND IT IS NOT ON ANYTHING IN THIS VALLEY, AND IT IS THE ONE FACT IN THE VOLUME THAT HAS NOT GONE WRONG, AND NOBODY IN THE ROOM SAYS SO, AND THE CHAPTER NOTICES THAT NOBODY SAYS SO, AND HE WROTE BESIDE IT THAT IT IS NOT A RELIEF** (`218:63`).
25. **THE MONEY IS NOT THE DIFFICULTY, AND THE CHAPTER PROVES IT BY ASKING THE WRONG QUESTION ON PURPOSE: nineteen people are asked what they need, each answers in about nine words, and NOT ONE OF THE NINETEEN ANSWERS IS MONEY, and two of the nineteen are about somebody being asked at the right time, and one of them is about the other end of a piece of paper** (`219:39`, `219:45`, `219:47`).
26. **THE LIST OF NINETEEN IS A DOCUMENT AND IT IS THE FIRST DOCUMENT ANYBODY IN THIS BASIN HAS EVER WRITTEN ABOUT ITSELF, and it is the back of a leaf of a hundred-year-old water-book, and it has a mark under every answer and not a tally of anything, and nobody signed it, and it has no heading because there is no form in this basin for it, and it went under a board in a cart shed with a stone on it, and a man of thirty-one has not read it and is not going to read it** (`219:5`, `219:37`, `219:39`, `219:61`, `219:67`).
27. **THE SECOND MATTER CAME BACK IN NINE DAYS AS A LETTER OF TWO PARAGRAPHS FROM A CLERK OF THE CHAMBER WHO DID HIS JOB PROPERLY, SAYING THE BENCH IS SEIZED OF THE MATTER AND WILL CONSIDER IT IN ITS OWN TIME AND GIVING NO TIME, and quoting back the first of the three rulings word for word, and the two matters together cost ten pounds and twelve and neither the writer of them nor the payer of them is going to be thanked** (`220:21`, `220:23`, `220:29`).
28. **HE READS THE SHELF SIDE OF HIS OWN BOOK OUT LOUD ON A BANK TO NINE PEOPLE AND STOPS AT THE TENTH ITEM AND SAYS HE IS NOT GOING TO SAY IT, AND NOBODY ASKS HIM WHICH YEAR IT WAS, AND HE IS NOT RELIEVED OF THE NOT BEING ASKED, AND THE CHAPTER SAYS SO** (`220:37`, `220:39`, `220:41`).
29. **THE SYSTEM PUTS NOTHING UP AND SAYS NOTHING WHATEVER ABOUT A DAM, OR NINETEEN HOUSES, OR A RULE THAT NAMED A BODY AND NOT A PERSON, OR A LETTER OF TWO PARAGRAPHS, and the second half of the volume's question is not asked out loud in this chapter and is not even a whisper, and nobody went down, and the chamber is seized of the matter and will consider it in its own time** (`220:61`).
30. **AND THE BATCH ENDS ON A PERSON DOING THEIR WORK, and it is not a ruling, not a discovery and not an answer: a gate at the closing of the water and a chair put back at a table, and the mud is not down** (`220:63`).

### 6. THE DEVICES, and none advanced

- **The leg. CANON and carried from Batch 0001: the finding it rests on is `155:7` — the leg goes when he is carrying a thing somebody else is going to have to read, and it comes back when the carrying stops. IN THIS BATCH IT DID NOT COME BACK, and that is a consequence of the carrying and not a rule and not a cure.** It is used once, on a wet clay bank in the dark at the fourth light at the sluice frame, as a fact and not as a discovery, and the reason is not given and he has not told one person in this valley what any of it is (`216:51`). **A woman of about forty says a sentence in Chapter 219 that comes within one step of it — that if he put his name at the bottom of her sheet it would be a list of what a man from four hundred miles off found out, and the four hundred miles is in it, and the leg is in it — and she says she has not asked about the leg and is not going to, and nobody in that road had said a word to him about it** (`219:61`, `219:63`). **NO FILE AND NO CHAPTER IN VOLUME 05 STATES A COUNT OF DAYS SINCE THE LEG CLEARED, AND NONE MAY.**
- **The rendering. Not staged in Batch 0001, not referred to, and not staged or referred to here.** Volume 04's last word on it is *not to be referred to again*.
- **The left hand, the vantage, the door in the picture, the token and the afterimage: none is mentioned in this batch and none is advanced.** The token is not touched and the fourth use stands: it took the words.
- **The continuity line is not refused again and no twelfth refusal is permitted, and it is not on the Red Basin's table.** Volume 04 refused it eleven times. It is not refused here and it is not paid off.
- **THE COUNT OF THE PEOPLE WHO KNOW HE CANNOT READ IS NOT PRINTED IN THIS PROJECT, in any file, in any figure, and it is not printed in this block, and this batch adds nothing to it in the fiction.** Refer to `154:53` and `164:49` and to nothing else. **It is exercised as a working constraint and it is a real one, and it was exercised four times in ten chapters: the standing order is read out by a chainman against the gate (`212:19`); the water-book page is read out on a wet bank by a chainman, twice, and he notices that the two readings differ in nothing (`216:67`); the nineteen answers are taken down in a hand that is not his (`219:45`); and the four pages are never once put into his hands.** **A first writing of Chapter 215 had the under-manager say out loud that a figure had twice been asked for by a man who could not read, and that was cut before delivery, because a sentence in a character's mouth that names the limit is a disclosure with a motive attached and the bar in this project is on the volume and not on the sentence.**

### 7. THE THIRTEEN INHERITED THREADS, AND EVERY ONE OF THEM COMES OUT OF THIS BATCH EXACTLY AS IT WENT IN

**Nothing in this list is resolved and this block resolves none of them. A batch that resolves one is a second reversal, and the outline forbids it.**

1. **The new question — whose memory is missing from the account that records his payment.** `198:35`, seventh line, a book six miles up a road that goes north, a number upon a sheet and not a name, no column for the year. **It is NOT raised again in this batch. It is not asked, not answered and not compressed into one word, and it is not even a whisper.** He reads nine items off the Shelf side of his own book out loud on a bank at `220:37`–`39` and **stops before the tenth and says he is not going to say it**, and a woman of about forty says *there is one more* and he says there is one more and he is not going to say it (`220:39`). **Nobody in the Red Basin asks him which year it was, and nobody ever has, and he is not relieved of the not being asked and the chapter says so.** The System does not raise it and does not answer it.
2. **The carrying, off nothing, with a finding about why.** A line that is not a claim is not a thing that comes off; a forbearance once a year and the next one fifty-two weeks off. **He carries the Ryefold matter through all ten chapters and puts it down once and it does not come off, and Volume 05 does not answer it and does not repeat the account's agent's three sentences.**
3. **The blank in the third line of the bench's own book, still a blank**, and the fourth sheet's question, still unasked, and the fourth hand still a stranger's hand nobody has asked. **Named in his own hand at `220:37` and nothing more.** The clerk of thirty-four's letter is in a bundle on a shelf in a room in Sallowby, tied with a knife-cut end, and it is NOT OPENED in this batch and the chapter does not say so again and does not need to. The entry is due in about nine months.
4. **Nesh Arrad, thirty-nine. Her name is on the list he reads out at `220:39` and NO DAY FIGURE IS PRINTED FOR HER**, and the reason is in his own hand and is in Chapter 210: the figure is a figure of the other side of that book and it would be the first place in this valley where the two numbers had been in the same sentence. **The letter has not arrived and cannot have arrived and no chapter of this batch says that it has.**
5. **The second notice of five shillings and fourpence, never paid for** — `220:39`, where he reads it out loud in his own words: never paid, and it is not going to be paid by me, and it is not going to be forgiven.
6. **The claim of forty-five pounds two shillings and eightpence to a carter of fifty-one with a boy, eleven miles off, not producible, no form for it, and he is right** — `220:39`, read out loud, unadvanced. **The Red Basin's own unproducible material — the standing order with no name on it, made by a man who has not been down to the water since the winter before the first shaking — is a different thing and does not stand in for it** (`212:24`).
7. **The dead man in the fourth line of a list of two hundred and forty and his wife, who knows she is on the fourth line and does not know her husband is not alive** — `220:37`, read out loud, unadvanced. **RUTH VELLAN IN THE RED BASIN IS A DIFFERENT DEAD PERSON, dead four years, whose name is on a half-deed drawn in the ninth turn in a hand that is not her husband's and not the scrivener's (`203:88`), and the two may not be joined and are not joined in this batch.**
8. **The box of about nine hundred and forty forms in a run of four thousand, the man with a key six miles off who is there two days out of seven** — `220:37`, read out loud, not found, and he says he is not going to ask for it this month. Not found and not to be found.
9. **The ninety-one numbered sheets in a sacking bag in a yard, about nine hundred doors, no one hundredth person, about four hundred carts not asked** — unadvanced. **The scrivener's about six hundred and forty deeds and about ninety Anner copies are a different number in a different trade and are not that number.**
10. **The class of three hundred and thirty-nine and the one boy of nineteen who is its only instrument** — unadvanced. **There is no boy of nineteen in this batch. A first writing of Chapter 215 put a boy of nineteen on the barrow at the Anner and it was taken out before delivery, because the one boy of nineteen in this project is a person in another county and a second one here would be a shadow of him.**
11. **The minute of the first of the forty-eighth week, one sheet, the only copy, in a drawer four miles off with nobody's name on it** — `220:37`, read out loud, unadvanced.
12. **A clerk of thirty-four's letter, unanswered, with the entry due in about nine months** — unadvanced, and see item 3.
13. **The thirteen standing prose defects carried from Volume 04** — `160:96`'s four hundred and thirty, `158:77` and `158:79`, `167:41`'s two pounds five, `174:53`'s eleventh day of the forty-fifth week, `173:55`'s first week after the cut, the forty-five times at 183 and 185, and `167:35`'s three days. **Carried, not repaired and not built on.**

### 8. WHAT A LATER PASS MUST NOT DO IN VOLUME 05

1. **Do not answer the new question and do not compress it into one word.** It is written once, at `210:39` and `210:41`, in his own hand, in a penny exercise book in a cart shed, with a place and a form of words, and he does not ask. **It is not raised again in this batch and no person in the Red Basin asks him which year it was, and he is not relieved of the not being asked.**
2. **Do not add a panel beyond the two in the volume, one at `207:67`–`68` and one at `217:35`–`36`, and do not add a day-count instance beyond the nine in Chapters 201–220 without putting its chapter and its basin day in `outline/volume-05.md` first, and do not print a count of instances without saying it is a count of instances.**
3. **Do not put a weekday anywhere and do not put a month-name anywhere.** The one month-name hit in the volume is the proper noun *Briar March* at `210:55`. **The five weekday hits in Batch 0002 were all the word *Tuesday* and all five were taken out; the basin's units are *the first light*, *the noon light*, *the closing of the water*, and a day of the week of the Shelf named by its number.**
4. **Do not print a count of the people who know he cannot read, anywhere, including in a hand-off note, and do not add to it in the fiction.** Refer to `154:53` and `164:49` and to nothing else.
5. **Do not give Ilyan an office, a party, a bench, a body, a remedy he chose alone, or a stage.** He is Stage 4, arrived in Chapter 177, and it is spent. A scan for *Remedy Drafter* over Chapters 201–220 returns zero.
6. **Do not produce a person. THE CLASS IS DEFINED AND THE COUNT IS STILL FOUR, and Batch 0002 added nobody.** A self-arrived person is somebody who enters the fiction as a participant in a scene on a day nobody fixed. The four are Ewan Troke (`201:19`), Fen Tregerran (`202:5`), Alo Vearn (`209:5`), and the man who comes through the north twice a year (`210:53`). **THE LINE IS THIN AND IT IS DRAWN HERE AND IT IS THE ONE A REVIEWER WILL LOOK FOR: in Batch 0002 the persons named for the first time are a girl of seventeen who has been in that lane since she was born (`211:21`), a woman of about forty who has been in the Seat's one room for nine years (`206:93`, `207:63`, `210:51`), a man of about forty who has stood on that causeway for twenty years (`211:5`), a man of fifty-six who does roofs on that lane and has done nine of them (`217:25`, `217:29`), a man of seventy-four who was born in the second house up that lane (`213:7`), the nineteen households of the flood, and a carter of about fifty-one with a boy who is not of this district and is named only as a claim. Naming a person who has been in a house the whole time is not producing a person, and Chapter 213 says out loud that the man of seventy-four was born in that lane, and Chapter 216 names its four dam-men as the four the pit could spare and does not name them.**
7. **Do not undo anything and do not relieve anybody.** The lease is answered and not renewed and not lost; the carrying is off nothing; the blank is a blank; the second notice is unpaid; nobody is redeemed; **the account's charge of four hundred and eighty-five pounds a year falls due at the close of that grain year and does not stop. And the batch does not pay the remedy and may not be read as paying it: ordinary witnesses revise his remedy in Batch 0003, at Chapters 221 to 230, and his name comes out of the document there because they are right, and nothing in this batch begins that, and the nineteen write nothing, and nobody forgives anybody.**
8. **Do not introduce a new enemy and do not make the world bigger.** Jory Vellan is an under-manager who is right about his dam and is not a villain, and the people who keep this volume's disputes from being settled are about thirty clerks in a building with a yard at Orison who did their jobs properly. No new faction, no new magic, no new people with a new name, no second city, no organisation.
9. **Do not open a redacted archive and do not have anybody ask for it.** One column heading, in a stranger's eye, at `208:85`. **Chapter 218 names that column out loud and says that the clerk of the Seat has never asked about it and is not going to, and the chapter is careful that the naming is not an asking and that nothing is opened** (`218:25`). The climax is that HE opens it, in Batch 0005.
10. **Do not let Sable Venn arrive, negotiate, or make an offer.** She was named once from a distance in Chapter 210 and one person in that room repeated the name wrong. **Batch 0002 did not name her at all.**
11. **Do not reach the stone and do not let anybody stand in front of it.** Anchor 8 remains missing beneath the Red Basin until Volume 14. **The second half of the volume's question — why it answers from beneath Orison — is not asked out loud in any chapter of this batch and is not even a whisper, as a panel, as a rendering, as a voice, or as a person remarking that anything answered, and Chapter 220 says in its own words that the chamber is seized of the matter and will consider it in its own time and that nobody went down.**
12. **Do not restore the right of refusal, answer the historical question, advance the authorship question or the Shale Mirror, name NK-3317, call for Tamsin, put Neris Ro or Bram Orren in a scene, answer the Bridgeward, send anybody to Carrisand, redeem Ansa Rell, or ask about Alma Gannock's boy of eight. Do not name the First Witness. Do not make Ors Halm the last problem in the series.**
13. **Do not restate the sentence at `156:63` and do not relieve him of it.** The four words were quoted exactly once afterwards, at `194:43`, and may not be quoted again, explained, or put in anybody's mouth. **What happened in this batch is that the CATEGORY was found again by an ordinary person, about water, in this basin, without those words — by a girl of seventeen at `211:29`, by a man of seventy-four at `213:41`, and by a woman of thirty-eight at `217:48` — and that the man who said the category once before recognised it in a different medium at `218:39` and said it in a different sentence and did not explain it and was not forgiven the first time either. That is a second finding in a second medium and not a restatement.**
14. **Do not use the 179 and 189 shape a third time.** A scan for uppercase CORRECT returns zero over Chapters 201–220.
15. **Do not restage the six devices Volume 05 spends, and each has been used once and none may be used again: a word defined twice in two margins (201); a deed in two pieces (203); the two-calendars-do-not-convert finding, said once in a salt-pan's mouth (202); the quick ruling's four complete lines as a discovery (207); the mark that is not a measurement (213); and the order's own logic examined (217, and the examination is the reversal).**

### 9. THE TWO MISSING VOLUME AUDITS, WHICH ARE STILL OPEN, AND NEITHER IS THIS PHASE'S

**Re-measured in the directories on 26 September 2026, after this batch, and not read out of a list: `reviews/volume-01/` holds batch-0001, batch-0003 and batch-0005 and NO volume audit; `reviews/volume-02/` holds batch-0003, batch-0004, batch-0005 and an audit; `reviews/volume-03/` holds `batch-0001.md` only and NO volume audit; `reviews/volume-04/` holds its audit. That is SEVEN batch reviews where TWENTY are expected, which is THIRTEEN missing, and TWO missing volume audits.** Twenty is the expectation about the twenty BATCHES OF CHAPTERS and not about prompt directories: there are nineteen `workspace/<volume>/batch-*/PROMPT.md` directories, because Volume 04's first batch — Chapters 151 to 160 — was written under phase id `next` and has no directory of its own. There are three volume-close phase directories, for Volumes 02, 03 and 04, and Volume 01 has none.
**AND `reviews/volume-05/` STILL DOES NOT EXIST, which is true and not an omission by this phase: the review of the Volume 05 planning phase was run by the controller and its receipt is `logs/plan-0001.review.log`, and nothing in this project may write a review into a directory it did not review from. THE REVIEW RECEIPT FOR THAT PLANNING PHASE IS SECTION 12 OF THE BLOCK BELOW THIS ONE and it is not edited by this phase and is not restated here.** **THE VOLUME 03 AUDIT HAS NOT RUN, and it is the urgent one, because it is the volume immediately before this one and its verdict may change what Volume 05 inherits. Every figure the Volume 03 close prints is therefore UNVERIFIED by this phase and a Volume 04 agent had to say exactly this and it is the honest form.**

### 10. TWO THINGS OWNED BY THE CONTROLLER, WHICH THIS BLOCK RECORDS AND DOES NOT FIX

**`state/phase-ledger.json` is controller-owned and is three volumes stale: it reads `currentPhase: batch-0002`, Volume 01, Chapters 11–20, status `planned`, attempts 0. NOTHING READS THAT FILE — `grep -rn "phase-ledger" scripts/ .github/` returns nothing — and `scripts/novel_runner.sh` selects the first `workspace/**/PROMPT.md` directory in `sort` order with no `.done`, `.blocked` or `.retired` marker, and writes `.done` itself. ### 12. THE DEFECTS THIS PASS FOUND IN BATCH 0001 WHILE READING IT, **recorded with a chapter, a line and the working, and NOT repaired in the prose and NOT built on**

**This section exists because the instruction for this phase was to record rather than repair, and because two of the four are the kind a reader can catch unaided and one of them is not, and the honest thing is to say which is which. NONE OF THE FOUR IS AN ARITHMETIC IMPOSSIBILITY, so none was repaired, and no line number in any Chapter 201 to 210 was moved.**

1. **`206:7` and `206:49` give the eleven marks to a man of sixty-eight who is at Coldbeck, and `206:9` names him as Davan Treloar, the man who drew the ninth-turn half of the marl-strip deed for the Anner. `207:11` names Ilya Kever, seventy-four, as *the man with the string*, and `206:69` and `207:13` both describe a boy of nineteen at the Anner in his household.** The two men cannot both be the man who cut the marks on a string, and the record does not say which is which. **Chapter 213 does not build on it: it uses a man of seventy-four with a string and a bucket of tar, does what the man does, and when the room wants to know whether he cut the eleven he says he is not going to tell them, because more than one man in this valley has been pointed at and he has never once been asked (`213:7`, `213:45`).** This is a **carried defect of Batch 0001 and not a fact of this volume**, and the Volume 05 close should decide it.
2. **`206:37` says the mark on the ninth doorpost is a foot higher than the mark on the fifth, and `206:45` says the mark on the fifth is a foot above the mark on the ninth. The two lines are in the same chapter and they cannot both be right.** **Chapter 213 does not build on it and does not choose: it asserts no direction between the two marks, and instead produces a third measurement — water against a new tar line on the ninth and a new tar line on the fifth, the water a foot above one old mark and a foot below the other, and a joiner of forty-one with his own shoulder on both old marks for nine years saying they are a foot apart (`213:25`, `213:31`).** The chapter's finding is stronger for not choosing, and the contradiction in `206` stands as a carried defect.
3. **`210:9` puts about four feet of water in the Hollow on day one hundred and fifty-eight with the sluice standing open, and the Batch 0002 card for Chapter 214 asked for a foot and a half in the Hollow on day one hundred and sixty-four.** The chapter does not use the card's figure, because the two cannot both be true and the card is not canon and the chapter is. **Chapter 214 does not print a depth at all; it says the lane cannot be walked and that the reason the question was never asked is not the water, it is that three days is not a figure (`214:37`).** And the rise is monotonic across the batch: two feet in the lane on day 159, water against the ninth doorpost on day 161, over the frame on day 164, a foot and a half over the mark at the ninth house on day 166, a foot and a bit in the bed on day 168.
4. **`outline/volume-05.md` line 178 priced red ochre by the hundredweight at £11, and ochre is priced by the ton at £4 at the pit's mouth and £11 at Sallowby, and the whole of the £7,700 a year rests on the ton.** The Batch 0001 chapter (`205:76`) is right and the outline's own table was wrong, and the outline was corrected at the site in the Batch 0002 section rather than in a banner, with the working printed and the working named as the winner. **The same table's bond row said the bond was lodged *at* the first shaking and it was lodged in the eleventh salting of the fortieth year, being the third salting after it, and that was corrected at the site too.** **Neither outline error ever reached a chapter and no chapter in Batch 0001 or Batch 0002 is affected.**
