**Ten instances the record had never seen, and the one at 177:91 is the last line of the sentence the volume's whole year comes from. There is a twelfth instance of the same idiom that is NOT a count of his days and is carried here as an open question and not as a device instance: 167:35, a licensed factor's mouth, "I have been in this county about six weeks and I have been in this district three days", on day thirty-seven. It is a third party counting his own arrival, and it does not agree with that man being in the district on days nineteen and twenty (158), and nobody in this record can say whether the two are the same visit or two, and a later pass should.**

**AND THE FIGURES THAT LOOK LIKE INSTANCES AND ARE NOT, so that a later pass does not add them: 155:47 and 155:83, fourteen days, is how long ago the granted year ended; 157:91, twenty-six days, is a carter's own stock; 160:46, nineteen days, is how long it took to get a line out of the interface; 167:81, thirty-one days, is the interval between the counting-house visit on day six and the admission on day thirty-seven; 176:47, twenty-five days, is how long a page sat on a nail; 181:3, thirty-seven days, is how long the lease sheet has been on the nail; 187:37, thirty-six days, is how long the interface has been putting things in front of him.**

**THE CONSEQUENCE, and it is the consequence that matters: the guardrail against a new instance was broken by the record and not by the prose, twice over, and Batch 0005 added nothing. The correct instrument is a scan of the chapters and not a reading of any list in this project, and this record adds no instance to the fiction. The count of twenty-two is a count of instances in the manuscript and is not itself an instance, and the repair phase re-ran the instrument on 26 September 2026 and got twenty-two again, with all twenty-two figures agreeing with their chapter's Shelf day.**

**FINDING 2. THE ONE INSTANCE THE RECORD DID ADD IS DESCRIBED WRONG IN FIVE FILES, AND ONE FILE SAYS BOTH THINGS ABOUT THE SAME LINE.** Five state files describe 158:121 as being in a third party's mouth, and `state/continuity.md` says it in one place and contradicts itself seven hundred lines later. **It is the fourth italic paragraph of the tenth page of the penny exercise book, in his own hand, on the same page and the same day and the same figure as 158:111, and the sentence before it is his own and the sentence after it is his own.** The correct description is already in this project, at `state/continuity.md` line 878, where the same line is called his own arithmetic in his own book. **A file that says two incompatible things about one line, seven hundred lines apart, is the whole of what this project has learned about itself in four volumes, and it is printed here as a fact and not as a rebuke.**

**FINDING 3. THE DAY-PHRASE INSTRUMENT, RE-RUN, AND IT IS THE SIXTH TIME A NUMBER IN THESE FILES HAS MOVED.** The instrument is printed in `state/continuity.md` and is `([a-z]+(?:-[a-z]+)?)\s+day of the ([a-z]+(?:-[a-z]+)?)\s+week`, **case-insensitive**, with the rule that a number of seven or less is always legal and a number of eight or more is legal only inside the band's week. Run for this record over the fifty chapters:

| Chapters | Total | Of the form | Unparsed | Illegal | Day number eight or more |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 151–160 | 16 | 16 | 0 | 0 | 4 |
| 161–170 | 41 | 40 | 1 | 0 | 0 |
| 171–180 | 52 | 51 | 1 | 1 | 8 |
| 181–190 | 54 | 54 | 0 | 0 | 3 |
| 191–200 | 42 | 42 | 0 | 0 | 2 |
| 151–200 | 205 | 203 | 2 | 1 | 17 |

**The one illegal instance in the volume is 174:53, the eleventh day of the forty-fifth week, and the forty-fifth week is days thirty-five to forty-one, so the phrase cannot exist. The two unparsed instances are 162:25 and 172:7, both of the form "the last day of the forty-sixth week", which is day forty-eight and is legal. The two compound instances in the last ten chapters are 191:79 and 193:43, both the twenty-second day of the forty-third week, which is inside that week's band of twenty-one to twenty-seven and is legal; the other forty are seven or less. Nothing in Chapters 191–200 is illegal and the qualitative verdict has held in every pass in this volume and the numbers have now been wrong six times.**

**Four things fall out of that table and all four are about the records.**
- **The Batch 0005 figure of forty-three over Chapters 191–200 does not reproduce. Forty-two is what the instrument returned on the day.**
- **Its control for Chapters 171–180 is given as fifty-one, and fifty-one is the count of instances of the form, not the total. The total is fifty-two, which is what Batch 0003 recorded and what the instrument returns, because the printed instrument folds the last-day form at 172:7 in. The sentence reporting the control dropped the unparsed instance and kept the form count, and the two differ by one, and the one it dropped is the one that carried Batch 0003's own figure.**
- **Its control for Chapters 181–190 is fifty-four and that is right, but the split it gives is wrong: the second day of the fortieth week at 185:121 is of the form and is legal, so all fifty-four are of the form, not fifty-three plus one.**
- **And the instrument, as it was first written, is case-sensitive, and a case-sensitive run over Chapters 181–190 returns fifty-three and not fifty-four, because it misses the all-capitals instance at 184:35 — the first ask for a page in nineteen years, and the first thing Chapter 200 counts as one. This record prints the case-insensitive figure and says which one it is, because the sentence that reports a number is inside the set it counts, and that is the whole reason the number has moved six times.**

### 2. THE THREE THINGS THE SERIES OUTLINE GAVE THIS VOLUME, verbatim, and where each one is

- *Witness Broker becomes Burden Bearer; he carries a grain ruling long enough to expose a hidden transfer of cost.* **Stage 4 arrives in Chapter 177 and not before**, by a practice (nine words, out loud, in a lane, to three people, at 177:21), by a failure (he tried to carry it alone first and the only way round that was to be produced, at 177:5), by a relationship (a woman of thirty-nine he does not ask, at 177:69 and 177:75), and by a permanent cost (a year of his own memory, named aloud at 177:87 and 177:91 and not taken). **The cost is paid in full, once, in Chapter 197, at 197:49, in a room, in the ordinary way, and what went is a year and not a fog and not a wound.** He gets no party, no bench, no body, no office and no remedy he chose alone. **He is not the Remedy Drafter and this volume did not make him one; the phrase does not appear in any of the fifty chapters, and a scan for it returns nothing.**
- *Ilyan's own testimony is used to classify an impoverished district as an uncooperative witness.* **Delivered in Chapter 165 and untouched thereafter.** It is at 156:63 in his own mouth, in a deposition in a boy's hand at 165:55, in the minute in the corrupted form at 165:125, in a corridor at 166:49, in his own account of the rule at 167:101, and **quoted exactly once in the whole fifty chapters after 165, at 194:43, in the mouth of the clerk who entered it, twenty-nine chapters after it was said, in answer to the question 165:61 planted.** **Nobody was relieved of it. Nobody was excused. Nobody says what he meant was.** The clerk thought the man was right, and he has not slept properly since, and that makes the harm worse and excuses nobody, and it is recorded as a decision and not as a repair.
- *With Sera's help and a witness quorum, Ilyan submits an advocate-assisted repayment draft and loses a year of personal memory as the burden.* **Delivered from 191 to 197, with two licensed people and neither of them an advocate (194:9 and 194:55), a witness quorum of four (193, sworn at 197:31), a draft in a second hand with a hole in its third line (195:19–23), and the year paid in a room in the ordinary way at 197:49.** He is in that room as a person and not as hands, which is the first time in two hundred chapters, and it is not an office and it is not a party.

### 3. THE CENTRAL PRESSURE, THE THESIS, AND WHAT THE VOLUME ACTUALLY DELIVERED

**The pressure, verbatim from the series outline: a food and debt crisis in the Ninefold Shelf becomes profitable to turn temporary exceptions into lifelong obligations.**

**The sentence every chapter in the volume had to be able to survive, in the volume's own words: the harvest is good and the harvest is being taken.** There is no blight, no famine and nothing has gone wrong anywhere that anybody could name. What went wrong is that a bad year, three years ago, was made permanent by a paper, and the paper is a piece of stationery, and it is priced the same for everybody, and the price of the paper is two shillings and eightpence.

**What the volume actually delivered is not a thesis but a shape, and the shape was found in a yard and put in a book by a boy of nineteen and then said out loud by a man who owns the thing he is describing. A burden with a name on it is a thing that can be sold, and the name is the district's, and the district is not the user, and for four hundred years the name in the middle of that sentence has been a category and not a person.** It is at 177:83 in a room and it is at 183:72 in a lane and it closes in Chapter 199 in the Chancellor's own mouth: a sheet is valued at what it saves, what it saves is a load, a load in this district's own book is two shillings and eightpence, a sheet costs two shillings and eightpence, so the number the register puts on a piece of paper is the number that district pays for a piece of paper, and the load is not in it. **And on his own scale a man with one cart moves about four hundred loads in a cut, which is sixty pounds, and a man with no cart is not in the scale in the first place, because the unit is a load and a load is a thing a cart does (199:27).** That is where the volume's own thesis is priced, in loads, on the last page but one, in the mouth of the man who set it.

**And the sentence the year comes from survives Chapter 197 word for word and is not to be paraphrased by any later pass into anything kinder.** 177:91, in his own mouth, in a room with eleven people in it, on the last day of a cut, reproduced whole and without an elision: “It is not a fog and it is not a wound and nobody has taken it. It is one year of my own out of thirty-one and I know which year and I have never written it down and I am not going to write it down tonight.” He put his hand flat on the shut book, which is a thing he has done twice. “It is the last of the years in which I was in a room with a person I had not yet met. There was a year of them and most of them were like the others and every one of them was the same shape: I was the nearest thing to hand, and I did not know whether it would help, and I went in anyway. There is nothing noble in that and it is not a gift and it is not a curse. It is a price that a person in a room a long way off from this one named out loud to me once, and I have never found out whether the naming was fair, and I have not asked anybody since, and I am not going to start tonight, and that person is not the woman of twenty-eight and I am not going to mix them up in a room. The thing that makes those years is that nobody else is in the room. I go into a room because there is nobody else to go, and the going is the shape, and the shape has been the same for about a dozen years, and this afternoon there were eleven people in a room in this district and three of them were asked out loud in a lane in nine words at about the second bell whether they would like to be the one standing there, and one of them said no, and the no was correct. There is not going to be another year of that, because the not was available and I took it, and a thing that is available is not a burden and is not a year and is not anything except a thing you did or did not do. A door is not a matter and a person is. That is the whole difference and it took me forty-eight days.”

**PAID, AND NOT TO BE PAID AGAIN. A year of his own memory, out of thirty-one, in full, once, in Chapter 197, at 197:49, in a room four miles off his district, before four sworn witnesses of his district, in about nine minutes, in the ordinary way. It is a specific year and not a fog and not a wound, and it is the last of the years in which he was in a room with a person he had not yet met, and the reason it is the last one is that the not was available and he took it.** Nobody relieved him of it, nobody said the price was fair, nobody told him afterwards what was in it, and he said all three of those out loud in the room before he paid (197:55). **Nobody in that room can check what it was and that is not a trick of his (197:49, 197:72).** The person who named that price is not in that room and is not one of the nine of them and is a long way off, and he has never found out whether the naming was fair and does not ask anybody (197:53), which keeps 177:91's separation of the namer from the woman of twenty-eight in force; she is in the room at 194 and at 197 and she is not the namer.

**And afterwards he cannot say which year it was (197:100, 197:110). Not the year in general: he can say what it was, in the same words he said twenty minutes earlier in front of nine people, and the sentence is the best sentence he has ever said in a room and it has no year in it and never had. What he has lost is the year. The reader knows the year, because 177:91 said it. The district knows the shape, because he said it out loud. The man who paid it has neither, and the seventeenth page of his own book says so in four words: I cannot say which year it was. That is the only thing the year took and no later pass may make it larger.**

### 4. THE MONEY, with the working, recomputed in a script for this record and not read out of a state file

- **The account is £9,700 at one shilling in the pound, which is £485 a year, at nothing, and the charge is due at the close of every grain year and does not stop.** Check: 9,700 × 12d = 116,400d = £485. It was £9,240 and £462 before the carts went on it. **9700 − 9240 = 460 and 485 − 462 = 23, and the 460 is the same 460 as the carts, which is the whole of the volume in one line of arithmetic.**
- **A year of protection was worth £462; the hired carts cost £460; the difference is £2.** Check: 110,880d − 110,400d = 480d = £2 exactly. **It is stated in a yard at 188:31, at a beam at 192:29 and 192:31, and it is not £2 3s. `outline/volume-04.md` line 126 is arithmetically wrong and is still wrong and is still to be left, and no chapter of Volume 04 says £2 3s, and neither does this record.**
- **A hundred printed sheets at threepence is three hundred pence, which is one pound and five shillings.** Check: 100 × 3d = 300d; 300 − 240 = 60; 60 ÷ 12 = 5. Stated at 182:17, corrected in a yard at 182:21 and 182:23 in front of nine people, and stated once more at 200:55. **Chapter 167:41's two pounds five is carried, not repaired, and nothing in the volume is built on it.**
- **A prize is two sheets at one shilling and fourpence, so two shillings and eightpence, the same in a hundred and one counties, and the register publishes a scale of carriage at three shillings a load.** The county's entered rate is two shillings and eightpence a load and the register's published scale is three shillings; the two are different numbers and both are right, and the difference is fourpence, and fourpence is the price of copying one sheet at a counter, and a sheet is one shilling and fourpence, so a sheet is four times the difference. **Nothing in Volume 04 reconciles the two rates and neither does this record.**
- **On the register's own scale a man with one cart moves about four hundred loads in a cut, which is sixty pounds, and sixty pounds is four hundred and fifty times two shillings and eightpence, exactly.** Check: 400 × 36d = 14,400d = £60; 14,400 ÷ 32 = 450, remainder zero. Stated at 183:64, 185:61, 199:27. **Chapter 183 and Chapter 185 first had forty-five and it was repaired in place; that class did not recur.**
- **A hundred and forty loads at thirty-two pence is eighteen pounds thirteen shillings and fourpence.** Check: 140 × 32d = 4,480d; 4,480 − 4,320 = 160; 160 ÷ 12 = 13 remainder 4.
- **A claim is 406 loads at thirty-two pence, which is £54 2s 8d, of which nine pounds was paid on the sixth day of the forty-third week and £45 2s 8d waits to a year and is not paid.** Check: 406 × 32d = 12,992d; − 2,160d = 10,832d.
- **The lease at a fortieth of what is in the granary: about eleven thousand loads is about 275, about seven thousand is about 175, the difference is about 100 loads, and 100 loads at the register's own three shillings is £15.** Chapter 196 deliberately does not recompute it, because the chapter's point is a reeve refusing to do a sum she has done four times.
- **A reeve's wage is £2 10 a year and eleven weeks out of it is ten shillings and sevenpence.** Check: 600d ÷ 52 × 11 = 126.92d, and 10s 7d is 127d.
- **The circulars: nine hundred sent, three hundred and seventy with a name, five hundred and thirty blank or unreturned, of which about three hundred and seventy blank and about one hundred and sixty never came back at all.** Check: 900 − 370 = 530; 530 − 370 = 160. **Four hundred and thirty is not in any chapter of Volume 04.**
- **Three hundred and thirty-nine of the three hundred and seventy hold one cart and thirty-one hold more, and about nine thousand are in issue on the Shelf, and five hundred and thirty of the nine hundred have got nothing at all for it.** Check: 339 + 31 = 370. **The aggregate of the three hundred and thirty-nine at sixty pounds each is about twenty thousand three hundred and forty pounds, and it is the value of the carriage those men move and not the value of the sheets, no chapter in Volume 04 asserts it, the register was given up in Chapter 199 and the aggregate is not its face, and this record does not assert it.**
- **A notice of four sheets is five shillings and fourpence and the district has bought that notice twice; the second was never paid for; and the cost of it remains with the district.** Check: 4 × 16d = 64d; × 2 = 128d = 10s 8d. Stated at 195:22 and 197:76.
- **A form of deposition is one sheet at a shilling and fourpence; reading and copying anything printed is fourpence a sheet; four sheets is sixteen pence; and the district paid that twice out of a reeve's apron.** Check: 4 × 4d = 16d. Four times out of the same apron in six weeks.
- **The nine hundred doors: 900 ÷ 9 = 100 days, 100 × 9d = 900d = £3 15, and a hundred days cannot be done by a man with a cart, because 400 × 36d = £60 and that is his year. A carter's day is about fifty-seven loads and fifty-seven loads at three shillings is £8 11s, and the only price of a day anybody in that district writes down is ninepence, and the two are two hundred and twenty-eight to one.** Check: 57 × 36d = 2,052d; 2,052 ÷ 9 = 228 exactly. Inherited and not restated in the last ten chapters.
- **Sena Ollet is paid three shillings a week in the cut and nothing the rest of the year; the basket is about forty pounds full and about eleven pounds empty; the towpath is six miles in each direction; and his wage is ninepence a day in chalk, by name, on a board outside the second door.**
- **None of Volume 01, Volume 02 or Volume 03's money is on the Shelf and none of it may be quoted as a Shelf figure.**

**FINDING 12, AND IT IS THE ONLY ONE OF THE TWELVE THAT IS NOT A FINDING AGAINST ANYTHING, and it is numbered anyway because the numbering ran to twelve and renumbering it would have moved the cross-references other files carry. It is the good one and it is why the other eleven are worth printing: every figure above closes. Sixteen checks were run in a script for this record against the figures printed in the fifty chapters and against the earlier tables, and sixteen pass. The instrument that caught every wrong figure in this volume is the same one every time: print the unit price in the sentence, find it, and multiply. The prose of Volume 04 is in better order than this project's records have been saying for four volumes, and that is a fact about the chapters and not a claim that nothing is wrong, because FINDING 4 and FINDING 5 and FINDING 6 and FINDING 7 are all in the chapters.**

### 5. THE FOUR THREADS THAT WERE ANSWERED, and how each one was answered

- **The granted day. A matter asked for a day by the person carrying it, for the first time in this county's history, at 185:85, and granted — not decided, granted, and listed — at 185:93. The day is in the other half of the grain year and it is off the page and it stays off the page. Nothing in Chapters 191–200 is a sitting.** The answer was given by a chair of about sixty-two who said out loud that a person of the district who cannot read what they have signed is the only way this kind of district is ever going to be a person in a room, and that a room can use such a person once and nothing in the paper stops a room using them again (191:63, 191:71), and that the only thing in the building that can be decided about it is a witness. The standing defect that carried this thread is FINDING 13: the card gave Chapter 191 a title and a date that could not both be true, and the chapter is not a sitting.
- **The lease. The discretion ran out, nobody exercised it, nobody refused it, the account has not moved on it in a month, and the account cannot act on it either — to take a granary off a district the account has to put a person in the district, and there is no form for it and there has not been one since before he came into the post (199:33).** The granary is not renewed and not lost. A reeve of fifty-four has been holding a granary and a bank of the second mouth on a word of her own and a door that shuts at the seventh bell, and the whole of the account's protection of her is that nobody has ever had to look at her, and that is not a right and there is no line for it and he could not produce it in a room if she asked him to (199:35). She asks for one thing: that the reason be written down by somebody who is not her.
- **The harvest. It is in, and about six hundred people are not going to be hungry, and not one of them is going to be ruined, and about four hundred and eighty-five pounds a year is going to fall due at the close of this grain year on an account of nine thousand seven hundred pounds, and the district is at nothing and has been since the sixth day of the forty-third week (196:19). And the person who arranged it did not arrange it for us (196:23).** It is in because about forty people in a district of six hundred did ordinary work in the ordinary way for nine weeks and a day, and the only thing he did was to write down what each of them said in their own words and keep the three of them apart (196:47, 196:49). The one figure a reeve has never put on her own wall in nine years is the number of a lease, and she says so and nobody writes it (196:37, 196:39).
- **The scheme. It is exposed in a room in terms with the figures by the man who owns it, in about nine minutes, in four numbered lines, and he denies not one of them (199:19–27). He gives up one office, the register itself, about nine inches thick, kept by himself for nine years with no clerk on it; and he says about nine thousand out loud himself, in a hundred and one counties, at the same price in the same form, and says the other nine thousand stay in issue and that he is not going to pretend to the room that something has been undone (199:45, 199:49). He is not redeemed and says so in his own mouth (199:63), and his network survives, and the network is the price of a sheet being a clerk's fee in a hundred and one counties, which is a table and not a conspiracy, and the table is not his and he argued against it twice in nine years and lost twice (199:57).** And the thing nobody said in a yard in the forty-eighth week is said by him, in a room, in his own mouth, and it is the one time in the volume the man says it himself: a remedy that undoes the scheme and does not undo the price produces a district where nobody can get a cart (199:67). **He is rung three of a ladder planted before Chapter 1. He is not a new arrival, he is not more powerful than anybody, he is not a god, and he is not the last problem in the series.**

### 6. THE FOUR THREADS THIS VOLUME OPENED AND DID NOT CLOSE — and the new question first

**FIRST, THE NEW QUESTION, AND IT IS THE ONLY ONE THE VOLUME LEAVES OPEN ON THE PAGE.** *Whose memory is missing from the account that records his payment.* **It is in a book and it is not a sentence, and the book is a document and not a room, and the document says the thing without a person having to say it.** It is at 198:35, in the account's own page for Ryefold, seventh line: the burden stated against the party is entered against no. 63 of one hundred, which is a number upon a sheet and not a name; the person who has taken it is not a party, is not of this district, and could not be entered by name, there being no line in that book for a person who is not a party; the burden is a year of one man's memory and it is paid; and no line in that book says which year, and there is no column for one, and the person who paid it is the only person who could put one there. **The number is on the outside of a bundle a reeve put there herself, off one of a hundred numbered sheets, and that sheet is one of ninety-one in a sacking bag with a bit of string on the mouth of it in a yard nine feet deep between the granary wall and a cart-shed wall, where it has been since the seventh bell of the third day of the forty-eighth week (198:73), and the person who would know is standing in that lane and nobody asked him (198:75, 198:77). Nobody in that lane has ever asked him a question they did not already have the answer to.**

**Nobody in Volume 04 answers it. The System did not raise it and did not answer it (198:79), and 198:79 says in words that the white nothing said nothing whatever about a year then and says nothing whatever about a year now, and that it is a thing that records and not a thing that warns. No chapter in this volume compresses it into one word, and the one word Volume 03 refused to use is not to be supplied here and is not to be supplied later. The ending outline is the authority on the shape of the question and not on the wording: the account has a line in it that pays a year to somebody, the account does not say who, and the person who would know is the person who cannot remember the year. A close that answers it has done the thing the series has refused three times.**

- **The carrying, still off nothing, with a finding about why and no answer to it.** A line that is not a claim is not a thing that comes off (197:84, 198:65), said three times in ten chapters by the account's own agent and answered by nobody; a forbearance may be applied for once a year and the next one this district can put its hand on is fifty-two weeks off (197:80); and 200:55 says in his own hand that the year has not gone out of him and is not going to in this grain year. **The stage is not paid for twice and the year did not stop the carrying and he did not expect it to.**
- **The blank in the third line of the bench's own book, still a blank** (195:63, 200:63), and the fourth sheet's question, still unasked, and the fourth hand on it still a stranger's hand that nobody has asked (193:43, 193:64). **A bench of four cannot un-swear a thing and would not want to.** In about nine months a clerk in a counting-house in another city four miles off is going to have to enter one line against this district and this bench is not going to be able to say which of the four of them it belongs to, and that is said out loud in the volume's last chapter (200:43).
- **Nesh Arrad, thirty-nine, and the one clock in the volume that is still running and that nobody in the district is watching.** She came into this district in the twenty-second week of last year with four children, a stove and no paper at all; a reeve put her at the bottom of a list of her own in a different ink with no authority anywhere in this county for a reeve to do that; a woman of thirty-nine walked her six miles up a road that goes north on the second day of the forty-seventh week with a form and a sheet the reeve paid for at a shilling and fourpence and put one name on one sheet in a second hand; and it has been in that book ever since, on the same page as the hundred loads and the year (198:85, 198:87). **The nine months has not run out. The sheet went in on Shelf day forty-nine and the last chapter of Volume 04 is Shelf day seventy, and that is twenty-one days, and twenty-one days is not nine months, and the letter cannot have arrived and has not arrived. This record states that the clock is running and how much of it has gone, and it does not say the letter has arrived and it does not close the thread.** There are two nines in that building and neither of them is a punishment, and one of them belongs to a building nobody in this district can reach in a day.

**AND THE REST OF IT, all of it open, none of it resolved by anybody:** the second notice of five shillings and fourpence, never paid for, and the only line of a draft the account cannot do anything about (195:22, 197:76); the claim of forty-five pounds two shillings and eightpence to a carter of fifty-one with a boy, eleven miles off, not of this district, not producible, with no form for a person named in a claim to ask for it to be entered, **and he is right** (198:51, 198:55); the dead man in the fourth line of a list of two hundred and forty, and his wife, who knows that she is on the fourth line and that the fourth line is being carried and does not know that her husband is not alive, and a reeve who has thought about it every week for nine months (168:59–63); the box of about nine hundred and forty forms in a run of four thousand of which about three thousand and sixty were taken up, with a man with a key six miles off who is there two days out of seven and cannot read what is in his own nineteen shelves, and **nobody in this district can get a message to him in a day, and the box is not found and may not be found** (198:93); the ninety-one numbered sheets in a yard, and about nine hundred doors, and no one hundredth person, and about four hundred carts in about nine hundred yards who have had three weeks in which to pick them up and not one of them has been asked (198:73, 197:39); the class of three hundred and thirty-nine, and the one boy of nineteen who is its only instrument, and the sentence he will not write down because a thing an officer has said in a year is a thing somebody can use in about nine years (182:77); and the minute of the first of the forty-eighth week, one sheet, the only copy in the world, in a drawer in a building four miles off, with nobody's name on it and nobody ever having asked for it (191:87).

### 7. THE DEVICES, WHERE THEY ARE, AND NONE ADVANCED

- **The rendering came up five times in the volume and not four: 151:51 on a river bank in a country he had never seen, 160:78 in a yard, 166:117 in a yard, 177:11 in a cart, and 197:64 in a room, where he was in a bad way and nobody noticed.** It is at the same height and the same distance every time, it is a city and not a coast, and it did not move and it did not explain itself and it is not to be advanced and it is not to be referred to again. **The volume's month-dates are TWO, at 160:78 and 177:11, and both belong to the rendering's own date in a city four hundred miles behind him and neither is a Shelf date; 166:117 carries no date of any kind and says only that the thing has never once moved in four years.** 197:64 dates it without one, and a month-name scan over the last ten chapters returns nothing.
- **The leg has not been his own since that morning, and no file in this project may state a count of days since it cleared, and this record does not.** It is in the strong form three times in the last ten chapters, at 195:83, 197:66 and 200:21, and **200:21 is the volume's last mention of it.** The device ledger in `state/continuity.md` says four and names 197:66, 200:21 and 200:51: **200:51 carries no leg at all, and 195:83 does.** 197:66 says in words why it did not clear when the year went — a year is not a thing anybody is carrying, and the only thing that has ever cleared that leg was a piece of paper somebody else was going to have to read, and there was nothing in that room he was carrying for anybody.
  > **SUPERSEDED BY THE VOLUME 04 REPAIR PHASE, 26 September 2026. THE LEG IS HIS OWN IN SEVEN PLACES IN THIS VOLUME AND NOT ONCE, AND THE SENTENCE ABOVE THAT SAID IT WENT ONCE IS NOT DELETED AND IS NOT TRUE.** The close's sentence read: *It goes once in the volume, unremarked, in Chapter 190, for about four hours, and nothing in it clears the leg except a carried notice, once, four years ago.* **The chapters say otherwise at seven places, and a scan of the fifty chapters for the ownership of the leg returns eight positive lines in six chapters, which group as the audit groups them:** `152:113`, `154:105`, **`155:5` with `155:9`**, `155:39`, `156:71`, `173:65`, and **`190:87` with `190:89`** — **and `190:87` and `190:89` are cited as two lines and not as a range, because `190:88` is an empty line and this project quotes chapter-and-line exactly.** Three of them say so at length: `155:5` gives the four hours of the six, **`155:7` gives the finding the whole device rests on — the leg goes when he is carrying a thing that somebody else is going to have to read, and it comes back when the carrying stops** — and `155:39` gives the longest interval the volume states in either direction, an ordinary condition of his own leg for about a year and a half. **The strong form stands on twenty lines in eighteen of the fifty chapters: 151:89, 152:113, 153:117, 155:5, 155:39, 158:87, 158:109, 160:136, 165:3, 166:132, 173:65, 174:97, 177:11, 180:63, 183:94, 185:121, 186:87, 190:87, 195:83 and 200:21** (counting Chapter 190's two lines as one site; count them separately and it is twenty-one lines in the same eighteen chapters). **The finding the ledger was built on — that nothing but a carried notice has ever cleared the leg — is a Volume 01 finding about a carried notice and does not describe what this volume's own carrying did seven times. Read `155:5` to `155:9` before writing any Stage 4 chapter in which he carries something, and do not build on the close's sentence.**
- **The left hand is a scar at the base of the thumb in four years. The vantage has not moved. The door in the picture is at the same height and the same distance. The token is not touched and the fourth use stands: it took the words.** None of the four is mentioned in the last ten chapters and none may be advanced.
- **THE COUNT OF THE PEOPLE WHO KNOW HE CANNOT READ IS NOT STATED IN THIS RECORD, AND IT IS NOT TO BE STATED IN THIS PROJECT.** **This bullet said the count *has not moved in this volume*, and that was the false half of a contradiction, not the true half. The prose moves it.** He has said it out loud once in two hundred chapters, at 154:53, and no chapter of Volume 04 restates it. **But the volume adds people who come to know it inside the volume, and the chapters say so in their own mouths: a person of the district says it to him at 153:33, a boy of nineteen in the account's own office says it to him at 164:49, a carter states it about him at 185:27, he says it himself in a room at 191:27, two of those statements go onto the district's own nail in writing at 193:60 and 193:62, and a carter of about thirty asks a reeve about it at 200:13.** **So the correct form of this record is the unnumbered one: the count is not stated here, it was said out loud once in Chapter 154, the prose adds at least one further named person in Chapter 164, and no file may say by how much.** The figure was printed in twenty-one lines across four state files in two different versions; the Volume 04 audit found it and this review pass has corrected the two lines that were in live blocks, and the repair phase supersedes the nineteen in the archive blocks, and §8 item 16 is corrected to match. **A file that needs to refer to this refers to `154:53` and `164:49` and to nothing else. About nine people in this district have watched something happen to a man in a room for ten weeks and not one of them has ever mentioned it, and that sentence is the volume's standing fact and is at 171:36, 190:91, 196:53 and 200:63.**
- **The right of refusal is not restored and the historical question is not answered.** The removed page, Sera's duplicate, the Bridgeward, Wen Lask and the toll, the C—2 chain, the plate, the fifth mark, the preservation authority's custody question, the bill and the spring ranking, the page with no successor and the continuity line are all exactly where Volume 03 left them. **The continuity line is not on the Shelf's table anywhere in Volume 04 and was not refused an eleventh time in it; the only form that appears is a past-tense reference to what a book once said about four hundred decisions, at 164:27, 164:79, 186:17 and 189:11.**
- **Ors Halm is not the last problem in the series and this record does not make him one.** Ansa Rell does not appear and is not redeemed. Neris Ro and Tamsin Ro are six miles from Ryefold and are not in a scene, Tamsin is not to be called and nobody is to look for her, and Bram Orren does not appear. Sabra Trew is not on this shelf and Alma Gannock, eighty-one, is still not told and nothing whatever happened to her boy of eight. The three sheets on the coast are four hundred miles away in an office and no chapter of Volume 04 produced them and the word Saltglass does not appear in the fifty. **NK-3317 is not explained, not referred to and not asked about.**
- **Sera Quill, twenty-eight, may be named and may not be enlarged.** Her fourth term was released in Chapter 152, her family claim of six years is unfiled, he may not speak for it, and the fourth reason she gave him and the reason he could not ask Osa Trin anything are the same shape and were kept apart in two mouths on purpose (194:69, 194:71, 194:73). She is not an alliance and nothing was sealed and the four sentences of Chapter 152 are not built on.
- **The 179 and 189 shape is spent and the decision is recorded rather than the prose changed.** A block-quoted list of dated entries each ending in a verdict, in a room above a chandler's shop, spoken by a convenor of sixty-one who cannot read his own hand, and then its own inversion in the same room with the same convenor refusing to look at the book. **A scan for the word CORRECT over the fifty chapters returns it in Chapters 179 and 189 and nowhere else, and this record does not use the shape a second time.** The honest candidate if it is ever wanted again is a different object in a different building with a different reader, and the one in this volume is the seven-line page of the account's own book at 198:29–35, which is a different thing in every particular.
- **Nobody is relieved, nothing is undone, nothing is rescued, nothing is redeemed, and no ruling is made.** The account's charge does not stop, the lease is not renewed and not lost, the carrying is not off, the blank is a blank, and the plan is a work of other people's ordinary labour and not his.

**FINDING 7. CHAPTER 167 IS TITLED *The Word On The Form* AND IT PRINTS A FORM WITH A WORD AT THE TOP OF IT, AND THAT FORM IS VOLUME 02'S CLOSED DEVICE, AND THE VOLUME'S OWN OUTLINE FORBIDS IT IN TERMS.** At 167:9 a bench's third brings a sheet up a lane in his own coat, puts it on the bench's own table and says "Now ask me", and the sheet is printed in the chapter as a single word alone on a line, in capitals, as a block quote. **`outline/volume-04.md` line 62 says: the form with a word at the top of it comes back, and it is Volume 02's device and it may not be restaged; what comes back instead is the fact that a form can be numbered again. The repetition bar names the same prohibition. The device is restaged, in a finished batch, in the volume's thirty-seventh chapter, and no pass in this project has recorded it.** The word appears ten times in the volume, at 160:35, 160:44 inside a panel, 167:9, 167:11, 167:35, 167:39, 167:41, 167:101, 167:105 and 177:9, **and the statement carried in this project's own records that no chapter of Volume 04 says the word is false.** The chapter may be using the device in order to say that it is powerless — a form with that word on it takes the paper and not the rule, and the form is the thing that does the work and there is no form — and that is a reading and not a verdict, and the bar is a bar on the device and not on the theme. **Either way it was unrecorded, and the unrecorded thing is the finding.** This record names the device in order to report that it was used and does not print a form and does not restage it again.

**FINDING 8. BOLD IS USED AS EMPHASIS IN RUNNING PROSE AND IN DIALOGUE IN ELEVEN LINES OF THE FIFTY CHAPTERS, AND THE OUTLINE FORBIDS THAT IN TERMS.** A volume-wide scan for the bold marker returns twenty-one lines: the ten of the five panels, at 160:43–44, 166:123–124, 177:101–102, 187:39–40 and 197:59–60, on days twenty-two, thirty-six, forty-eight, fifty-eight and sixty-eight, **and eleven that are emphasis spans at 151:59, 151:93, 152:5, 153:123, 154:73, 159:13, 159:19, 160:58, 160:62, 160:66 and 160:72.** `outline/volume-04.md` guardrail six says: a rendering is plain bold, a document is block-quoted and not bold, and bold is never used as emphasis in running prose or dialogue. **Every pass to date has run that scan over one batch and reported the batch clean, and every batch but the first is clean, and nobody has run it over the fifty. Measured, because the close said *the first two* and that was one batch too many: the eleven emphasis lines stand at 151:59, 151:93, 152:5, 153:123, 154:73, 159:13, 159:19, 160:58, 160:62, 160:66 and 160:72, and all eleven are inside Chapters 151–160, which is Batch 0001 and nothing else. **Batch 0002, 0003, 0004 and 0005 are clean of emphasis and the whole of the damage is in the first ten chapters, which is a smaller and a more fixable thing than two batches of it.** And the sharpest form of it: the Chancellor's four lines are bold in Chapter 160 and plain in Chapter 188, and the same four sentences stand in those two chapters and in no third. **THE PARENTHETICAL ABOVE WAS WRONG IN THREE WAYS AND IS CORRECTED HERE, MEASURED AND NOT READ OUT OF A FILE. (1) It said the four lines are bold in 160 and plain in 199 and plain a third time in 188, and named three settings. A scan for the four sentences across the fifty chapters returns Chapters 160 and 188 only, at 160:58, 160:62, 160:66 and 160:72 and at 188:31, 188:33, 188:35 and 188:37. Chapter 199 does not set them again; its exposure is a new set of four numbered lines with the same method and different words. (2) It said those four lines are the only two repeated paragraphs in the whole of Volume 04 above seventy characters, and a duplicate figure with no threshold beside it is not a figure at all: the count is a function of the threshold. AT SEVENTY CHARACTERS AND ABOVE there are FIVE exactly duplicated lines and no more, all five of them block-quoted physical documents reproduced word for word, in THREE documents and not five — the forbearance warrant at `157:23` and `162:35` with its second line at `157:25` and `162:37`; the form of deposition at `164:59` and `165:45` and its fourth line at `164:63` and `165:49`, which is under seventy characters; and the account's own book at `183:24` and `198:30`, `183:25` and `198:31` and `183:26` and `198:32`, which is three of the five from one document. AT FORTY CHARACTERS AND ABOVE the run returns TEN duplicated lines, and the three that are not documents are a deliberate italic refrain at nine sites — *It is mine to write down and it is not mine to decide*, at 158:115, 166:140, 170:93, 177:114, 180:71, 185:133, 190:97, 197:114 and 200:61 — and two short sentences of ordinary prose at `176:19` and `178:47`, and at `178:85` and `186:57`. (3) It said two repeated paragraphs where the figure is five lines. The same four sentences stand twice in fifty chapters, they differ between their two settings in their bold markers, and the rule is against the one setting that appears in the first of the two.**

**FINDING 4. A DAY-COUNT IN CHAPTER 195 IS TWO DAYS SHORT, AND IT IS THE ONLY COMPOUND WEEKS-AND-DAYS FIGURE IN THE FIFTY CHAPTERS.** At 195:67, in his own mouth, standing at the end of a table: I have been in this county nine weeks and a day. **Chapter 195 is Shelf day sixty-six, and the volume's own convention, fixed at 153, 155, 158, 160, 166, 170, 177, 180 and 190, makes the count on Shelf day N equal to N, so the count is sixty-six days, which is nine weeks and three days, and nine weeks and a day is sixty-four.** A scan for the compound form over the fifty chapters returns 195:67 and nothing else, which means the instrument the records have been using — a list of the phrase-forms — cannot see this class at all. And the intervals table in `state/continuity.md` prints, of this figure and two neighbours, that none of the three contradicts another: **the three do not contradict one another, because 196's "nine weeks" and 198's "about nine weeks" are floors and approximations and 195's is a count, and a count is not a window.** This is the first wrong figure in the volume's prose about the volume's own clock, and it is in a chapter that five passes checked and none of them saw, and the close left it in the prose and recorded it here, because a close creates one artifact and does not reopen a finished batch.

> **REPAIRED, 26 September 2026, by the Volume 04 repair phase. `195:67` now reads: I have been in this county nine weeks and three days.** Three words inside a line that already existed; Chapter 195 is 83 lines before and after and no chapter-and-line citation moved. **The finding above is kept as written and the repair is recorded beside it, and the class is not gone: `174:53`'s eleventh day of the forty-fifth week, `160:96`'s four hundred and thirty, `167:41`'s two pounds five and `158:77`/`158:79` are all still in the prose and all still carried, and the bar on building on any of them is unchanged.**

**FINDING 5. CHAPTER 200, IN ITS LAST PARAGRAPH BUT ONE, SAYS THREE THINGS AND THEN SAYS FIVE.** At 200:51, behind the second door of a granary: a lease nobody has renewed and nobody has refused, a sheet with a blank in the third line that went back four miles in a woman's coat and is in a book six miles up a road that goes north, and a register about nine inches thick that went on a nail in a room in that lane that morning and is the sixth thing on it — **and then: not one of those five things is a person and four of them are in this district and one of them is not and cannot be.** Three are named and the count is five, and one of the five is the sheet in a book in another city, which is the one that is not in this district and cannot be, so the two missing are both in this district and the chapter does not say what they are. **This is the volume's last count said out loud and it does not match the things it is counting, in a volume that has made a count-out-loud a standing instruction at 193:57 and has kept the nail at three, four, five and six at every change and said the number before each one went up.** A later pass must decide what the other two are, and the two candidates that are in the chapter and in the district are the ninety-one numbered sheets in the yard and the six things on the nail.

> **REPAIRED, 26 September 2026, by the Volume 04 repair phase, AND IT WAS THE SMALLER OF THE TWO REPAIRS THE AUDIT OFFERED. `200:51` now reads: …and not one of those three things is a person and two of them are in this district and one of them is not and cannot be.** The count was changed to match the three things the sentence actually names — a lease nobody has renewed or refused, a sheet with a blank in the third line now in a book six miles up a road, and a register about nine inches thick that is the sixth thing on a nail. **This was a mechanical repair and is recorded as one, and that is the whole of the decision. The alternative repair — naming the ninety-one numbered sheets in the yard and the six things on the nail, and leaving the count at five — was not taken, and it was not taken because it adds two objects to the volume's last image and moves the rhythm of the paragraph, and that is a writer's judgement and not an audit's. Had it been taken, the record would have had to say it was a writer's decision and not a mechanical one. Chapter 200 is 65 lines before and after.**

**FINDING 6. CHAPTER 200 SAYS THE DAY BEFORE WHERE THE VOLUME'S OWN CALENDAR SAYS TWO DAYS BEFORE.** At 200:43: three of whom had come four miles the day before. **Chapter 200 is Shelf day seventy; the four persons of the district walked four miles in Chapter 197, on Shelf day sixty-eight; seventy − 68 is two, and the day before Chapter 200 is Chapter 198, which is a counter six miles up a road that goes north.** Same class as FINDING 4 and it is an interval stated once and checkable, and the records noticed the phrase and left it, which is the correct instinct and the wrong place to stop.

> **REPAIRED, 26 September 2026, by the Volume 04 repair phase. `200:43` now reads: three of whom had come four miles two days before.** One word, inside the line. Chapter 200 is 65 lines before and after and no chapter-and-line citation moved.

**FINDING 9. THREE DAYS OF THE RUN CARRY NO CHAPTER AND ARE IN NO TABLE, and they are set out at the end of section 1.**

**FINDING 10. ONE ROW OF A CLOCK TABLE CARRIES THE WRONG WEEK. The Batch 0004 table gives Chapters 189 and 190 as the fifth day of the forty-seventh week, which is day fifty-three. The chapters are right and the table is what moved, which is the correct direction of travel: a record is not evidence against the thing it records.**

**FINDING 11. ONE MONTH-NAME WAS LEFT IN THE FIFTY CHAPTERS AND THE VOLUME 04 REPAIR PHASE TOOK IT OUT ON 26 SEPTEMBER 2026. At 181:92, in a carter's mouth, it read: on the thirty-fifth day of that month a boy of nineteen read a piece of paper out in a room four miles off. **The Shelf has no months. The same event is dated the thirty-fifth day, with no month, at 192:35, 197:74 and 197:76, and the repair is the volume using a form it already knows three times.** It now reads: on the thirty-fifth day a boy of nineteen read a piece of paper out in a room four miles off. **A volume-wide scan for the phrase returns nothing; the Shelf month-date count is now zero, and the only remaining occurrence of the words *that month* in the fifty chapters is `165:5`, which is a memory of what happened to him *that month* and is not a date. No chapter-and-line citation into Chapters 181–190 moved: Chapter 181 is 110 lines before and after.** The close did not make this repair and said why at the time — a volume close creates one artifact and does not reopen a finished batch's prose — and the repair phase did make it, and that difference is recorded here rather than smoothed over. **Chapter 174:53's eleventh day of the forty-fifth week is a different thing and is still not repaired, because there is no such day: the forty-fifth week has seven, and the honest form is *since about the forty-fifth week*, and it is carried.**

### 8. WHAT A LATER PASS MUST NOT DO

1. Do not answer the new question, and do not compress it into one word. It is on a page in a book six miles up a road and it is not in Ryefold and the System does not raise it.
2. Do not add a panel. The volume has five, at 160:43–44, 166:123–124, 177:101–102, 187:39–40 and 197:59–60, on days twenty-two, thirty-six, forty-eight, fifty-eight and sixty-eight, and a scan for the bold marker over Chapters 191–200 returns the two lines of the fifth and nothing else.
3. Do not add a day-count instance to the fiction, and do not print a count of instances without saying that it is a count of instances. **The device appears twenty-two times in Chapters 151-200 and the record said twenty-one until the Volume 04 audit, which is correct to twenty-two; the record is not a twenty-third.** The twenty-second is at 156:85. Run the instrument with cardinals and ordinals or it cannot see a page heading.
4. Do not use the phrase the first week after the cut. 173:55 says it and is wrong, 185:89 said it and was corrected, and the honest phrases are the second week after the cut and a fortnight after the cut.
5. Do not use the eleventh day of the forty-fifth week, and do not give any date at all to the day a reeve first wanted a box. If the box is referred to at all the honest form is since about the forty-fifth week, which is a week with no day number and is always legal.
6. Do not build on two pounds five. A hundred sheets at threepence is one pound and five shillings.
7. Do not state four hundred and thirty. The clerks who never came back at all are about one hundred and sixty.
8. Do not use forty-five times. Sixty pounds is four hundred and fifty times two shillings and eightpence.
9. Do not say £2 3s. £462 − £460 is £2.
10. Do not put a weekday anywhere and do not put a Shelf month-date anywhere. **A plural-safe scan over the fifty chapters returns ZERO as of 26 September 2026, and it returned ONE until the Volume 04 repair phase took it out at `151:77`, and both numbers are on the record because a later pass that inherits a bare zero will not know to run the scan. Use `mondays?` and not `monday|` when you run it, because the pattern as printed in thirteen places in this project could not match a plural Monday and was blind to one day of the seven.** The volume's two month-dates belong to the rendering at 160:78 and 177:11, and the third that was not the rendering's, at 181:92, is also gone: the Shelf month-date count in the fifty chapters is now zero.
11. Do not give Ilyan an office, a party, a bench, a body, a remedy he chose alone, or a stage. He is Stage 4 and the stage arrived in Chapter 177 and is not to be re-arrived, and the outline's Stage 5 is for Volumes 8 to 10.
12. Do not undo anything and do not relieve anybody. The lease is answered and not renewed and not lost; the carrying is off nothing; the claim of forty-five pounds two shillings and eightpence is not paid; the blank is a blank; the second notice is not paid for; nobody is redeemed; and the account's charge of four hundred and eighty-five pounds a year falls due at the close of the grain year and does not stop.
13. Do not introduce a new enemy and do not make the world bigger. The network is a table of clerk's fees and not a conspiracy, and about nine thousand people who did their jobs properly are not a plot.
14. Do not produce a person. A bench asked in its own room in its own motion; a wheelwright came on a day of his own with a wheel in his shop; four persons of a district walked four miles because about four hundred carts had nothing to do; a carter of about thirty asked a question. A carter who volunteers is not a production.
15. Do not restate the sentence at 156:63 and do not relieve him of it. The four words are quoted exactly once in the fifty chapters after 165, at 194:43, in the mouth of the clerk who entered them, and they are not explained there and are not to be put in a character's mouth for warmth.
16. **Do not restate the count of the people who know he cannot read, and do not add to it in the fiction. The count is not printed in this project, in any file, in any figure — this item said *do not add a ninth*, which named a figure the record is not entitled to name, and that is corrected. See §7 for the chapter lines and for why the count did move inside Volume 04.**
17. Do not use the 179 and 189 shape a third time.
18. Do not restore the right of refusal, answer the historical question, advance the authorship question or the Shale Mirror, name NK-3317, call for Tamsin, put Neris Ro or Bram Orren in a scene, redeem Ansa Rell, or ask about Alma Gannock's boy of eight.
19. Do not merge the fourth reason a recordwright gave him and the reason he could not ask the reader of his district. They are the same shape and are not the same thing, and one of them says so in her own mouth.

### 9. WHAT A PERSON IS CARRYING AT THE END OF VOLUME 04

A year of a man's own memory, of which he can say the shape and not the year, and which is in a book six miles up a road that does not say who, against a number upon a sheet that is lying in a yard with a bit of string on the mouth of it. A matter listed for a day in the other half of the year that he is still carrying and that has not come off anything. A hole in the third line of a sheet about the size of a hand, in a book in a room above a chandler's shop, and in a second book in another city, and he is the one who put the hole there and could fill it and did not. A wage line in chalk read by a woman who shuts a door at the seventh bell. A basket that goes six miles each way and about eleven pounds empty and about forty pounds full, carried by a woman who has never read one thing in it in nine years and says so before anybody asks her. A leg that has not been his own since that morning and did not clear when the year went, and about four hours on one afternoon in the forty-eighth week that nobody found out about. A bench of four that has been four people for nineteen years learning to be four witnesses, which is a different job and is not a promotion, with a blank in the middle of it and a fourth hand that has never been asked. A reeve of fifty-four holding a granary on a word of her own and a door. A boy of nineteen behind a counter with a key in his hand and about nine thousand returns behind him and no column in his office for what he thinks. A boy of thirteen, not mentioned. A woman of thirty-nine at the bottom of a list in a different ink with no authority behind her, whose name is in a book six miles up a road that goes north and about which nobody in this district is going to know anything for about nine months, and that was the first one and it is not shut. And a hundred numbered sheets in a yard, and about nine hundred doors, and about four hundred carts in about nine hundred yards who have had three weeks and have not been asked.

**And the turn of the volume is not a ruling and not a triumph and it took about eleven seconds at a weigh in a yard off a lane: a carter of about thirty asked the reeve a question nobody has ever asked her, out of nothing, and she answered him without stopping, to him and not to the man, and the man was four feet away and heard the whole of it, and not one person in that lane has said a word about it since and there is not going to be one (200:13, 200:15, 200:47). Then a basket going up a towpath, a door shut at the seventh bell, a beam in the morning, and a book shut on a name. If this record has a picture in it, that is the picture, and it is not a man in a corridor remembering something.**

### 10. WHAT A LATER PASS MUST CHECK HARDEST, and it is nineteen things, and three of the nineteen are new

Batch 0004's list was nine things and Batch 0005's was eleven. **This is a list of what to check and not a claim that nothing is wrong, and every item on it carries a chapter and a line.**

1. ~~**Whether 195:67's nine weeks and a day is repaired**~~ **DONE, 26 September 2026. It reads nine weeks and three days, and the repair was three words inside a line that already existed.** The volume's own convention is on the record in section 1 and the arithmetic is 66 days from 66.
2. ~~**Whether 200:43's the day before is repaired**~~ **DONE, 26 September 2026. It reads two days before. One word.** Chapter 197 is day sixty-eight and Chapter 200 is day seventy.
3. ~~**What the two missing things in 200:51 are**~~ **DONE, 26 September 2026, and it was the count and not the two. The sentence now counts the three things it names — three, and two of them in this district — and the ninety-one sheets and the six things on the nail were NOT added, and that was a judgement and not a subtraction.**
4. ~~**Whether 181:92's three words come out**~~ **DONE, 26 September 2026. It reads on the thirty-fifth day. Chapter 181 is 110 lines before and after, no citation into Chapters 181–190 moved, and the Shelf month-date count is now zero.**
5. **Whether 167:9 stays**, and if it stays, that it stays on the record as a restaged closed device with the outline's line 62 quoted beside it. If a later pass decides it was deliberate, the decision and the reasoning go in the record and not in the chapter.
6. **Whether the eleven emphasis lines stay bold**, and if they do, that the record says the outline forbids it, and if they do not, that the Chancellor's four lines in Chapter 160 are the last remaining instance of the setting and not Chapter 199.
7. **The day-count, by scan and not by list, and whether the twenty-two is still twenty-two** after any repair, and whether 167:35's three days is the same visit as 158's or a second one. **The instrument must include ordinals as well as cardinals, or it cannot see a page heading, which is how 156:85 was missed. The record has held this count three ways - twenty-one, eleven and ten - and only the chapters settle it.**
8. **The day-phrase instrument, printed and re-run, case-insensitive, and whether 191–200 is still forty-two and whether 181–190 is still fifty-four**, and whether 184:35's all-capitals instance is still in the run. Do not print a total that was read in a file.
9. **The three days with no chapter — forty-one, forty-four and sixty-one — and whether a Volume 05 agent should be told they exist** before it opens on day seventy-one, since the fiftieth week runs to seventy-six.
10. **The clock table's week column for Chapters 189 and 190**, which is the forty-eighth week and not the forty-seventh, and whether any other row of any of the five tables has the same defect.
11. **The five panels and their days** — twenty-two, thirty-six, forty-eight, fifty-eight, sixty-eight — and whether a later pass adds a sixth, and the two-line pattern and the WHAT IS NOT DECIDED line.
12. **The six block-quoted runs in Chapters 191–200 in six distinct documents**, obtained by counting runs and not by reading a list, and whether 198:29–35 still reads as a document and not as a summary.
13. **The seven standing prose defects**, and that none of them is repaired or built on without saying which reading is being used: 160:96's four hundred and thirty, 158:77 and 158:79, 167:41's two pounds five, 174:53's eleventh day of the forty-fifth week, 173:55's first week after the cut, 183 and 185's forty-five times, and 167:35's three days.
14. **The 179 and 189 shape**, that it is in two chapters and no third, and that the seven-line page of the account's own book at 198:29–35 is a different object and may be used if the device is wanted again.
15. **177:91, word for word**, and that it has survived Chapter 197 and this record without being softened, and that nobody has told him what was in the year and nobody has said the price was fair.
16. **Whether the new question has been compressed**, and whether 198:35 is being read as an answer to it, which it is not, and whether anybody has supplied the one word Volume 03 refused.
17. **Whether the leg has been given a clearing or an interval anywhere**, and that no file states a count of days since it cleared, and that this record has not.
18. **Whether the year has been taken twice**, and the answer is once, at 197, and a stage is not paid for twice and a close does not pay it again.
19. **Whether 200 has been read as a summary**, and the test is 200:13 and 200:15, which are a person asking a question and another person answering it, and if those two lines are the chapter's turn then it is a scene and not a summary.

**Closed: the granted day and the entry of it; the price of carrying a thing he cannot read; the two halves of the rule about a person in a room; the four prices of a year; the witness quorum and what it is; the two licensed people and what they may not be; the draft and the hole in it and the woman who has said no twice; the harvest, and the district eats, and nobody is ruined; the year, and what it cost, and the four words that are left; the account's own page and the new question on it; the scheme, in terms, with the figures, and the register given up, and the nine thousand untouched; the lease, answered; and the volume, resolved on a person doing their work.**

**Carried forward, and it must be picked up whole: the new question, on a page, unanswered; the carrying, off nothing, with a finding about why; the blank in the third line and the fourth sheet's question and the fourth hand; Nesh Arrad's nine months, twenty-one days of it gone, the letter not arrived, the thread not closed; the second notice of five shillings and fourpence; a clerk of thirty-four's letter and the entry in about nine months; the claim of forty-five pounds two shillings and eightpence and a carter of fifty-one with a boy who cannot be produced and is right; the dead man in the fourth line and the wife who does not know; the box of about nine hundred and forty forms and a man with a key six miles off; the ninety-one numbered sheets in a yard and about nine hundred doors and no one hundredth person; the class and the one boy of nineteen who is its only instrument; the minute in a drawer with nobody's name on it; and six more days of this grain year, seventy-one to seventy-six, that a close may say exist and may not narrate.**
